P297. [DONE] [T:xhigh] [11:50-11:54] Polygon world leftovers. (a) Floating leaves, a floating log and pumpkin, and tree roots that stop short of the ground; (b) block outlines show when he hits things, and blocks show under water: "it is not supposed to have a block base", so the field world must not draw or need block cells there; (c) rocky outcrops still blocky unless intended; (d) logs are far too thick, and a broken tree turns into a thicker log. Oct 15 evening: see P327 (hell and the Ash Vault in polygon worlds). Done Oct 16, together with P327 (one PR): (a) grounded trees and props in polygon worlds: trunk feet, root flares and stumps reach down to the lowest drawn ground round the foot (straight down under the flare, so a trunk on a slope no longer stands on its uphill side with its foot in the air), roots leave the trunk low and run down into the ground, a log in a crown that no limb walk reaches is a thin bough instead of a thick cut log hanging in the leaves (the floating log), a pumpkin, melon or similar lone form sits on the drawn surface instead of the block's foot over a dip (the floating pumpkin), moss and mushrooms the generator set on a fallen trunk come off (the trunk lies lower and thinner on the ground now), and trees read their ground through the 3x3 chunks' field stores (the built layer alone is air under them, so no trunk ever "stood" and the leaves of half-read trees floated); (b) no block grid where he looks or swims: aiming at polygon ground marks a ring on the drawn surface (it follows the ground's slope; a lit arc fills it as you mine) instead of a block outline, and the block crack overlay is gone on that ground (Quest WorldRenderer and the Mac Renderer); under and at the water: the water's surface runs on over low banks until the ground rises over it and its corners with no water round them sink just under the drawn ground, so the waterline is the bank's own curve, not a saw-tooth of cell edges; the water's depth shading follows the drawn bed, and no water side faces stand against a smooth bank; (c) rocky outcrops: boulders in polygon worlds are written into the field as rough rounded shapes (andesite, stone and tuff) and draw round, no longer a stack of cubes (block worlds keep their old boulders); (d) thinner logs: a living trunk's radius at its foot is 0.24 for a 5-tall tree, 0.31 at 10, 0.37 from 14 (was 0.33-0.44), fallen trunks 0.26, stumps keep a tree's thickness (only built timber, cabin walls and posts, nearly fills its block); a felled tree (P298) draws as round bark logs of the standing trunk's thickness with end caps instead of a block hull, so a broken tree no longer turns into a thicker log. Check: questcheck --view renders of his spots (the forest at 2955,137,624 and the shore at 2768,132,-111, seed 6665340019718134347): trunks grounded and thinner, the waterline a smooth curve.
P298. [DONE] [T:high] [11:53-11:54] Tree felling: break the base and the tree falls over (physics), and logs can be carried as whole objects. (a) Cut a trunk and the tree topples with weight and sound; (b) the fallen trunk becomes a log object you can pick up and carry, or chop into wood; (c) leaves drop and fade. Done Oct 16: (a) breaking a log under a tree (3+ logs above with leaves, resting on nothing else) fells it: the trunk and crown come loose as one rigid body (a Felled Tree on the ship physics, with no upright hold) and topple away from you under gravity with a creak, landing with a crash, a rustle and a little shake; 2x2 trunks, cabin walls and stumps that still rest on something stay standing; (b) once it lies still the bare trunk replaces it where it lies: chop it there (each log mined drops a log), or with an empty hand (or any non-block item) use it to pick it up whole (up to 24 logs; heavier ones give a dull knock until cut down) and carry it across your view in front of you, out of everyone's way; use again sets it down, thrown a little the way you look; logs float; (c) the crown's leaves drop away in falling leaf bits, with the saplings, sticks and apples a decaying crown gives in survival.

P303. [DONE] [T:high] [12:09; corrected by Remington 18:13] The right GRIP sometimes sticks (not the thumbstick): now and then the game acts as if the right grip is still held after he lets go, so things keep moving or stay grabbed. Intermittent. (a) Find the cause in grip input handling (missed release event, analog grip threshold with no hysteresis, state not cleared on focus loss or pause, or a held object keeping the grip latched); (b) fix it so a released grip always releases, with press/release thresholds and a reset on pause, menu and tracking loss; (c) check the left grip and both triggers for the same bug. Oct 15 evening repeat [18:28, 18:37, 18:42]: the LEFT grip sticks sometimes too ("sometimes can't grip left, sometimes can't grip right"); switching from the far-right hotbar slot to the far-left one wouldn't take until he spammed it, likely the same input fault. Done Oct 16: (a) cause: every grip and trigger was a fixed 0.6 cut on the analog value with no hysteresis, so a relaxed hand whose fingers still rest on the grip (0.6-0.7) read as held forever (a held creature or body stayed in the hand, and the next squeeze never counted because no release came between: the hotbar step that needed spamming), and a value hovering at the cut flickered; also a hand that stopped being tracked only cleared its hold, which left a carried corpse pinned to the hand's last spot; (b) grips and triggers are now VRButtons: down past 0.55 (and 0.3 above the lowest value since the last release, so a squeeze from a resting hand counts), let go 0.3 under their peak (never above 0.7 for a full squeeze), and a menu, the pause, death, focus loss or tracking loss lets go of whatever the hand held without a throw (HandGrab.letGo, ragdoll released too); (c) the left grip, both triggers (pad use/attack, mining toggle, hotbar laser pick, bow nock, deliberate blade hits, menu clicks) and the helm's descend all go through the same states.

P305. [DONE] [T:high] [12:10] Bullets should damage blocks, and enemies trying to reach him should shoot through blocks. (a) Bullet hits chip and break weak blocks by calibre (glass, wood, leaves first); (b) AI that can't path to him shoots through cover or walls; (c) keep it Quest-safe (cap changes per frame). Oct 15 evening repeat [19:06-19:08]: rounds still don't damage blocks; breaking a wall is slow for futuristic weapons. He likes the rocket launcher crater. Done Oct 16: (a) rounds wear the blocks they strike by calibre and shoot them out once worn through (BlockWear.swift): glass and panes at the first round, leaves at one, soft ground in about three rifle rounds, planks in five, stone in eleven, cobble and brick in thirteen, metal blocks in a magazine; a sniper round takes stone in three; obsidian, armoured, tinted and steel blocks, bedrock and anything holding a block entity never break; a round that breaks glass, leaves, a pane or wool goes on through with what it has left; (a, his repeat) the arc gun's bolt burns three times as hard and bores on into the block behind, so a cobble wall goes a bolt at a time; worn blocks shed more grit past half; (b) soldiers' rounds wear blocks too (60 %); a soldier with a shot ready that knows where he is (seen in the last 12 s or locked on) and can't path to him (or is within 16 blocks) stands and fires through up to three wearable blocks of cover, and one in sight behind a window or a plank or two fires through it (the clear-shot check now allows two wearable blocks); rockets keep their old rules; (c) Quest-safe: at most 4 blocks shot out a frame (further wear waits for the next round), at most 768 worn blocks remembered, wear heals after two minutes, and the cover check runs only when a soldier's shot is ready.

P308. [DONE] [T:high] [12:15] Sword fighting is underdeveloped (with P272). (a) A real melee loop: wind-ups, blocks and parries, staggers, hit reactions, and damage by swing speed and angle; (b) enemy melee AI that uses it; (c) good hit feel (sound, haptics, marks). Done Oct 16 (SwordPlay.swift, on top of the VR wind-ups, parries, guards and the P272 duel): (a) the melee loop: a blade's damage follows how it struck (a clean cut across the blade all of it, dragged along its length 55 %, a thrust tip-first 115 %; square into the body all, a graze 70 %, times the speed and wind-up rules); a blade held out across the line of a landing strike blocks it (no damage, a clang and sparks where they meet, the attacker thrown back off balance); a hard blow into a wind-up breaks the strike off and rocks the mob back (stagger), a weak one it shrugs off invites a counter; (b) enemy melee AI: every wind-up strike now comes high (head) or low (hips, about 3 in 10), so the block must meet it where it comes; hostiles feint about 1 wind-up in 7 (it breaks off and the real one follows at once); a guard that turned or parried his blow, or a weak blow, is answered with a riposte (a short 0.22 s wind-up); after a blocked strike they step back to their spacing; (c) hit feel: each landed blow sounds of what it struck (flesh thuds, harder on a big blow; armour rings; bone and stone knock) on top of the HitFeel haptics and the slash marks, and a block jolts both hands.

Done Oct 16: (a) the Ash Vault siren is now a motor air-raid siren (original synthesis, AshSiren.swift): the rotor spins up into a howl over about four seconds, holds, and runs down over five, looping for as long as the alarm lasts; (b) every garrison that sends squads (HQ, staging, motor pools, fuel depots, supply, the towns, the airfield) has a siren on a mast over its centre, the two nearest wail together and fade out by about 380 blocks; (c) a garrison he clears winds its siren down, and all of them stop when the alarm falls back below Alarm or the vault is won.
P320. [DONE] [CLAIMED parallel run, Oct 15 evening] [T:med] [18:54] Add a Panzerfaust-style single-shot anti-tank launcher (name it in-world, real WWII weapons may use their real names). (a) Model, one-shot then discard or reload a warhead; (b) big damage to vehicles and blocks, crater like the rocket launcher; (c) enemies carry and use it; (d) loot and a recipe. Done Oct 16: the Faustrohr (our own name; "Panzer" names are banned by the name check), the Ashguard's single-shot anti-tank launcher. (a) A thin olive tube with the fat drab warhead on its muzzle, leaf sight and trigger lever; one shot, then a slow 3.4 s reload of the next Faustrohr Warhead (the warhead leaves and returns with the reload); (b) its shaped charge does 90 straight through a vehicle's plate (more from behind) on top of a bigger blast that craters the ground like the rocket launcher; (c) three in ten Ashguard heavies carry one and fire it at range; (d) found in Ashguard armouries (tube and warheads) and supply depots (warheads); crafted from 4 iron and a lever (the tube) and TNT, 2 iron and copper (2 warheads).
P321. [DONE] [CLAIMED parallel run, Oct 15 evening] [T:med] [19:07-19:08] Machine-gun rounds don't seem to kill German soldiers (rockets do), in creative. (a) Reproduce: MG and rifle hits on every soldier type; (b) fix any damage, hitbox or armour bug; (c) check in survival and creative; (d) see P302 for difficulty tuning. Done Oct 16: (a) reproduced from the numbers: a round on a soldier went through four integer steps in Mob.hit (the deep army's power, the toughness roll, then the melee armour formula meant for sword blows), so a Hummel round did 0 or 1 to an Ashguard heavy (about 240 rounds to drop one) and 1-2 to a trooper; rockets killed because a direct rocket hit kills outright; (b) a soldier's round is now worked out once in fractions in Armory.impactMob: class plate soaks as before, otherwise rank armour takes 1.5 % a point (at most 35 %), then the toughness roll, and Mob.hit takes it as dealt (Mob.roundHit), so an Ashguard trooper drops in about 13 machine-gun body hits and 8 rifle hits, fewer to the head; (c) nothing in the round path depends on game mode, so survival and creative match; (d) per-level difficulty stays with P302's health table.
P322. [DONE] [T:high] [18:58-18:59] Jetpack and flight. (a) The jetpack thrusts where he leans or points, so he can fly forward fast ("Iron Man my way around"), not only straight up; (b) fuel use scales with thrust; (c) the Hannibal suit can fly too, with heavy fuel use; (d) Hannibal enemies have about a 1% chance to drop their suit; (e) comfort vignette in step. Done Oct 16: (a) jump held thrusts as before, and the move stick pushed forward with it leans the thrust where he looks (the headset's gaze in VR): he flies along it at up to 16 b/s (22 sprinting), the lift holding him up and the strafe sliding him sideways, easing in and out over a quarter second, so he can fly forward fast rather than only climb; (b) fuel burns with the thrust: 1x hovering or climbing, up to 2.5x at full forward thrust and 3.2x sprinting; an empty tank now fizzles instead of a text toast (P328); (c) the full Hannibal plate flies the same way with no jetpack, at three times the burn, feeding on coal, charcoal or blaze powder taken one at a time from the pack as it thrusts (free in creative); (d) a Hannibal trooper's plate set now drops about 1 time in 100 (other troop classes keep 35 %); (e) the VR comfort vignette rises with jet flight speed and the thrust leaning in, in step with the thrust easing.
P323. [DONE] [T:high] [18:48-19:00] Digging down in hell he fell into a void ("a Nether under the Nether", towers of netherite-looking blocks, lava everywhere) and was digging through void. (a) Find the generator gap or chunk state that leaves void below hell; (b) fix the cause so the Ash Vault layer joins cleanly below hell; (c) the Ash Vault entry (the charge or special rock) is findable in survival (he never saw it); (d) scan a few seeds. Done Oct 16: (a) not a generator gap: at his spot (seed 3931512493184456980, about 11680, -13090) he had dug through the Sealrock in creative into the Ash Vault, the open cavern below hell, about 135 blocks from roof to floor, with its spires and lava; above y 128 the cavern drew the hell band's dark red fog, so it read as a void, and the notes' place line read hell's biome at every depth; the place line now names the layer (Upper Crust, Sealrock, Lower Crust, Ash Vault, Molten Core); (b) the vault's own ash haze, fog and light now fill the whole cavern up to its roof (Mac and Quest renderers), with a paler haze, the ember glow kept low and the fog starting further out, so dropping in reads as a lit cavern under a rock roof; (c) one chunk in five has a short seam of Sunderite Ore in hell's floor just above the Sealrock, so digging down to the rock that won't break turns up the ore that breaks it, glowing beside it (the keeps' forges and the Ashguard's chamber with a set charge, P317, show the rest); (d) questcheck --deepscan checks five seeds, 24 chunks each starting at his column: seal holes 0, crust air 0, hell floor air 0, vault roof cells not open 0 on all five, Sunderite seams in 3-6 of 24 chunks.
P324. [DONE] [T:high] [18:54, 19:03, 19:05, 19:07] Drive the German vehicles: "I've got to be able to drive a tank... core feature". (a) Enter, drive and fire the tanks (hands on controls, P282 rules); (b) add their other vehicles: rocket half-tracks, trucks, motorcycles with sidecars; (c) some vehicles drive around and patrol, not all parked; (d) enemy crews use them against him; (e) he can commandeer any of them once cleared. Done Oct 16 (new Sources/AshCommandeer.swift): (a) use a Cinder Tank, half-track or truck once it is cleared (no Ashguard on foot within 20 blocks) or beaten under a third of its health, and it becomes his: its crew climbs out fighting (tank 3, half-track 2, truck 1), and the hull is rebuilt in place on the ship system as a drivable vehicle in the army's black and red, with him at the helm (the hands-on-controls rules of P282 apply). The tank keeps its gun on a turret that follows his view and fires with the trigger, and the half-track's twin guns fire light, quick rounds. A hatch still held (Ashguard near) gives a dull knock and stays shut, with no text; the Behemoth and the field guns stay the army's; (b) half-tracks carry a four-rocket rack: seen from 14-60 blocks off, they loose a salvo about every 9 s on top of the pintle MG. Trucks drive now (3.6 b/s), and one half-track or truck in two keeps an Ashguard Sidecar parked beside it on level ground: a motorcycle (two wheels in line, engine, helm and handlebars) with a car on its own wheel carrying a light machine gun, free to take at its helm; (c) half the tanks, half-tracks and trucks patrol while calm, driving a loop of four points 26 blocks round where they stood (kept across loads); they turn to fight when they see him, and a leg they can't finish is given up after 40 s; (d) the crews still fight from their vehicles (tank gun and MG, half-track MG plus the new rockets), and a hull knocked under a third bails out its crew, who come at him on foot, leaving it empty to take; (e) commandeering works on any of the three once cleared, and on the sidecars at any time. Check: questcheck --ashdrive on a level stone yard: the cleared tank is taken with him at the helm, its crew of 3 out, and it drives 40.9 blocks in 8 s; a half-track at a quarter health stands empty; sidecars parked beside 2 of 6 trucks. Found on the way, not fixed here: on natural slopes any wheeled hull with one block of clearance hangs up where a step meets it mid-body (the Vehicle Works' own Field Tank moved 0.8 blocks in 4 s on the same rolling field, 21.7 on the level yard).
P325. [DONE] [T:xhigh] [19:12-19:16] The frigate needs work. (a) A big fixed main gun at the back/keel that fires straight along the ship's axis (aim by turning the whole ship), a flat fast shot, not an arc; (b) a full interior: hallways, doors, rooms, a bridge, like a base, not a hollow shell (original design, not a copy of any game's ship); (c) a ship-like flight model: pitch and bank, momentum carries forward, no strafing up/down/left/right; (d) crashing into the ground damages or kills; (e) say clearly why it won't move when it can't ("needs engines" etc.) and how to fix it; (f) its first flight works in survival and creative. Done Oct 16: (a) The Tidebreaker is fixed along the keel and runs the hull's whole length. The 63-block barrel between the booms leads to an accelerator housing down the middle of the gun deck (z 64-115, banded with glowing coils), a keel fairing under the spine, and the breech in the stern between two capacitor banks (mainGunLength 115). The helm's shot leaves along the barrel wherever the bow points, so you aim by turning and pitching the ship. The slug is flat and fast (700 blocks/s, no drop; MainGun.slugSpeed, gravity 0), and the aim ring traces that straight line (mainGunAimPoint). The old view-pitch aim (CapitalState.gunPitch) is gone. (b) A full interior, an original layout in new Meridian Deck Grating and Bulkhead Panel blocks (CapitalFrigate.swift): Deck floors at y 8, 14 and 20 through the booms and spine, plus the bridge deck. Boom corridors with cabins (bunks, lockers, lamps) behind doors on two levels. The gun deck: crew consoles, the port hangar (the only open flank door, for the winch), the starboard hold and the breech room. The main deck corridor with fire control, mess, galley, bunk rooms, infirmary, armoury, chart room, signals, stores and workshop. The upper deck's officers' cabins. A ladder trunk from the gun deck to the bridge, and an engine room with bulkheads, a catwalk and ladders. Bridge stations and a plot table. Bulkhead doors on a ship open and close with use (ShipPlay.shipInteract; trapdoors and gates too). No straight line crosses the hull from side to side any more. (c) A ship-like flight model for a commandeered frigate (ShipManager.flyCommandeered): The climb control pitches the bow (it holds the angle, up to 26 degrees). Steer turns the ship and banks it into the turn. The drive pushes along the bow only: way builds and coasts down slowly, sideways drift bleeds off, and nothing moves it straight up, down or sideways. New VehicleControls kind .warship: on Quest the right stick's up/down pitches (VehicleControls.vrPitch), so the left grip no longer sinks the ship. The reclined look-flick is skipped at that helm. (d) A commandeered frigate is solid against the ground (ShipManager.hullContact: the hull outline probed against world blocks, leaves ignored). A closing speed of 4-14 blocks/s at the touching part tears hull and ground, shakes and hurts those aboard, and stops it. Capital.crashSpeed (14) or more wrecks it where it lies: explosions and fire at the contacts, the drive blown, the pilot thrown from the helm and, in survival, hurt (3 x (speed 8) half-hearts, lethal at speed). The old "keep 3 blocks over the ground" lift is now for the drop craft only. (e) No more false repairs book: Wrecks.guide returns nil for kinematic Capital hulls, so taking the frigate's helm opens no "needs a propeller" book. Why it won't move is shown without instruction text: The vehicle panel has a Drive row: a bar for the engines left, and "Engines out" in red under the 40% line, where the drive gives nothing (Capital.drive). Top speed scales with the engines left, and smoke rises from the engine room's empty engine sockets (engineCells are kept for frigates too). Placing Engine blocks back restores the drive. Pitch is shown on the same row, and altitude shows for a warship. (f) First flight from the helm works in survival and creative: take command, no menu, throttle and pitch fly it (FrigateCheck). Tests updated: MainGunTests and FactionTests point the hull with ShipManager.pointHull instead of passing a pitch (barrel length 115); the RideCheck frigate lap and placements move to the port gun deck beside the accelerator. Check: questcheck --frigatecheck (Sources/FrigateCheck.swift, also Mac --frigatecheck): interior counts and no see-through line, a corridor door opens and closes, no repairs book, drive at 30% engines reads 0, flight in survival and creative (along the bow, pitch climb, momentum, banked turn with little slip), the slug along the barrel with no drop, and a bow-first crash wrecks it and kills the survival pilot.
P326. [DONE] [T:xhigh] [18:49-19:04] The Ash Vault looks "too much like hell". Redesign it as its own place: the worst scenes of WWII for the Germans. (a) No lava rivers, less murky sky; foggy, depressing weather but living vegetation: dark forests, no man's land, mud and craters; (b) set pieces: crashed planes, burnt-out vehicles, massacre scenes of fallen Allied soldiers (dead Germans aren't scary, dead Allies are); (c) German bases modelled on real kinds (camps, gun positions, rail lines, a heavy-water site), with their real WWII weapons by name (MP40 and the rest); (d) the existing camps, tanks, rail line and flags he liked stay; (e) old saves migrate; (f) a later full WWII campaign is NOT in scope (he wants the game ready to ship first). Done Oct 16: (a) The Ash Vault is a grey war front, not hell: the lava rivers, blackstone, basalt and soul soil are gone; the floor is grass, podzol, moss and mud under a low grey overcast and thick fog (AshSky), with dark woods, plants and mud creeks living on it, and a churned no man's land of shell holes and mud round the citadel (AshFront.swift, DeepGen). (b) 405 set pieces of five kinds stand round the citadel: crashed bombers and fighters, knocked-out tanks, ambushed convoys and the broken wall, each with wreck blocks and its fallen; the fallen are Allied soldiers in olive drab lying where they died (14 at a wall scene), only while the player is in the vault (AshFallen). (c) Real kinds of German site join the camps: flak batteries (6 guns, 8 men, 4 chests) and a heavy-water plant (penstocks, electrolysis cells, 10 men, 3 chests) (AshSites); the arms carry their real names (Kar98k, MP40 with its own 17-part model and carried by officers, StG 44, Panzerfaust) and the heavy tank is the Tiger Tank, with "Destroy a Tiger Tank"; arms and vehicle names only, logged in docs/status/ip-renames.md and tools/coined-terms.txt. (d) The camps, tanks, rail line and flags stay as they were. (e) Old saves migrate: chunks saved with the old river and camp ground get the new floor in block and field worlds (AshFront.migrate), and new sites, scenes and craters keep off ground an old save already had. (f) No campaign added. Check: questcheck --ashfront (AshFrontCheck.swift), all checks passed (0 lava cells, 8910 of 8924 living tops, 405 scenes, both flak batteries, the plant, the names, 4 migrations, the guard); headset views of the floor, no man's land, flak site, plant and fallen looked right; namecheck --coined 0 hits.

Done Oct 16, together with P297 (one PR): (a) hell's ground in the field style: Pale Ash (soul_sand) is a plain cube now (it was a 14/16-tall shape, so it fell out of the field and its valleys drew as holes: "digging through the void"); it still slows feet; the ash dunes in the valleys are lifted onto the field from the generator's own dune depth, so they draw smooth; at his spot (5962,300,-4327) 98.9% of the surface is the generator's field and the cells rounded from blocks fell from 2.8% to 1.1% (no more "open soul_sand"); the Ash Vault's ground already draws from the field (95.7% at the camp at 300,48,300, 94.4% at the command post): what reads as blocks there is its built camps, trenches and rail embankment, which P326 rebuilds; (b) the green sheen over dug pits was the far ring's ground showing across the hole: the far ring now sinks out of sight inside the near world's edge, so a crater or shaft shows its real depth (checked from the bottom of a 30-block shaft); (c) digging down: looking steeply down while standing on polygon ground targets the ground cell under your own feet (the bowl a dig leaves belongs to the cells round it, so aiming into it took the side cells and the hole widened instead of going down), the ring marks it, and once it breaks you step onto the hole's middle and drop in, as on a block grid; (d) checked against P297 and shipped with it.

## TALKING NPCS (Remington, Oct 16 15:37): queued for later, do not start before Remington says go
P329. [CLAIMED by Remington: ON HOLD until he says go] [T:xhigh] [Remington, Oct 16 15:37] Talking NPCs: the AI voices the characters instead of only directing events (builds on P266, the Director). His words: "have them voice the characters... keep a memory of how you treat, how you talk to characters in the game... you just speak out loud and they'll be able to hear you... they'll just react, but it'll be the AI deciding how they react. That way there's more of a connection with the NPCs." (a) Speech in: hold a button (or look at an NPC and talk) and the headset mic transcribes him (on-device speech recognition first, cloud as an option); (b) the NPC's reply is decided by the model from a compact prompt: who the NPC is (role, faction, town, mood), what he just said, what is happening around them, and that NPC's memory of him; the reply comes back as strict JSON (spoken line, emotion, action from a fixed list: trade, give a quest, flee, attack, follow, refuse, call guards, gift, remember), validated by the game, never run as code; (c) voice out: text-to-speech with a distinct voice per NPC (age, sex, accent picked once and saved), lip/jaw movement and head turned to him; (d) memory: each NPC keeps a short log of how he treated them (kind, rude, threatening, helped, robbed, lied), summarised so it stays small, and their attitude, prices, help and hostility follow it; gossip spreads the gist to their town, so a town remembers him; (e) backends use the P266 DirectorBackend split: rules fallback (canned lines by mood) always works, on-device small model where it can run in time, cloud model behind a settings toggle; replies must start speaking within about 2 s or fall back to a canned line so it never stalls; (f) never costs frames: model, speech and voice run off the render thread; (g) settings: Talking NPCs on/off, backend, push-to-talk vs look-to-talk; (h) saves: every NPC's memory and voice persist; (i) IP and content rules apply to everything NPCs say (name filter, no real people), and the NO TEXT rule (no subtitles unless he turns them on in settings).

## PLAYTEST Oct 16 midday (P330-P339): build 0.129 (60d57643), 11:50-12:01, polygon world, a base, hell; tiered (see TIERS)
Notes: docs/playtests/2026-10-16/voice-notes.md. He played the 11:41 build, so P297, P325-P328 (in the 15:52 build) may already cover some; check first. He loved: the 42 cm flak guns ("this thing fucks things up"), the sky islands, morning sun, the new hell biomes (the purple one most), lava-rock paths, the fire phantoms, dropping straight into hell. He wants a new game title some day ("we're turning away from blocks... the title is Blocksmith"): his call, do not rename anything.

It is gone only while a sortie is up (an alerted citadel flies the crewed one off and lands it back) or for good once the player has taken it.
P334. [DONE] [T:high] [11:55-11:57] Drop ships. (a) Enemy drop ships have crew inside actually flying them; (b) drop ship guns do almost nothing: give them damage on the order of the flak guns; (c) flight feel like an agile military VTOL (inspiration: the Falcon in Halo Reach; original design, no copied assets): bank into turns, hover, strafe, nose follows the stick; (d) comfort: he felt like he was falling over while flying it; keep the horizon steady (cockpit-fixed frame, limited roll shown to the eyes) with an option for full roll. STEPS (written Oct 17): 1. Capital.dropship() gets crew posts, a pilot behind the helm and a gunner under the turret ring (CapitalShips.swift); check: two posts. 2. launchDropship/callDropship copy the posts into the craft's CapitalState and dropshipTick seats them through the frigate's crew loop, factored out as spawnCrew; check: a called dropship carries two soldiers with deck === it. 3. A dead pilot means the craft loses control and goes down (the wreck path); a dead gunner silences its gun (turretManned); check: the sim. 4. Guns: the dropship's autocannon fires Armory slugs at flak strength (a 1.7 burst like AirDefence plus 7 on a direct hit, red tracers, a proximity fuze against fliers) instead of 1.8 ship shells; check: the numbers. 5. Flight: a commandeered dropship flies as a VTOL (commandeeredTick): the stick's forward/back accelerates along the nose and dips it, left/right strafes and banks into the slide, the turn input yaws with the nose following smoothly and banking into the turn, climb lifts, no input hovers in place; check: input sim prints speed, bank and pitch. 6. Quest: at a dropship's helm the right stick yaws (x) and climbs (y) instead of snap-turning (VehicleControls.vrRight, QuestControls), with matching prompts; check: compile and the control read. 7. AI dropships bank into their turns and dip the nose as they speed up, with the same attitude code, so enemy craft look flown; check: the sim. 8. Comfort: the view keeps a level horizon (the rig follows yaw only, as before); a Quest option, Craft Bank Gentle or Full, limits how far the hull you ride banks and pitches (gentle 8 and 5 degrees, full 22 and 12); check: the option reads through. Done Oct 18 (steps 1-8): (a, steps 1-3) drop ships carry crew: a pilot at the helm and a gunner under the turret, seated by the frigate's crew loop (now spawnCrew, shared); a dead gunner silences the gun; a dead pilot sends the craft down: it yaws off and sinks under idling engines until it sits on the ground, not wrecked, and only then can it be taken from the helm (a crewed, uncaptured drop ship needs its pilot dead first); (b, step 4) its autocannon fires Armory slugs at flak strength (shell rounds, 7 on a direct hit, flak blast power, tracers, a proximity fuze against flying or riding players) instead of 1.8 ship shells; (c, steps 5-7) a commandeered drop ship flies as a VTOL: left stick forward 28 / back 12 along the nose and strafe 14, right stick x turns (eased) and y climbs 9 (RB/LB too), throttle set directly with no telegraph; nose and wings pitch and bank with the acceleration (steerAttitude), the AI ships bank and pitch the same way; the Quest helm takes the right stick for it (no snap turn); (d, step 8) Options > Comfort: Craft Bank Gentle (default, about 5 degrees pitch / 8 bank) or Full (12 / 22). Check (--p334 probe, local): a called drop ship carries 2 crew, the AI banks up to 6.8 degrees, a dead pilot leaves it settled on the ground at speed 0, not wrecked, and commandeerable; flown: climb 9 b/s, forward 27.9, strafe 13.9, turn 1.3 rad/s, banking 8 degrees in a turn. Its gun did not fire in the probe (no hostile target in reach there).

P337. [T:xhigh] [11:53-11:56] Railways. (a) Rails look wrong (gravel drawn over them) and lines end abruptly; (b) procedural rail lines that connect towns, villages and bases across the world, following the ground with cuttings, bridges and tunnels; (c) working trains that run them (locomotive and cars, stations), which he can ride and drive; (d) works in field (polygon) worlds first. P338. [T:xhigh] [11:52, 11:58-11:59] POLYGON FOR REAL (see POLYGON STRICT and THE POLYGON DIRECTION): "we're still operating from a block-by-block basis... these are problems with the fact that we're still using a block overlay... big task, I know, it's gotta be done." What he saw: tree trunks not attached to the ground, dripstone not joined to the ground and drawn as "paper fold" cards up close, lava not meeting the ground, the far view still bad. (a) Finish the handoff's open steps: step 9 pathing on the field, step 10 at least two density samples per block (finer if the budget allows), step 11 water and lava as field-surface fluids that meet the ground; (b) every natural feature is a real mesh joined to the field: dripstone, stalactites, spikes, roots, trunks, logs, boulders; no cards or billboards for anything solid; (c) ground under and around structures, roads and rails is shaped in the field, not cut or banked as cells; (d) the far view draws the field's real silhouette at every distance; (e) add a debug counter of natural-terrain block faces and cell lookups per frame in a field world; both must read 0 on his seed at the spots in the notes; (f) list in the BUILT note every place that still reads the block grid in a field world (grep World.block / getBlock / cell lookups on field-world paths) and why; anything left is a bug to fix, not a note; (g) when built, tag it as built and awaiting his playtest per POLYGON STRICT, never DONE. [Remington, Oct 16 16:00: "there can't be a grid... no block grid... it's gotta figure something out. Whatever it is, it's got to be compute efficient."] (h) No block grid anywhere the player can see or feel: no cube cells in terrain, digging, placing, collision, pathing or the far view. Internally the field can keep sample points (every smooth-terrain engine does), but they must be fine enough and adaptive (sparse chunks, an octree or level-of-detail samples: finer where there is detail, coarser where there is none) so nothing rounds to block size; (i) player building moves off the block grid too: building pieces (walls, floors, beams, stairs, roofs, doors, windows) placed freely and snapped to each other and to the ground, not to a world grid; existing block worlds and builds keep loading; (j) it must cost no more frame time or memory than the block engine it replaces on the Quest: measure both and put the numbers in the BUILT note. Oct 16 evening repeat (build 46151e50) [19:42, 19:46, 19:51, 19:58, 20:04, 20:06, 20:09]: "this is all blocks"; lava still conforms to blocks; walking around he can see where a raised block level used to be; water still shows blocks; floating blocks in a ravine; town plank floors make bumps in the ground; a log floating over water. STEPS (Oct 17, medium effort): 1 list every field-world code path that still reads the block grid (World.block / getBlock / cell lookups) with file:line in docs/status/polygon-field.md; 2 add the two debug counters (natural block faces drawn, cell lookups per frame) to the debug overlay; 3 handoff step 9: Pathfinding.standCost / open use World.fieldBoxes / SmoothField.restHeight in field worlds; 4 step 10: two samples per block in FieldTerrain (fd/fm doubled per axis), save format versioned, old field saves upsampled on load; 5 step 11: water and lava as field-surface fluids meshed against the field; 6 dripstone, spikes, roots, trunks, logs and boulders as real meshes joined to the field (no cards for solids); 7 ground under structures, roads and rails shaped in the field; 8 player building pieces placed freely and snapping to each other and the ground in field worlds (block worlds unchanged); 9 the far view comes from P339; 10 both counters read 0 at the spots in his notes; built note with numbers. Oct 17 playtest (build 129, 77871062) [20:28]: water not settling onto the beach; his words: "I'm hoping when we change the whole block thing, everything kind of fits into place... a whole physics engine modeled after the real world... don't just fix the error specifically, fix the physics behind it so the error doesn't happen." Fix root causes in the field/physics, not one-off patches.
P339. [T:xhigh] [11:50-11:57, 12:00-12:01] Performance and the far view, again ("this one's been failed too many times... we're not getting performance"): auto render fell to 15 and 20, and far terrain "still sucks". (a) The game records its own performance: when frame time stays bad, it saves a capture (scene, position, render distance, CPU vs GPU time, top systems, draw and triangle counts) next to the voice notes so the pull brings it in, and a short haptic or sound tells him a capture was taken ("hey, we've got an issue"); (b) use his captures from the next playtest and QuestSim runs of his seed to fix the real top costs; (c) target: holds render 24 at 72 fps in his world on the surface and in hell; (d) measured before and after numbers from the device in the BUILT note; (e) when built, tag it as built and awaiting his playtest per POLYGON STRICT, not DONE. [Remington, Oct 16 16:00: "performance is still not where I'd like it to be. It's good, not great."] Performance is the top priority alongside P338: every frame-time win counts. Oct 16 evening repeat (build 46151e50) [20:04, 20:08, 20:14]: forcing 24 he got 12 fps; even in survival at 20 chunks the ground around him takes forever to appear; a far volcano draws before the land around it. His idea, build it: two distances, a short SIMULATION distance where things move and think, and a long VIEW distance where everything is drawn (trees, buildings, mobs as still poses) but nothing updates. [Remington, Oct 17 13:09, NEW PLAN, replaces the earlier parts]: "24 render distance, who cares... you can see 24, but it would be the loading distance that matters... what entities and things are actually working in that distance, but you can still see really far... replace the current thing where the distance looks less polished, like a compression. I have always complained about it in each of my runs." STEPS: 1 split the setting in two: VIEW distance fixed at 24 chunks (everything is drawn) and an ACTIVE distance slider (4-16, default 8): inside it mobs, vehicles, physics, fluids, crops, machines and AI run; outside it nothing ticks, chunks are generated, meshed and drawn only, and mobs out there are drawn in their last pose; 2 the Auto governor never touches the view distance again: when frames suffer it lowers the active distance and far mesh density instead; 3 far land is the real world, not FarRing's coloured height field or the horizon shell: chunks out to 24 draw from simplified meshes of the actual terrain with the same materials, textures and lighting as near ground, so it reads as the same world, just less dense; trees and buildings far off as simplified real meshes or impostors baked from the real model with matching lighting, never flat blobs; retire FarRing / HorizonRing inside 24 chunks; 4 far chunk meshing and uploads run on worker threads spread across frames, far chunks cached compactly, memory measured on the Quest; 5 measure on the headset, his seed, surface and hell: 72 fps at view 24 / active 8; 6 screenshots at 8, 16 and 24 chunks in the built note. Oct 17 playtest (build 129, 77871062) [15:32, 16:04, 20:24-20:25]: heavy lag at the start; "something's hogging up all the compute"; the Auto governor dropped render distance to 8 and he was not happy ("love that", sarcastic). With the NEW PLAN the view distance never drops; find what spiked at 16:04 (log frame times by subsystem).

P341. [DONE] [T:high] [19:42-19:44] The Burgundy knights. (a) Make them actually burgundy (deep red), not the current colour; (b) several knight classes with their own looks, arms and fighting styles (e.g. sword and shield, two-hander, spear, mounted, a captain); (c) they really sword-fight him using the P308 melee system: guard, cuts from angles, feints, staggers and ripostes, not just walking into him. Checked on host renders of each style idle and winding up.

P347. [DONE] [T:high] [retagged from med Oct 17: a new Ash Vault set piece with its own raid and liberation loop, prisoner behaviour and a content-rules review] [19:53] A prison camp set piece in the Ash Vault (he suggested a concentration camp): a German camp he can raid and liberate, freeing the prisoners. Treat it with weight, as the horror the Ash Vault is about, never as decoration or a joke; check it against Meta store content rules before it ships. Done Oct 18 (PrisonCamp.swift, AshSites.swift "prisoncamp"; content notes in docs/design/prison-camp.md): (a) the camp: the Ashguard's prison camp south-east of the citadel at 160, 300, inside the Ember Line: a double wire fence on stakes with one gate (lamps on its beam, the wire leaves standing open), four watchtowers with a sentry and a lamp each, the gatehouse, the guards' block with the commandant's papers (command and armoury chests), a bare roll-call ground, and four long huts of raw planks with two tiers of bare bunks; no sign and no text; 12 guards and a truck; (b) the prisoners (33): civilians of their own model, gaunt, in worn, patched, ill-fitting clothes, no stripes, numbers or badges; most sit on the bunks' edge, some stand in the huts and five on the roll-call ground; they turn their heads toward him when he is near; (c) they can never be harmed: every blow, shot, blast or fire is ignored, they belong to no side so no soldier aims at them, and nothing rewards harming them; (d) liberation: when no Ashguard soldier is left alive inside the wire and he is there for three seconds, the camp is free (kept with the world, vault state "prisoncamp"), the gate's bolt sounds, and the prisoners rise one by one, walk out of their huts, across the ground, through the gate and up the road, and are gone once out of his sight; (e) content check against the Meta store rules: a fictional army, no real camp, people or Nazi symbols, no killing or cruelty shown, no bodies, gas chambers or ovens, nothing for comedy, the prisoners never targets (docs/design/prison-camp.md). Saves whose vault already had that ground keep it as it was. Old saves keep whatever they already generated. P353. [DONE] [T:high] [retagged from med Oct 17: hand and arm poses fitted to every held item on every character model, a rig-wide pass] [20:05] A beached pirate's gun passes through his arm. Fix it generally: held items and body parts don't pass through each other (hand and arm poses fit the item held), on every character. Done Oct 18 (RigClearance.swift, SoldierRig.swift, PoseCheck.swift): (a) the cause: every character that holds a weapon (every soldier rank, the corsairs, the Ashguard, crews, bounty hunters, the marshal and chancellor: all one rig) placed the weapon and the hands by stance and bent the elbows one fixed way, with nothing stopping the weapon from running through an arm or the chest; measured over every rank, every weapon and every stance, 353 of 1052 poses had it through an arm or the body (low ready and the march carry worst, the corsair's carbine among them); (b) the fix, for all of them at once: after a stance places the weapon, the rig measures how deep its centre line (butt to muzzle and its magazine) sinks into each arm (upper arm and forearm, the fist holding it excepted) and the torso, swings each elbow out round the weapon (out, down or back, whichever clears it), and if that is not enough moves the weapon clear with the hands on it, keeping them within reach; a slung or holstered weapon is moved off the body the same way; (c) the pose check now holds every rank, weapon and stance to it: after the fix none is deeper than 0.45 px (the arm's own surface), and no other pose check changed (aim, hands on the grip and fore grip, muzzle ahead, boots on the ground). Other creatures hold their items at the end of a simple arm and were not affected. Checked: questcheck --posecheck-only (weapon clearance: 353 of 1052 before, worst after 0.45 px; the 96 remaining failures are the old ones for the needle and breaker in a seat and the chancellor's left hand, unchanged).

Checked: questcheck --frigatecheck (all P325 checks plus the new ones: rooms and doors, no take-over away from the helm, careful landing 0 blocks lost and no harm, the crash's broken hull and 0 lights left, a 30-degree shot's crater 5.2 blocks on along the shot).
P358. [BUILT, awaiting his playtest] [T:high] [Remington, Oct 16 21:42: "the difficulty one is one I've asked for many times, easy is too hard. Also the Nazis are still too tough."] P302 built the five levels to his spec (Sources/Difficulty.swift), yet in play Easy still kills him fast and German soldiers still soak up too many rounds, so the numbers don't reach the player. His spec stands: enemy health realistic and the same on every level; the levels change his side (damage taken, armour, health) and the enemy's edge (speed, aim, smarts, numbers); Easy is a cakewalk for a new player, Normal a seasoned player dies several times learning, Hard is for people who want to struggle. (a) Realistic enemy health: a soldier (every German type, Ashguard included, brutes too) dies to one or two pistol or rifle hits to the body, one to the head, a short burst from a machine gun, and any direct cannon or rocket hit; write a table of hits-to-kill per weapon per soldier type, measured in the game, into the built note; (b) audit every way damage reaches the player (bullets, melee, explosions, artillery, tank and vehicle guns, fire, falls, drowning, hell's creatures) and route each one through the difficulty row, so nothing bypasses it; list each path and its numbers; (c) a measured check per level: a scripted run through an Ash Vault camp and a German base as a mid-geared player, deaths and damage taken per level, and Easy must come out at zero or one death; (d) when built, tag it as built and awaiting his playtest (as POLYGON STRICT does), never DONE: only he closes it. Built Oct 18, awaiting his playtest (Difficulty.swift, Game.swift hurtPlayer/damage/levelShare, Ballistics.swift impactMob, DifficultyCheck.swift): (a) soldiers die like men, whatever their plate, rank or toughness roll (the Ash Marshal and the Chancellor stay bosses): one round to the head, one heavy rifle round (9 or more: Kar98k, Fernblick, Longwatch) to the body, two rifle or pistol rounds, four lighter rounds (MP40, Hummel, Kettensturm), any direct rocket or shell. Measured in the game (questcheck --difficulty-only), body / head, the same for every type (Ashguard recruit, trooper, marksman, MG gunner, officer, heavy, and a Capital heavy in Hannibal plate): Falke pistol 2/1, Sechser revolver 2/1, MP40 4/1, StG 44 2/1, Kar98k 1/1, Kettensturm MG 4/1, rocket 1, cannon shell 1. (b) every way damage reaches the player now takes the level's row: attacks through hurtPlayer (mob melee and bites, soldiers' rounds, beams, blasts and grenades, artillery and mortar shells, tank, half-track and deck guns, ship cannons, the Tidebreaker's shockwave, hell's creatures: Searwings, Scorchlings, Slagthrowers, the Brand Order, crashes) with the share, the single-blow cap and, new, a per-second limit; and everything else through Game.damage (falls, fire and lava, drowning, freezing, cactus and berry bushes, lightning, falling anvils, crash landings, magic and wither, poison, heatstroke), which before took full damage on every level; the void and starving (its own hunger row) aside. Numbers: Very Easy takes a fifth of a blow (was a quarter), at most 1.5 hearts a blow and 1.5 hearts in any second; Easy a third (was 0.45), at most 2.5 hearts a blow and 2.5 in any second (a machine gun's stream was a dozen capped hits a second); Normal, Hard and Very Hard unchanged (full, 1.5x, 2x). (c) the fight check, a mid-geared player (iron set, StG 44) standing his ground in the open against an Ash Vault camp (6) and a German base (10 in two waves): Very Easy 0 deaths (camp 0 half-hearts, base 1), Easy 0 deaths (1, 0); Normal camp 0 deaths, base 13; Hard 21 and 46; Very Hard 70 and 22 with soldiers left (no cover used, so the upper levels read harsher than play).

(a) Ship them on the Mac and the Quest (APK assets/music/), streamed from disk, never loaded whole: AVAudioFile on the Mac, AMediaExtractor + AMediaCodec on the Quest; (b) they replace the procedural composer as the default soundtrack (keep the composer as a "Classic" option; Custom Music still works); (c) a music director picks the track from the game state with 3-4 s crossfades and never restarts a track while the same state continues: title -> main_title; loading -> loading_screen; surface day/night -> overworld_daytime / overworld_night; rain, snow, desert, jungle, swamp, mountains, old growth forest (taiga and forests), ocean (on a boat or open water) -> ocean_sailing, underwater, caves (underground), the_hollow, emberdeep, sky_islands, village (in a village), capital_city (in a Capital city), military_base_infiltration (inside a German base unseen), danger_nearby (enemies close), combat, boss_battle, raid_incoming (Director raid), creative_mode, ash_vault_front (Ash Vault calm), ash_vault_battle (Ash Vault fighting), no_mans_land (that biome), hell_exploration (hell default), hell_purple_wastes, hell_red_forest, brimstone_keep, knight_duel (melee with a knight), frigate_command, drop_ship_flight, submarine, helicopter, trains_and_railways (riding a train), death, victory (story beaten); (d) calm states leave 30-90 s of quiet between tracks; combat, danger and boss take over at once and hand back with a crossfade; (e) loudness: measure each track and store a gain so all play at one level; (f) the APK stays under Meta's size limit; (g) the Mac app name and title use Sunderfall (P340). Oct 17 playtest (build 129, 77871062) [15:39]: cave music doesn't seem to fade in when he goes underground. Done Oct 18: (a) the 40 tracks ship and stream from disk: the Mac app bundle's Resources/Music/sunderfall (build.sh copies them), played by two AVAudioPlayerNodes with scheduleFile (SoundtrackMac.swift); the Quest APK's assets/music (stored uncompressed, -0 m4a), decoded a block at a time by AMediaExtractor + AMediaCodec from the APK's file descriptor (quest/src/android/MediaTrackDecoder.swift, libmediandk), resampled 48 to 44.1 kHz into a ring per deck and mixed by the software mixer (quest/src/common/QuestTracks.swift); (b) Options > Audio > Soundtrack is now Sunderfall (the default), My Music, Mixed (own music and Classic) or Classic (the procedural composer); on the Quest the row is back and switches Sunderfall / Classic; the composer still plays when no track files are there; (c) a music director (Soundtrack.swift) picks one of the 40 states from the game (title page, loading, credits = victory, death, bosses, a Brand knight within 7 blocks, the Ash Vault front / battle / no man's land, raids, fights, the craft at the helm: frigate, drop ship, submarine, helicopter, riding a train, a Steelhold base with its garrison near, enemies near, Emberdeep, the Hollow and its outer islands as sky_islands, hell's Gloamwood as purple wastes and Charwood Thicket as red forest, Brimstone Keep, deep caves, underwater, creative, capital city, village, boats and ocean, snow, rain, night, desert, jungle, swamp, mountains, forests and taiga as old growth forest, else daytime), crossfades 3.5 s (equal-power) on a change and never restarts the track while its state goes on; a state has to hold 2 s before a calm track changes; (d) a calm track that ends rests 30-90 s before it plays again; combat, danger, bosses, knights, raids, death and victory cut in at once (1.5 s) and hand back over 4 s; (e) loudness: tools/soundtrack_gains.sh measured every track with ffmpeg's EBU R128 meter and wrote Sources/SoundtrackGains.swift (each track to -16 LUFS, the composer's level, peaks held under -1 dBFS); (f) the APK grows by 89 MB, well under Meta's 1 GB APK limit; (g) already done by P340 (the Mac app name and title read Sunderfall). The Oct 17 note (cave music not fading in underground): going underground is now a state change, so the caves track fades in after 2 s even during another state's quiet.

(a) Swimming sound is repetitive: rotate several takes and vary pitch; (b) hitting stone plays two sounds at once and both are wrong: one correct stone-hit sound only; (c) the item pickup sound ("receiving a block") sounds weird: replace with a soft natural pickup; (d) the "full" sound when eating is cringe: remove it; (e) villager and trader "hmm" sounds are ridiculous, the trader both talks and hums, and every buy or sell plays a strange shouting voice: remove the hums and the shout, use a quiet coin/cloth sound for a trade; (f) walking through ferns is too repetitive: more variants for every footstep surface (log which names need more takes in the done note so the overseer can generate them); (g) two unexplained odd sounds (16:07 near a structure, 20:18 after dropping items): log every sound played with its name in debug builds so these can be found, and fix them. Done Oct 18: (a) swimming: the stroke now takes turns through three takes (the recording, and the same resampled 0.91x and 1.09x) with a per-play pitch and level jitter; (b) mining: a chip's knock is the strike's one sound (the 0.25 s mining tap no longer lands on top of it); the stone take itself is the recorded hit_stone (a single knock); if it still reads wrong, it needs a new take; (c) picking up: a soft muffled pat with a little cloth rustle, synthesized (the recorded pickup take is gone); (d) no burp after eating; (e) villagers and traders no longer hum or grunt (idle "hmm", yes, no, and the hum in place of a line with no voice take); a trade is two coins clinking into a purse with a cloth rustle (the recorded shouting take is gone); (f) footsteps on every surface and brushing through plants also turn through three times their takes (resampled lower and higher). Names that still need more recorded takes (overseer): every step_* has 3 (step_wool and step_glass 2), plantBrush 1, swim 1; (g) every sound played is logged (a 64-entry ring, SoundLog.swift) and every voice note and bug note now carries "sounds": each sound of the 12 s before the note, by name, with its age and distance. The two odd sounds of 16:07 and 20:18 are not identified from the notes alone; the 20:18 one after dropping items was most likely the old pickup take (replaced in c); the next note near either will name it.

P368. [DONE] [T:high] (retagged from med Oct 18: the video path needs the eye image handed from Vulkan to the hardware encoder's input surface (a swapchain on AMediaCodec's ANativeWindow, or a GPU downscale plus YUV readback), a packet ring, MP4 muxing and headset timing of the cost: more than a medium pass) [Remington, Oct 17 20:59] Voice notes get video. Playtest builds only (STORE=1 leaves it out with the rest of the voice-note code). STEPS: 1 keep a rolling 10 s video buffer in memory: each frame, downscale the left-eye image on the GPU to 960x540 (or 1/4 of the eye) and feed it to the Quest's hardware H.264 encoder (AMediaCodec, surface input, ~2 Mbps, 30 fps, keyframe every 1 s), keeping the last 10 s of encoded packets in a ring buffer; nothing is written to storage while he is silent; 2 when a voice note starts, write the buffered 10 s plus everything until the note ends (plus 2 s) into vn-<stamp>.mp4 next to the note's audio and sidecar (AMediaMuxer), and list it in the sidecar; 3 cost: the downscale and encode must cost under 0.5 ms of frame time; measure it on the headset with and without and put both numbers in the done note; a settings toggle (playtest builds) turns it off for performance runs; 4 storage cap: if the voicenotes folder passes 2 GB, the game deletes the oldest clips first (audio and sidecars stay); 5 tools/quest-bugnotes.py: pull the mp4s with the rest, extract 4 frames per clip (start of buffer, when he starts talking, middle, end) with ffmpeg into voice-shots/ as small JPGs linked from the note, keep the mp4 on the Mac only (~/Documents/Blocksmith/QuestVoiceNotes, not the repo), and make deleting pulled files from the headset the default (--keep to skip); 6 the existing single screenshot stays as the fallback when there is no clip. DONE Oct 18 (built; headset cost numbers pending his next session): (1) while Bug Notes listens, about 30 times a second the frame thread blits the left eye (HUD included) to a quarter-size staging buffer on the GPU (QuestScreenshot.record, four buffers in turn, no CPU copy on the frame thread); a utility queue turns it into NV12 once that frame's fence has signalled and feeds the hardware H.264 encoder (quest/c/voice/voicevideo.c: AMediaCodec, 2 Mb/s, 30 fps, a keyframe every second), which keeps the last 10 s of packets in a memory ring; nothing is written while he is silent; (2) a note starting opens vn-<stamp>.mp4 (AMediaMuxer) with the buffered 10 s, then everything until 2 s after the note ends; a dropped note's clip is deleted; the sidecar lists the video; (3) cost: every 10 s the log says "voicevideo: frame thread X ms a capture; convert + encode Y ms on the queue; N pictures, D dropped"; Options > Interface > Bug Note Video turns it off; the with/without numbers come from his next headset session; (4) the 2 GB cap deletes the oldest clips first, then audio as before; (5) tools/quest-bugnotes.py pulls the mp4s, extracts 4 frames per clip with ffmpeg (start of buffer, when he starts talking, middle, end) into voice-shots as JPGs linked from the note, keeps the mp4s on the Mac only, and deletes pulled finished notes from the headset by default (--keep skips it); (6) the single screenshot stays when there is no clip. STORE=1 leaves all of it out (storecheck greps bs_venc_, AMediaMuxer_ and voicevideo: 0 failures on the playtest APK). Check: the playtest APK and the host build compile clean.

P371. [DONE] [T:high] [Remington, Oct 18 07:54:54] Physical held items. "If I hold on to an item and swing my arm, that thing should go flying. Should be able to hit stuff too. Full-on ragdoll physics." STEPS: (1) A held item follows the hand with real momentum; letting go of grip mid-swing throws it with the hand's velocity and spin. (2) Thrown items fly, bounce and come to rest physically, and can be picked up again. (3) A thrown or swung item that hits a creature or person does damage scaled by mass and speed (a sword hurts, bread barely). (4) A creature or person killed or knocked hard goes into a ragdoll: limbs fall with gravity and collide with the ground and blocks, no animation snap. (5) Hard hits on living targets give a short partial ragdoll stagger. (6) Keep cost low: ragdolls sleep after settling, max few active at once. (7) Test once on the Quest: throw the sword at a cow and at a bandit. DONE Oct 18 (built; his headset throw at a cow and a bandit is step 7): (1) on the headset, the right grip held on whatever is in the hand (not a gun) is a hold; swung and let go, it leaves the hand with the hand's tracked speed (smoothed, times 1.6, at most 24 b/s); a quick grip tap still steps the hotbar, now on release (QuestControls); (2) it flies as a dropped item, bounces off the ground and walls (ItemEntity.thrown), comes to rest and is picked up again by walking over it; (3) in flight it hits the first creature or person in its way once, for the item's own attack times its weight (a weapon or tool whole, a block a fair lump, food or a feather little) by its speed: an iron sword hard thrown does 6, a loaf 0; the hit knocks back by weight and speed, and on a townsperson it counts as an attack (Mob.provoke); (4) a kill drops the body as a ragdoll as every death already does (MobRagdolls); (5) a hard hit (4+ damage or over 14 b/s, and any melee blow of 6+) staggers them for half a second (Mob.stagger: rocked back and twisted in MobAnim.pose); (6) at most 6 flights tracked, each ends at rest or after 5 s, and ragdolls keep their existing pool and sleep; check (questcheck --throw-only): the sword thrown at a cow hits once for 6, staggers it, lands and is picked up; the loaf at a townsman hits for 0 and is picked up; the APK compiles.

## THE POLYGON DIRECTION (Remington, Oct 13 22:05): the biggest item now, take right after P221-P251
He played the polygon world on the 21:25 build and decided: "this will be the way forward, no more Minecrafty stuff." He loves the forests, fallen logs, caves and new village buildings. What is wrong comes from blocks still being the base under the smooth surface. A frozen block-world fallback build is kept on the Mac (git tag blocks-fallback-oct13); do NOT maintain a parallel block version, put the effort into the polygon game. Build these thoroughly; each is big, so split into several PRs (one per working piece), each pushed and merged.
PG1. [DONE] No blocks underneath: the polygon world's terrain is a true smooth volumetric field (density/SDF data meshed with dual contouring or marching cubes, materials blended), not smoothed block cells. Digging, explosions and bullets carve the field and break pieces off procedurally (the realistic-world fracture work), including the ground itself. Saves store the field. Done Oct 14 (PRs #253-#260, docs/status/polygon-field.md): a Polygons world keeps its natural ground as a chunked density field (an Int8 distance sample per block cell, 1/32-block precision, plus a material id per sample), shaped by each dimension's own continuous generator fields (93-100% of surface cells), saved and loaded as the field, meshed by dual contouring with blended materials near and far; digging, blasts and rounds subtract from it and broken pieces are rigid-body debris. Still remaining: one sample per block, so shapes under a block across round off; a few surface cells (ground a structure cut or banked, stalactites, spike bases) take a smoothed shape from their cells; materials are the generator's choice per cell; water and lava stay cell fluids (their surfaces meet the smooth ground).
PG2. [DONE] Physics on the surface: the player, creatures, items and vehicles collide with the mesh, not with block cells. Sheep and animals never sink into or hover over the ground; moving through caves is easy (capsule vs mesh, crouch to pass low gaps); ragdolls rest on the real surface. Done Oct 14 (PRs #254-#260): the player, mobs, animals, items, debris and ragdolls meet the field (quarter-block columns from the trilinear field as drawn; ragdolls and debris against its distance), vehicles' hulls and wheels the density; dropped bodies rest within 0.02 block of the drawn surface on average; field caves walk easier than block caves. Still remaining: AI pathing plans on cells (a cell inside the ground counts as ground).
PG3. [DONE] Distance: far terrain is drawn as cheap smooth meshes that keep the real silhouette, never square blocks (with F4).
PG4. [DONE] Objects as real shapes: tree trunks show their bark outside, never their inside; birch trees get real birch shapes (not random procedural sticks); fallen logs sit on the ground (none floating); pumpkins are round, and can be picked up instead of breaking apart; everything that looks boxy gets its real shape.
PG5. [DONE] Roads on smooth ground blend into each other and into the terrain.
PG6. [DONE] Port all content onto the polygon world: towns, bases, Capital sites, structures, caves, hell and the Reich layers, so a polygon world plays the whole game. Then make Polygon the default for new worlds. Done Oct 14 (PRs #255-#260): ores, caves, water, lava, structures, towns, bases, Capital sites, the Emberdeep, the Hollow and the Deep's hell band and Ash Vault generate into the field world; a field-world playthrough runs spawn to the Ash Chancellor's ending with all 124 checks passing; new worlds (Create New World and Quick New World) are field worlds, old block worlds keep loading as block worlds. Still remaining: structures stay placed blocks on the field (by design); an old block world is not converted to a field.
PG7. [DONE] Creative mode: no damage to the player.

## PLAYTEST Oct 13 night (P221-P251): top priority with the polygon items
He played the 21:25 build (3148f425, before PR #112 merged the R/F/S batch) 21:27-22:02; notes in docs/playtests/2026-10-13/voice-notes.md after 21:27. Ragdolls (21:40), freezes (21:41, 21:57) and render-distance lag (21:52) may be covered by #112; check on the current build first.

Some guns spawn without their top, half generated: fix the generation. Put a helicopter on a tower landing pad that he can steal.

Walk the whole completion path in code from his existing world and fix anything that would block finishing it. Gameplay fixes only, no test bots. Traced (Oct 14): his World376 sits about 19,300 blocks from the world centre, and the Ash Vault citadel (the Marshal, the last battle, the bunker) is under the centre, so breaking through where he plays means a 19 km march underground. The Ember Charge was loot only (Capital command rooms, fleet frigates he has never seen). Fixed: a forged Ember Charge (3 Titanium Ingots, 3 TNT, a gold block); the Wardstone hint, the charge toast and the goal say to go down near the world's centre and how far it is; the last few units of a battle wave glow and the toast gives their bearing after 25 s, so a straggler stuck in a trench can't stall the battle. Keeps, Sunderite, flux, the blast furnace, the seal, the vault fall and the bunker door had no blocker.
Praise, keep: smaller guns like the one at 21:30, the new village buildings, caves, fallen logs, the cannon, German gun names, grip-left now works, walking through double doors, snow and changing weather.

 (a) Intent (Q2 c, ready to sell): no setting on the Quest that cannot do anything there; nothing that makes a paying player wonder whether the game thinks he has a mouse.
 (b) The Keyboard & Mouse page is gone from the Quest's options; its rows that matter move: Auto-Jump onto the controls page, Reset Tutorial Hints onto Accessibility (on every platform).
 (c) The Controller page is called Touch Controls on the Quest and lists only what the Touch controllers use: the controller row, Vibration, Flight Stick and Auto-Jump; stick sensitivities, curves, dead zones, aim assist, stick layout, sneak and sprint styles and button prompt styles (a gamepad's) are hidden there.
 (d) Page cycling and the "Page X of N - next page" line count only the pages shown; Options on the Quest opens on Video, not a controller page.
 (e) Every moved row keeps its value, its help line and its place in Reset Options; the Mac keeps all its pages (with Auto-Jump also on its Controller page and Reset Tutorial Hints also in Accessibility).

(b) PauseMenu.hostHiddenCats = [Keyboard & Mouse] on the Quest; Auto-Jump now also sits on the Controller / Touch Controls page and Reset Tutorial Hints on Accessibility (every platform). (c) on the Quest the Controller page is named Touch Controls and shows the controller row, Vibration, Flight Stick and Auto-Jump; the look sensitivities, acceleration, curve, invert, dead zones, max input thresholds, aim assist, stick layout, sneak and auto-sprint styles and button prompt style are hidden (hostHidden). Q2c-console-3. [DONE] The headset's loading scene says what it is doing and teaches while he waits (it showed a bar and spinning blocks, no words, for up to a minute on first launch).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, first hour and onboarding): a wait that explains itself and is worth something.
 (b) Under the bar: what is happening ("Painting textures", "Opening the world", "Generating terrain", "Saving the world") and how far along, in percent.
 (c) Below that a tip that changes every eight seconds: how to open the journal, call the horse, track a target, the comfort options, that the world saves every minute, the Director after the story; short, original wording.

(b) under the bar the loader's own stage and percent ("Generating terrain  55%", "Saving the world  2%", "Painting textures", "Opening the world", "Joining <host>"). (c) ten tips in turn, eight seconds each (journal from the chest pocket, Y calls the horse, Track a Target, VR Comfort & Controls, the minute autosave and Saved badge, grips and the weapon wheel, the hip magazine, sneak and sprint clicks, the Director, crouching through gaps). Q2c-console-4. [DONE] Dying is clear: where his things are and how to get them back (the first death in a paid game should never feel like losing everything).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, first hour, nothing confusing): a death explains itself and points the way back.
 (b) The death screen says it in words he can read: "You were slain by Hisser" (not "Player was slain by Hisser"), where his things are ("in a chest where you fell, at 120, -340, on your map", or "on the ground where you fell: about five minutes before they are gone"), and that he keeps his experience; long lines wrap inside the panel.
 (c) After Respawn the chest is tracked at once (the HUD bearing and the gold beacon) unless he is already following something, with a toast giving the distance and the direction.
 (d) Getting them back closes the loop: when the chest is emptied or broken the "Your things" pin goes from the map, tracking returns to what it was, and a toast says he has his things back.
 (e) The stash is remembered with the world (a quit before going back keeps the pin and the tracking).
 Done Oct 15 (console; Game.die/respawn, BaseSundries DeathStash/deathDetail/stashHint/stashTick, DeathMenu, Renderer death panel, MapCache.unpinDeath, Waymarks.tick): (a) a death now explains itself and leads back. (b) the death message speaks to him ("You were blown up by Powderwight", "You drowned"); under the score, wrapped to the panel (which grows to fit), "Your things are in a chest where you fell, at 120, -340, on your map." or, when no chest spot was found, "Your things are on the ground where you fell: about five minutes before they are gone.", then "You keep your experience." (c) Respawn (bed, world spawn or rebirth anchor) tracks the stash's map pin unless something else is tracked and says "Your things are 412 blocks north: follow the beacon" (or that the chest is beside him). (d) once a second, with the stash's chunk loaded: chests broken or emptied remove the "Your things" pin, put tracking back and say "You have your things back" with a chime. (e) the stash (position, chests, dimension) is saved in the world extras ("deathStash"); the pin was already saved with the map marks. Not seen on the headset.

("Health, hunger and gathering again...") with Cancel first under the cursor; its help line says it asks. Q2c-console-9. [DONE] First-hour survival lessons in the headset (the headset's lessons covered the controls, horses, towns, honour, contracts and guns, but nothing told a new player about crafting, hunger, nightfall or low health; the Mac's first-steps tips are off in VR).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, onboarding): the survival basics are taught in the moment they matter, in VR terms.
 (b) Crafting: the first time he carries logs, how to open the inventory (hold Y) and turn logs into planks and sticks in its grid, and that a crafting table gives the bigger grid.
 (c) Hunger: the first time hunger falls to seven drumsticks, how to eat (food in hand, hold the left trigger) and that a full stomach is what heals him.
 (d) Nightfall: at his first dusk on the surface in survival, that monsters come out in the dark and the three answers (torches, a shelter, a bed: use it to sleep through the night and wake there after a death).
 (e) Low health: the first time he drops to four hearts, to step back, eat and let it come back.
 (f) Each once per player (saved with the other lessons), one at a time, only in survival; the calm ones wait until no monster is chasing him; left-handed players read their own hands.
 Done Oct 15 (console; HudExtras.vrLessons, VRLesson craft/hunger/night/hurt): (a) four survival lessons join the headset's lessons, after the controls lesson. (b) carrying any log: "Crafting: hold Y for the inventory; its small grid turns logs into planks and planks into sticks. Make a crafting table there for the big grid". (c) hunger at 14 (seven drumsticks): how to eat with the left trigger, that a full bar heals and zero hurts. (d) first dusk (day fraction 0.42-0.52) on the surface: monsters at night; torches, walls, or a bed to skip the night and wake there after a death. (e) health at 8 (four hearts): step back, eat, health returns while the hunger bar is nearly full. (f) saved in the same vrLessons bits (once per player), one per tick, survival only; hunger, night and crafting wait while a hostile within 16 blocks is after him; mirrored hands for left-handed play.

Q2c-console-12. [DONE] Messages never vanish before they can be read (the HUD shows one message at a time and every new one replaced the last at once, so two arriving together, a death and its chest, a radio call and a raid horn, a lesson and a pickup, lost the first one unread).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing confusing): every message stays up long enough to read.
 (b) A message stays at least 1.2 s plus 0.03 s a letter (up to 3.5 s) before a newer one takes its place; newer ones wait their turn in order.
 (c) At most four wait; a line already waiting is not queued twice; past four the oldest waiting one gives way (the journal keeps them all).
 (d) Every message still goes into the journal the moment it arrives, in the order it arrived.
 (e) While others wait, the one showing hands over as soon as its reading time is up instead of lingering its full life.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Game.onToast/toastQueue/readFor/toastQueueTick, HudExtras.tick): (a) messages arriving together are shown one after another. (b) a new message waits while the one showing has been up less than readFor (1.2 s + 0.03 s a letter, at most 3.5 s, and never longer than its own life); a line that extends the one showing (the journal's "Added to journal" note) replaces it in place. (c) up to four wait, no duplicates, the oldest waiting one drops past four. (d) logMessage runs on arrival, so the journal's order is the order they came. (e) each frame (HudExtras.tick, menus included) the next waiting one takes over as soon as the one showing has had its reading time.

 (c) A Privacy row in About says plainly what leaves the device: nothing, unless he starts Play Together (his local network) or turns on the Director's Cloud planner (a summary of his world to the address he set); no account, no tracking.
 (d) docs/store/credits-and-licences.md and docs/store/privacy-policy.md say the same (the runner and model rows; the Director section: rules and on-device planning stay on the headset, the cloud planner is off unless chosen, and the headset build only has the built-in mock server).
