Q2c-console-22. [DONE] Take and Store buttons on chests (emptying a chest or putting the stone away meant one laser drag per stack, the slowest chore in a VR inventory).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, comfort in the headset): looting and storing are one press each.
 (b) Every chest screen (chests, barrels, large and trapped chests, supply crates, safes, death chests) has Take and Store buttons at its top right.
 (c) Take moves everything it can into his pack and hotbar; what does not fit stays in the chest.
 (d) Store puts away what the chest already holds: every stack in his pack (not the hotbar) of an item already in the chest goes in, filling partial stacks first; nothing else moves.
 (e) Feedback: the chest's own sound and a short line ("Took 12 stacks", "Stored 5 stacks", "Nothing in your pack matches this chest", "Your pack is full").
 (f) A trapped chest and a town's chests behave as if he had moved the items by hand (the theft rules see them the same way).
 Done Oct 15 (console; Menu.addChestButtons/chestButton, ChestMenu, DoubleChestMenu, Renderer): (a) looting and storing are one press. (b) every ChestMenu (chests, barrels, trapped chests, supply crates, safes, death chests, any container that uses it) and every Large Chest has Store (30 px) and Take (28 px) buttons at the top right. (c) Take adds each chest stack to the pack and hotbar (PlayerInventory.add) and leaves the rest. (d) Store moves every pack stack (slots 9-35) whose item the chest already holds into it, merging into partial stacks first (ItemContainer.add), across both halves of a large chest. (e) a pickup sound or a click and "Took 12 stacks", "Took 4 stacks: your pack is full", "The chest is empty", "Stored 5 stacks" or "Nothing in your pack matches this chest". (f) the buttons only change the container and the inventory, which the town theft check and the trapped chest's circuit read as before.
Q2c-console-23. [DONE] The Director respects the world's difficulty (P266's events ignored it: a Peaceful world, where monsters never come, could still get raiders, outlaw gangs and rifts of fire creatures).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing that breaks the promise a setting makes): Peaceful stays peaceful, Easy stays gentle.
 (b) On Peaceful only peaceful events come: the travelling trader, the messenger's gift, the great storm, the eruption, a wreck with no scavengers, a supply drop with no guards, a legendary beast sighted; raids, bounties, strikes, hostages, outposts, rivals, rifts, warbands, the dead and prowlers never do.
 (c) On Easy the fighting events bring fewer foes (each count kept to the bottom of its range) and the rival is less tough.
 (d) Both planners follow it: the rule director's pool and the model's "allowed" list leave the fighting templates out on Peaceful, and the game's own checks clamp whatever a model asks for.
 (e) The Director page's status says so on Peaceful ("Peaceful: only peaceful events").
 Done Oct 15 (console; Director.peaceful/foeKeys, unfit, accept, enqueue, statusLines): (a) the Director keeps the world's difficulty promise. (b) Director.unfit rules out every template but trader, gift, storm, eruption, wreck, supply drop and legend on Peaceful, and accept sets their scavengers and guards to 0 there. (c) on Easy, posse, guards, troops, counts and scavengers are capped at a quarter of the way up their ranges and the rival's toughness at its lowest plus 0.5. (d) the summary's allowed list (rules and model) follows unfit, and enqueue drops any non-peaceful plan on Peaceful whoever wrote it. (e) the status line in the Director page's help ends "(Peaceful: only peaceful events)".
Q2c-console-24. [DONE] The Director picks its moments (P266 could start an event while he slept in a bed, while the credits or the victory epilogue rolled, or the second a world finished loading or he respawned).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing jarring): an event never interrupts a moment that is not play.
 (b) No new event while he is asleep, while the credits or the epilogue roll, in the first minute after a world loads, or in the first minute after a respawn.
 (c) The Director's first radio call ("the land is stirring") waits until the credits and the epilogue are over and he is back in play, with no menu open.
 (d) The page's status names the wait ("next waits: you are asleep", "the credits are rolling", "you have just arrived", "you have just come back").
 Done Oct 15 (console; Director.unsafe/arrivedAt/diedAt, second): (a) events start only in play. (b) Director.unsafe now also waits while g.sleeping, while g.credits is set (the credits and the Ashguard epilogue), for 60 s after the Director first ticks in a loaded world, and for 60 s after the last moment he was dead. (c) the wake call needs no credits, no menu, not paused and alive. (d) the wait reasons go into the status line ("next waits: you are asleep" and the others).
Q2c-console-25. [DONE] Developer wording and test worlds out of a store player's way (the Worlds page offered "Sparkstone Workshop (circuits)", a creative test world he never made; Interface called the coordinates readout "Debug Info"; the pause menu said "Play Together (network)").
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing that looks unfinished): every row reads like a player's option.
 (b) The Sparkstone Workshop row shows only with Cheats & Shortcuts on (it builds a creative test world).
 (c) "Debug Info" becomes "Coordinates & Details" (position, biome, frame rate), its help line saying so.
 (d) "Play Together (network)..." becomes "Play Together...", its help line already saying it is over Wi-Fi.
 (e) Nothing else changes: the same ids, values and keyboard keys (F3 still toggles it on the Mac).
 Done Oct 15 (console; PauseMenu worlds, interface and main pages): (a) the rows read as player options. (b) the Worlds page lists Sparkstone Workshop only while Cheats & Shortcuts is on (On in playtest and Mac builds, Off in the store APK). (c) Interface shows "Coordinates & Details: Off" with the help "Shows your position, the biome, the frame rate and chunk details in the corner (F3 on the keyboard)." (d) the main pause row reads "Play Together...". (e) ids, values and the F3 key are unchanged.
Q2c-console-26. [DONE] No dead settings left in the headset's options (after Q2c-console-1: Video's Brightness changed a value the headset never reads, its own Brightness living on the VR Comfort page; the crosshair styles, Couch Mode (TV), Safe Area and the split-screen layout did nothing useful in VR).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, clear settings): every row on the headset's options pages acts in the headset.
 (b) Video's Brightness on the headset is the headset's own (the same setting as on VR Comfort & Controls), so changing it in either place changes the view.
 (c) The crosshair style rows are hidden on the headset (the laser replaces the crosshair there).
 (d) The TV rows are hidden on the headset: Couch Mode (TV), Safe Area and Split Screen layout (the HUD size stays under GUI Scale).
 (e) The Mac keeps all of them as they were.
 Done Oct 15 (console; PauseMenu.hostVideoRows and the q_ action route, QuestOptions.install): (a) the headset's options pages hold only working rows. (b) on the headset the Video page's Brightness row is the host's q_bright (QuestSettings.brightness, which the renderer reads), stepping and labelled the same as on VR Comfort & Controls; the shared Brightness (Settings.brightness, read only by the Mac) is hidden there. (c) both Crosshair rows hidden in the headset. (d) Couch Mode (TV), Safe Area and Split Screen layout hidden in the headset (GUI Scale stays). (e) the Mac's pages are unchanged.
Q2c-console-27. [DONE] The last dead rows in the headset's options (Audio offered a Mac music folder the headset doesn't have, Accessibility a Narrator with no system voice behind it, and Tutorial Hints did nothing to the headset's lessons).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, clear settings): the headset's Audio and Accessibility pages only show what works there.
 (b) Audio: Soundtrack, My Music, Shuffle and My Music Volume are hidden on the headset (there is no own-music folder there; Skip Track and Show Song Titles stay).
 (c) Accessibility: Narrator is hidden on the headset (no system voice).
 (d) Tutorial Hints now switches the headset's lessons on and off as well as the Mac's tips, so the row does what it says everywhere.
 (e) Its help line says it covers the lessons too.
 Done Oct 15 (console; QuestOptions hostHidden, ContextPrompts.lessonDone, PauseMenu help): (a) the headset's Audio and Accessibility pages are all live rows now. (b) Soundtrack, My Music, Shuffle and My Music Volume hidden on the headset (QuestSound.custom is nil there); Skip Track and Show Song Titles stay. (c) Narrator hidden on the headset (it speaks through AVFoundation, which the headset build has not). (d) with Tutorial Hints Off every headset lesson counts as done (lessonDone), so none show, while status prompts (an empty gun, the helm and turret controls, the gun lesson) still do; back On, the lessons not yet seen return. (e) help: "First-steps tips while you play, and the headset's lessons (crafting, hunger, night, riding...)."
Q2c-console-28. [DONE] The journal's Events shelf stays current (a Director event's entry was written when it began and when it ended: its "time left" went stale, a moving gang's or rival's place was where it started, and nothing said how much was left to do).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing misleading): the shelf tells the truth whenever he opens it.
 (b) Every minute of play each running event's entry is rewritten: time left, where it is now (a gang on its route, a rival or a messenger on the move), and how far along it is.
 (c) Progress where it can be counted: "3 of 5 outlaws left", "2 kidnappers left, then free the hostage", "the chest still holds most of its load", "waiting for nightfall at Dunmore".
 (d) An entry keeps the day and hour it began (refreshing no longer moves it in the list or changes its date).
 (e) A finished entry leads with its outcome, then the story of it.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Director.file/lastFiled, DirectorEvents.progress): (a) the Events shelf is rewritten while events run. (b) once a minute of play every running event's entry is refiled: time left, the marker's current place (gangs, rivals, messengers and peddlers move it), and a "Now:" line. (c) progress per template: the outlaw and his gang ("3 of 5 outlaws left"), kidnappers then the hostage, the chest's load, soldiers, creatures or prowlers left, waiting for nightfall, the strike's countdown, the messenger or rival on the way, the storm, the legend, the eruption. (d) refiling no longer touches an entry's day and hour. (e) finished entries start with the outcome line ("Won: $120, honour", "Failed: ...").
Q2c-console-29. [DONE] New journal entries stand out (documents, orders, posters and Director events pile into the journal, but nothing said there was something new to read; in his notes: "where's the journal?").
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, onboarding): he knows when there is something new to read and finds it at once.
 (b) The pause menu's row reads "Journal (3 new)" when documents have arrived since he last opened it.
 (c) In the journal's list each unread document is marked "New" in its second line, and opening the journal lands on the newest one.
 (d) Opening the journal (the pause row or the chest pocket in VR) counts everything as read; messages never count as new, only documents.
 (e) A world just loaded starts with nothing marked new (no stale badge from an old session).
 Done Oct 15 (console; Game.journalSeen/journalNewDocs, Game.openJournal, JournalMenu.markNew, PauseMenu): (a) new reading is flagged. (b) the pause row reads "Journal (3 new)" while documents (entries with pages: orders, logs, notes, pamphlets, posters, letters, Director events) have been added since the last visit. (c) each such entry's second line starts "New -" in the list, which still opens on the newest. (d) every openJournal (the pause row, the chest pocket, the Director page's link) marks all as read; messages never count. (e) loading a world sets the seen mark to the journal's length.
Q2c-console-30. [DONE] The World Map says what a marker is when he points at it (unnamed markers were a bare letter on a square: K, L, P, R, D, h, s, p, # meant nothing to a new player, and the key line only covered B, G, V and patrols).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, clear and readable): every marker on the map can be read.
 (b) Pointing at a marker (the laser, the mouse) shows a small card by it: what it is ("Sky isle", "Legendary animal's den", "Railway station"), its name when it has one, and how far it is from him.
 (c) The card also works for his home marker ("Home: your bed"), the tracked target, Director events and his things.
 (d) The card stays inside the map, never under the pointer's tip, and reads at the headset's panel scale.
 (e) Nothing changes for tapping: a tap still follows the marker or drops a waypoint (Q2c-console-20).
 Done Oct 15 (console; MapMenu.drawLines): (a) every marker reads under the pointer. (b) on the surface the map finds the marker nearest the pointer within three cells and draws a dark card by it: the marker's kind (MapCache.style name), its name after a colon when it has one, and "(420 blocks)" from him. (c) his home ("Home: where you wake" or "Spawn: ...") counts too, and so does every kept mark: contracts, bounties, his horse and where it fell, ships, vehicles, Director events, waypoints and "Your things". (d) the card sits up and right of the pointer, flips left at the map's right edge and below at its top, at the HUD scale (the headset panel's too). (e) tapping is unchanged.
Q2c-console-31. [DONE] The first-launch setup lets him choose his first world's difficulty (on the headset the first world is made before the setup page opens, so it was always Normal, then locked for good).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, first hour): a new player picks how hard his first world is before he plays it.
 (b) The setup page shows "First World: Normal" while that world is brand new; pointing and pulling the trigger steps Peaceful, Easy, Normal, Hard.
 (c) Only while the world is new (its first in-game day and his first ten minutes in it): after that the row is gone and the difficulty is fixed as before; reopening the setup later never shows it.
 (d) The choice takes effect at once (monster rules, hunger) and is saved with the world.
 (e) Its help line says what the levels mean and that it is fixed once he plays on; the page's subtitle mentions the choice while the row is there.
 Done Oct 15 (console; QuestOptions.setupPage/newWorld/act, Hooks.game, QuestApp): (a) the first world's difficulty is his choice. (b) the setup page gains "First World: Normal" above Start Playing, stepping Peaceful, Easy, Normal, Hard (a host value row, so the stick steps it too). (c) shown and changeable only while the open world is on its first in-game day and the session is under ten minutes old (QuestOptions.newWorld); afterwards the row is gone and the difficulty stays locked. (d) it sets Game.difficulty, which the monster and hunger rules read live and the world extras save. (e) help: "Peaceful: no monsters. Easy: gentler monsters and hunger. Normal: as intended. Hard: tougher in every way. Fixed once you play on."; subtitle "Set up how you play and how hard your first world is...". Not seen on the headset.

 (a) Intent (Q2 c, first impression): the title screen is clear about what happens next and lets a new VR player get comfortable first.
 (b) The first row reads "Continue: World1 (Survival, day 27)" for a world already played, "Play: World1" for a new one.
 (c) On the headset, VR Comfort & Controls sits on the title screen too.
 (d) The title's subtitle (when there is no "saved" note) reads the world's difficulty and terrain ("Normal, polygon terrain").
 (e) Nothing else moves: Worlds, Options and Quit as before.

Q2c-console-33. [DONE] Silent no-ops explain themselves (mining iron ore with a wooden pickaxe broke it and dropped nothing, and holding food with a full hunger bar did nothing, both without a word: the two most common "is it broken?" moments of a first hour).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing broken-looking): when an action gives nothing, the game says why.
 (b) Breaking a block with the wrong or too weak a tool, so that it drops nothing: "Iron Ore needs a Stone Pickaxe or better to drop anything" (the tool named as the game names it), once per kind of block per session.
 (c) Trying to eat with a full hunger bar: "You're full", at most every eight seconds, and not when the food would be planted or placed where he points.
 (d) Survival only; creative never shows them.
 (e) Wording reads right: "an Iron Pickaxe", shears named without "or better", the block named as its item (Deepslate Iron Ore), and every state of a block (lit and unlit redstone ore) counts as one kind.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/Game.swift): (a) both silent no-ops now toast a reason; (b) Game.harvestHint, called from breakBlock when drop && survival && !Mining.canHarvest, names the tier and tool from Blocks.tool/harvestLevel via the item registry, remembered per groupBase in Game.harvestTold; (c) fullToldAt gates "You're full" to once per 8 s, skipped for drinks, always-edible foods and when the food would be planted or placed at the target; (d) both checks require survival; (e) a/an chosen by the first letter, shears without "or better", block name from Items.item(forBlock:), groupBase key so states share one hint.
Q2c-console-34. [DONE] Near death is felt, not just shown (in the headset the hearts sit low on the HUD, below where he looks in a fight; nothing else changed at two hearts, so the first warning of being nearly dead was dying. The gamepad already had a heartbeat rumble; the Touch controllers, the view and the sound had nothing).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing confusing in the first hour): he knows he is about to die while he can still back off and eat.
 (b) At 4 health or less (two hearts) in survival a lub-dub heartbeat beats in both Touch controllers about once a second, quicker (every 0.75 s) at one heart or less; the gamepad's heartbeat rumble keeps the same beat.
 (c) The edges of the view pulse a faint dark red with each beat (a soft ring like the hurt glow, weaker with Screen Effects off) in the headset; the Mac screen gets the same edge pulse, never a full-screen flash.
 (d) A quiet heartbeat thump sounds with each beat (not positional; the subtitle reads "Heart beats").
 (e) The hearts on the HUD jiggle while health is that low, as in the reference game, so a glance at them shows it too.
 (f) Never in creative, while dead, paused, in a menu, asleep or during the credits; it stops the moment health climbs above two hearts, and the existing Low health lesson still comes first.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/Controller.swift, Renderer.swift, quest/src/app/QuestControls.swift, quest/tools/extract_hud.py): (a) a heartbeat you feel, see and hear from two hearts down; (b) Feedback.tick times beats (1.1 s, 0.75 s at one heart) and counts them in heartBeats; QuestControls plays a lub (0.45) then a dub 0.2 s later (0.3) in both Touch controllers; the gamepad rumble rides the same beat; (c) Feedback.heartPulse (1 on a beat, fading) draws a dark red edge ring in QuestControls.screenEffects and a gradient border on the Mac HUD (off on the headset's HUD panel via extract_hud.py), both scaled by Screen Effects; (d) g.sfx(.wardenHeartbeat, 0.3) unpositioned with each beat (subtitle "Heart beats"); (e) Renderer's hearts hop a pixel up or down, hashed 20 times a second per heart, at 4 health or less; (f) guard on survival, alive, no menu, not paused, not asleep, no credits; the pulse is cleared at once when any fails or health is above 4; the Low health lesson (8 health) still comes first.
Q2c-console-35. [DONE] Vibration means every buzz in the headset (Options' Vibration row only reached the few buzzes routed through the gamepad layer: the hurt thump, mining, sword hits, the bow string, the heartbeat and the menus' ticks went straight to the Touch controllers, so Vibration Off still buzzed; and the row was only under Touch Controls and Accessibility, not on VR Comfort & Controls).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, comfort options that do what they say): a player who finds the buzzing tiring or worn controllers rattly turns it down once and it is down everywhere.
 (b) Every Touch controller buzz scales with Vibration: Off silences all of them, 35% is gentle, 70% (the default) feels exactly as tuned, 100% is firmer (capped at full strength).
 (c) Stepping the row buzzes both hands once at the new strength (nothing at Off), so he feels what he chose, from whichever page he changed it on.
 (d) VR Comfort & Controls gets a "Controller Vibration" row, the same value as Touch Controls and Accessibility, stepping left and right like the other comfort values.
 (e) The help line says what it covers (hits, mining, attacks, the bow, low health, explosions, menus) and that Off stops them all.
 (f) Reset Options puts it back to 70%, and Dominant Hand Left keeps every buzz in the right hand (the scale applies after the hands are swapped).
 Done Oct 15 (console; quest/src/app/QuestHands.swift, QuestOptions.swift, Sources/PauseMenu.swift): (a) one Vibration value now governs the headset's haptics; (b) HandedInput.haptic, the one path every Touch buzz takes, multiplies the amplitude by rumble / 0.7 (capped at 1, nothing below 0.01), so Off is silent and the default unchanged; the gamepad-layer buzzes were already scaled and go straight to the runtime, so nothing is scaled twice; (c) every Vibration row (q_vib and the shared rumble row) calls PadManager.shared.rumble(0.8, 0.15), which on the headset is QuestPad's buzz at the new strength; (d) "Controller Vibration: 70%" row on VR Comfort & Controls (q_vib in hostValues, steps 0/35/70/100% both ways); (e) q_vib help and the shared rumble help list hits, mining, attacks, the bow, low health, explosions and menus, and say Off stops it all; (f) Settings.resetAll already sets rumble = 0.7 (Reset Options runs it before QuestSettings.reset); the scale is applied after physical(hand), so handedness is untouched.
Q2c-console-36. [DONE] Worn-out gear warns before it breaks and says when it does (a tool broke with a glass-block shatter and a toast, no warning first; armour pieces and Glider Wings wore out with no word and armour simply vanished from his body; a shield broke with a sound and no message: losing hard-won iron without warning is the classic first-hour sting).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing confusing or broken-looking): he always knows a tool or a piece of armour is about to go, in time to repair or replace it, and never wonders where his helmet went.
 (b) Held gear (tools, weapons, fishing rods, shears, flint and steel) warns once as it crosses its last tenth of durability (at least 3 uses): "Your Iron Pickaxe is nearly worn out (25 uses left)"; repaired gear warns again the next time it gets there.
 (c) Armour (melee hits, gunfire and thorns) and Glider Wings warn the same way ("Your Iron Boots are nearly worn out"), and an armour piece that breaks says so ("Your Iron Helmet broke") with the break sound; a shield says "Your Shield broke".
 (d) Anything breaking plays the item-break snap (not a glass block's shatter) and buzzes the controllers (Touch and gamepad, scaled by Vibration).
 (e) A nearly worn-out item's durability bar pulses red on the hotbar and in the inventory, so a glance shows which tool is about to go.
 (f) Anvil-renamed gear is called by its name; survival only (creative gear never wears, so it never warns).
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/Game.swift, GameCombat.swift, SixIron.swift, Controller.swift, Renderer.swift): (a) every wear step on his gear now goes through Game.wearNote; (b) damageHeld calls wearNote(was, now): a toast with the uses left when the stack crosses max(3, durability / 10) left, so a repaired tool warns again; (c) melee armour wear, thorns wear and SixIron gunfire wear call wearNote(uses: false), which also says "Your X broke" with the break sound when a piece goes; Glider Wings warn per second of flight; the shield calls it with sound: false (its own shieldBreak plays); (d) .itemBreak replaces the glass-block shatter, and Feedback.sound buzzes the controllers on .itemBreak and .shieldBreak (QuestPad on the headset, so Vibration scales it); (e) Renderer.itemIcon pulses the empty part of the durability bar red at the same threshold, on the hotbar and in every inventory grid; (f) the name is the stack's anvil label when it has one; wearNote guards on survival, and is/are follows a plural name (Boots, Leggings, Shears, Glider Wings).
Q2c-console-37. [DONE] The game goes quiet when he steps away and sounds paused when paused (opening the Meta menu or lifting the headset left rain, monsters and music playing over the system menu, because the mixer kept rendering while the world stopped ticking; and the pause menu sounded exactly like play).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, ready to sell): a store game falls silent when you leave it and is heard to be paused, the way every Quest title behaves.
 (b) Losing input focus (the system menu, the headset taken off, a system dialog) fades every game sound out in about a third of a second: music, jukebox discs, ambience, loops and interface sounds.
 (c) Coming back fades them in over about half a second (no blast of rain at full volume); the music carries on from where it was.
 (d) While the game is paused (the pause menu, or "Headset tracking lost: paused") the world and its music drop to about a third, so the pause is heard as well as seen; the menu's own clicks stay at full volume and resuming brings the world back up smoothly.
 (e) The title screen is not ducked: its music plays at the full Music volume although the world behind it is paused.
 (f) On Options > Audio the duck lifts, so the Master, Music and other sliders are heard at the level he is actually setting.
 Done Oct 15 (console; quest/src/common/QuestSound.swift, quest/src/app/QuestApp.swift): (a) the headset's mixer now follows focus and pause; (b) SoundEngine.setAway sets awayTarget 0 when XR input focus is lost and renderBlock multiplies the whole mix (world bus with music and discs, and the interface bus) by awayGain, gliding out with a ~0.13 s time constant; (c) back in with ~0.23 s; MusicStream is never stopped, so the piece resumes where its ring left off; (d) duckTarget 0.33 while g.paused scales only the world bus and music (scratch), the interface bus mixed in after it at full level, glide ~0.17 s; (e) no duck while the pause menu cameFromTitle; (f) no duck while PauseMenu shows page .options with cat .audio.
Q2c-console-38. [DONE] The pack's crafting book shows what needs a Crafting Table (the inventory's 2x2 book silently left out every recipe that needs the 3x3 grid: with planks and sticks in his pack the pickaxe was simply not there, and searching "pickaxe" said no recipe matches: the first hour's "why can't I make a pickaxe?").
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, first-hour clarity): the pack's book never hides a recipe; it says where to make it.
 (b) The 2x2 book lists the table recipes too, in their tabs, in search results and in Craftable Now when he has the items, each tile marked with a small Crafting Table badge; the ones he can make right here come first.
 (c) A table recipe whose items he has gets a gold rim (ready, take it to a table); its tile stays dimmed and carries no "x6" count, since it can't be made here.
 (d) Selecting one shows "Needs a Crafting Table" in the detail panel where "You can make N" would be, with its ingredients and what he has of each as usual, so he can gather them.
 (e) Trying to craft one from the pack (click, trigger, Craft buttons) makes nothing and says "Needs a Crafting Table" with the soft refusal click.
 (f) While table recipes are ready and he carries no Crafting Table, the Crafting Table's tile pulses gold so the next step is obvious; the table's own 3x3 book is unchanged.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/CraftingBook.swift): (a) nothing is hidden from the pack's book any more; (b) CraftingBookMenu.catalogue(2) appends, per category, every catalogue(3) result the 2x2 grid can't make; refresh puts fitsHere recipes first; tabs, Craftable Now and search all draw from it; a 11-px Crafting Table badge sits in the tile's top-right corner; (c) table recipes are drawn not-ok (dimmed, no x-count tag) with a gold rim when RecipeBook.craftable says the items are all there; (d) the detail panel shows "Needs a / Crafting Table" in amber in place of You can make / Missing items, the ingredient grid and counts unchanged; (e) craftTile refuses !fitsHere with lastCraftMessage "Needs a Crafting Table" and the soft click (tiles, trigger, Craft 1 / stack / max / amount), and padHold skips them; (f) refresh sets tableReady when any table recipe is craftable; then the Crafting Table tile pulses a gold frame while the pack holds none; catalogue(3) (the table's book) is built exactly as before.
Q2c-console-39. [DONE] A block that can't go where he aims says why (pressing place while standing in the cell, a torch aimed at a ceiling or a glass wall, a sapling on stone, a ladder on a floor, a door without room, a block at the top of the world: each did nothing at all, the Oct 12 "I'm not able to place blocks" kind of moment).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing broken-looking): a refused placement is never silent; he learns the rule on the spot.
 (b) A solid block aimed into his own space while he stands on the ground says "You're standing there"; into a creature's, "Something is in the way" (no message mid-jump, where pillar timing misses are normal).
 (c) Support rules are named: "Oak Sapling needs grass, dirt or farmland under it", "Rails need a solid block under them", "Torches need a solid block below or beside them", "Torches can't hang from a ceiling", "Torches need a solid wall", "Ladders go on the side of a block", "Signs go on top or on the side of a block".
 (d) A door without room says "A door needs two free blocks with ground under them" (or "You're standing there"); a block above the build limit says "That's as high as you can build".
 (e) Feedback: the soft refusal click and a light tick in the hands (Touch or gamepad, scaled by Vibration) with the message.
 (f) Only on a fresh press, each reason at most every four seconds, so holding the trigger to place in a row never repeats it; creative and survival alike (these are placement rules, not survival ones).
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/Game.swift): (a) Game.placeRefused(why) is called wherever block placement used to return in silence; (b) the final placement test's new else branch: own cell while onGround -> "You're standing there", a mob's -> "Something is in the way", nothing while airborne; (c) unsupported plants name the held item, rails and torches their rule; torch ceiling / non-solid wall, ladder on a floor or ceiling and sign under a block refuse in their shape cases; (d) the door's guard tells standing-there from no-room, and the replaceable/height guard says "That's as high as you can build" at y >= CH; (e) placeRefused plays .click at 0.25 and PadManager.shared.rumble(0.15, 0.03) (QuestPad on the headset, scaled by Vibration) with the toast; (f) every call is behind useNow, and refusedAt keeps each reason to once per 4 s; no survival check.
Q2c-console-40. [DONE] The furnace says what it is doing and why it is stuck (it showed only a flame and an arrow: with no fuel, a full output slot, or an item it can't smelt it simply sat there, and a non-fuel item dropped on the fuel slot just bounced back: the first hour's "why isn't it cooking?").
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, first-hour clarity): one glance at a furnace, smoker or blast furnace tells him what it is doing, how long it will take, and what to do when it is stuck.
 (b) A status line between the slots and the pack: "Smelting Raw Iron: 7 left" with "About 70 s" under it while it works, counting down live.
 (c) When stuck it says why in amber: "Add fuel below: coal, charcoal, logs or planks"; "Take out the Iron Ingot to keep going"; "Rotten Flesh can't be smelted"; and while running short, "Fuel for 3 more: add more to finish".
 (d) An empty furnace says what goes where ("Put something to smelt in the top slot, fuel below"); one burning with nothing in it says it is burning its fuel.
 (e) Dropping something that isn't fuel on the fuel slot says "Stone isn't fuel" on the status line for a few seconds with the soft refusal click, instead of a silent bounce.
 (f) Smokers and blast furnaces use their own words and speed ("Cooking", twice as fast), and an item that belongs in another kind says where ("Raw Iron needs a Furnace or Blast Furnace" in a smoker).
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/Menu.swift, Renderer.swift): (a) FurnaceMenu.status works out the state every frame from its block entity; (b) the panel is 186 tall with the pack at y 104 and its label at 93, and Renderer draws up to two status lines at y 74 and 83 (wrapped to 160 px; one line each when there are two) under the flame and arrow; the time left counts cook progress at 20 or 40 units a second; (c) stuck states return amber lines: no fuel and nothing burning, an output slot holding something else or full, no smelting recipe, and fuel (burn + fuel slot + progress) for fewer items than are waiting; (d) empty and burning-empty messages; (e) FurnaceMenu.click refuses a carried non-fuel on the .fuel slot with note "X isn't fuel" for 3 s and the 0.25 click; (f) be.mob picks Cooking/cook for a smoker and the doubled rate for both fast kinds; a recipe not allowed in this kind lists the kinds BlockEntity.allowed accepts.
Q2c-console-41. [DONE] Headset lessons for the rest of the first hour (in the headset no control hints are drawn in play, so the one-time lessons are the only teacher: the crafting lesson still described a grid in the inventory that is gone, and boats, minecarts, other mounts, swimming and the hold-B drop had no lesson at all).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, onboarding): every first-hour moment that needs a button he hasn't used yet gets one short line, the first time it matters.
 (b) The crafting lesson matches the crafting book: hold Y for the inventory and point at Craft; logs make planks, planks make sticks and a Crafting Table; recipes with a table badge need one placed.
 (c) The first time in a boat: push the left stick forward to row, sideways to steer; B gets out.
 (d) The first time in a minecart: push the left stick forward to roll the way you look along the rails; B gets out.
 (e) The first ride on any other animal (donkey, mule, llama, camel, pig, strider): the left stick steers (a pig or strider follows the bait on its rod); B gets off. The horse keeps its own lesson.
 (f) The first dive in survival: hold A to swim up; the bubbles above the hunger bar are his air, and when they run out he starts to drown.
 (g) The first item dropped by holding B: holding B drops what is in his hand; walk over it to pick it back up (an accidental drop is understood at once).
 (h) Each once ever, mirrored for a left-handed player, brought back by Reset Tutorial Hints and silenced by Tutorial Hints Off, like the existing lessons.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/HudExtras.swift, Game.swift): (a) five new VRLesson bits (boat, cart, mount, water, drop) in ContextPrompts.vrLessons beside the old ones; (b) the craft lesson text now names the Craft button, planks, sticks, the Crafting Table and the table badge (Q2c-console-38); (c) g.riding.kind == .boat; (d) .minecart (riderPush along the look, as Rails.updateMinecart drives it); (e) lessonMounts (donkey, mule, llama, trader llama, camel, pig, strider, skeleton and zombie horses), the horse lesson unchanged; (f) survival and player.headInWater, taught straight after the low-health lesson, before the calm ones; (g) Game.dropHeld stamps heldDroppedAt and the drop lesson follows within 3 s; (h) all go through teach (once ever in UserDefaults vrLessons, mirrorHands for a left hand), resetLessons clears every bit and lessonDone is true with Tutorial Hints Off.
Q2c-console-42. [DONE] Controller trouble is explained (a controller that stops being tracked, out of the cameras' view, put down or with a flat battery, made the game quietly ignore that hand after half a second: its laser and held item vanished and its stick and buttons went dead, with no word; with both put down the world kept running).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing broken-looking): when a hand stops working he is told why and what to do.
 (b) A controller lost for more than two seconds in play is named: "Right controller lost: bring it into view of the headset, or check its battery", once per loss.
 (c) Its return is said ("Right controller is back") and felt (a short buzz in that controller).
 (d) Both controllers put down for three seconds in play: the game pauses ("Controllers put down: paused") so nothing happens to him while he is away from them.
 (e) After half a minute on one controller the way to play with one is named: the moving hand lost -> Controls: One Hand (the dominant stick also walks); the aiming hand lost -> Dominant Hand: Left/Right (the other controller aims). Once a session.
 (f) Names follow the physical controller (Dominant Hand Left swaps the logical hands); no pause while a menu, the pause screen or the credits are up.
 Done Oct 15 (console; quest/src/app/QuestControls.swift): (a) QuestControls.controllerWatch runs every frame right after HandedInput.refresh; (b) per logical hand it times HandedInput.lost and toasts the physical controller's name and the fix after 2 s, once per loss (not while both are gone); (c) on return it toasts "is back" and buzzes that hand (0.35, 80 ms) through HandedInput, so Vibration scales it; (d) both lost for 3 s with no menu, no pause, no credits and alive sets game.paused (which opens the pause menu) and toasts "Controllers put down: paused"; (e) at 30 s on one controller, once a session: the move hand lost suggests One Hand (skipped when it is already on), the aim hand lost suggests the other Dominant Hand; (f) the name comes from the logical hand through QuestSettings.leftHanded; the pause test skips menus, pause and credits.
Q2c-console-43. [DONE] Lessons can be read again (each headset lesson shows once as a passing message; one missed in a fight or forgotten an hour later could only come back by resetting every tip and lesson from the start).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, onboarding): what the game has taught him stays at hand.
 (b) "Lessons..." on VR Comfort & Controls (beside Touch Controls) opens a page listing every lesson he has been shown, by name, in first-hour order (the basics, crafting, eating, the first night, low health, swimming, dropping things, boats, minecarts, horses, other mounts, towns, contracts, honour, guns).
 (c) Pointing at one shows its full text in the help line, wrapped, with the buttons mirrored for a left-handed player.
 (d) Lessons not met yet are only counted ("4 more come up as you play"), never spoiled.
 (e) Reset Tutorial Hints sits on the page too, doing exactly what the Accessibility row does (one shared reset).
 (f) With Tutorial Hints off the page says so and where to turn them back on.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/HudExtras.swift, PauseMenu.swift, quest/src/app/QuestOptions.swift): (a) the lesson texts moved into one table, ContextPrompts.lessonInfo (teach(g, lesson) reads it), so play and the page share them; (b) VR Comfort & Controls has "Lessons..." (host:lessons) under Touch Controls; QuestOptions.lessonsPage lists the seen lessons (lessonSeen reads the saved bits whatever the setting) in lessonInfo's first-hour order, plus Guns when Settings.gunLesson is set (gunLessonText); (c) each row's id lesson:N gets its text as hostHelp, run through mirrorHands for a left hand, shown by the pause menu's 3-line help; (d) unseen ones are counted in a "N more come up as you play" row; (e) PauseMenu.resetHints (the Accessibility row's body, moved) is called by both Reset Tutorial Hints rows; (f) with tutorialHints off the page shows only the note and Back.
Q2c-console-44. [DONE] The pause screen says why the game paused itself (the headset pauses on its own when he opens the Meta menu or lifts the headset, when tracking is lost, and now when the controllers are put down; he came back to a plain "Game Paused" with the toast that explained it faded out behind the panel).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing confusing): coming back to a paused game, he sees at once why it stopped and that nothing was lost.
 (b) The pause screen's subtitle names the cause: "Paused while you were away", "Paused: the headset lost tracking (light the room or wipe its cameras)", "Paused: controllers put down".
 (c) Away a minute or more, it says for how long ("Paused while you were away (12 min)"), and once the away-save has run, "your world is saved".
 (d) A soft chime plays as he comes back to the paused game, so he hears it is waiting for him.
 (e) The reason stays until he resumes; a pause he opens himself keeps the usual subtitle (world, day, difficulty, saved ago), and a disconnected gamepad's notice (Mac) still comes first.
 (f) The headset going to sleep (the session stopping) counts as away too: the same reason, the time away measured on the wall clock across the sleep, and the save.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/PauseMenu.swift, Game.swift, quest/src/app/QuestApp.swift, QuestControls.swift): (a) PauseMenu.autoReason, set by the host when it pauses by itself; (b) the main page's subtitle shows it; QuestApp.awayPause sets "Paused while you were away" (focus lost or session stopped), the tracking-loss pause and QuestControls' controllers-down pause set theirs, each rebuilding the open pause menu; (c) on focus regained the reason is rewritten with the minutes away (from awayStart, the wall clock) and "your world is saved" once savedAway is set; (d) .click at 0.35 plays as he comes back; (e) Game.paused's didSet clears the reason on resume; a self-opened pause never sets one; the Mac's pad-disconnected subtitle is checked first; (f) the session-stopped branch calls awayPause too and the wall-clock start spans the sleep.

Q2c-console-46. [DONE] Headset lessons for the first-hour tools (only guns and the bow taught their use in the headset; a fishing rod's fight, the shield, farming and the bucket each hide a button or a rule nobody guesses, and lava from a bucket is a classic first-hour death).

 (c) A shield: hold the left trigger to raise it; it stops hits from the front, and an axe can knock it down for a moment.
 (d) A hoe or seeds: the left trigger with a hoe turns grass or dirt into farmland; seeds go on farmland; crops need light and water within four blocks.
 (e) A bucket: the left trigger scoops water or lava and pours it again; poured lava burns everything near it, him included.

Q2c-console-49. [DONE] Menu and HUD size in the headset (the menu panel and the floating HUD had one fixed size each: text that is comfortable for one pair of eyes, lenses and IPD is small or blurry at the edge of the sweet spot for another, and there was no way to make it bigger).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, comfort and accessibility): he can make the menus and the HUD as big as he needs to read them.

 (d) The laser keeps hitting what it points at: the hit test uses the same scaled panel.
 (e) Reset Options puts both back to Normal; each has a help line saying what it changes.
 (f) Even Extra Large keeps the whole menu inside the view (under 80 degrees wide), so nothing ends up in the lens edges.
 Done Oct 15 (console; quest/src/app/QuestSettings.swift, QuestControls.swift, QuestOptions.swift): (a) two saved options, QuestSettings.menuScale and hudScale; (b) "Menu Size" (q_menusize: 0.85, 1, 1.15, 1.25) on VR Comfort & Controls; QuestControls.menuPanelSize is applied every frame while a menu is up, so the open menu resizes as he steps it; (c) "HUD Size" (q_hudsize: 0.9, 1, 1.15) through hudPanelSize, whose height also places the hotbar at HUD Position's drop; (d) both only change panelSize, which the laser's hit test (menuPointer) and the panel drawing share; (e) both keys are in QuestSettings.snapshot, so Reset Options clears them, and each has a help line; (f) 1.5 m x 1.25 at 1.15 m is about 78 degrees wide.
Q2c-console-50. [DONE] A pickup feed: what he just picked up (in the headset the pack is out of sight: mined blocks, loot and drops vanished into it with only a pop, so he couldn't tell what he got, or whether he got it, without opening the inventory).

 (b) A short feed right of the hotbar: the item's icon and "+4 Oak Log", newest at the bottom, up to three lines (two while a gun is in hand, clear of its ammo count).

Q2c-console-51. [DONE] Low storage is warned before saves fail (a headset whose storage fills up, with videos, other games' downloads, captures, makes the world's saves fail; S2 reports the failure after it happens, when progress may already be lost).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, save safety): he hears about a full headset while there is still room to fix it.
 (b) When a world is being played, free storage where the worlds live is measured at once and every five minutes, off the frame thread; under 300 MB he is told "Storage almost full (120 MB left): free some space on the headset so your world keeps saving", once until it is freed.
 (c) Under 60 MB it says "Storage full: your world may stop saving" every two minutes, with the save badge's red "Save failed" look until there is room.
 (d) The pause screen's subtitle adds "storage low" beside the saved time while it is under 300 MB.
 (e) The Worlds page's subtitle shows the free space while it is low ("120 MB free on this headset").
 (f) Freeing space stops every warning at the next check; a storage that can't be measured never warns.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/HudExtras.swift, Game.swift, PauseMenu.swift): (a) StorageCheck, ticked by a persistent Game each frame; (b) it measures FileManager.attributesOfFileSystem(.systemFreeSize) for the world's save dir on a utility queue when the world starts (a new dir) and every 300 s, behind a lock; under 300 MB one toast with the MB left (warnedLow until it is back over); (c) under 60 MB a toast every 120 s and SaveBadge.storageFullAt, which SaveBadge.lines draws as a red "Storage full" badge for 30 s (a real save failure keeps its own wording); (d) the pause main subtitle appends " - storage low"; (e) the Worlds page's subtitle reads "Storage almost full: N MB free"; (f) at 300 MB or more warnedLow resets and nothing shows; an unmeasurable store leaves freeMB at -1, which never warns. (The lettered (e) said "on this headset": the line is shared with the Mac, so it names no device.)
Q2c-console-52. [DONE] The creature he is fighting shows its health (in the headset a sword swing, an arrow or a shot gave a flash and a sound but no sense of how hurt the creature was, or whether the last hit landed at all; only bosses had a bar).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, clarity and feel in VR combat): he can see each hit land and how close the fight is to over.
 (b) After he hurts a creature, a bar under the boss bars at the top of the HUD shows its name and health, for three seconds after his last hit on it.
 (c) The bar flashes white for a moment at each hit; when the creature dies it empties and fades instead of vanishing.
 (d) Colours say what it is: hostile red, animals green, townsfolk and other neutrals gold.

 (a) Intent (Q2 c, accessibility from the first minute): the first screen he meets can be made readable and suited to him.
 (b) A "Menu Size" row in the setup (Small to Extra Large, Q2c-console-49); the setup panel itself grows or shrinks as he steps it.

 (f) Each new row has the same help line it has elsewhere, and Start Playing stays the last row.
 (g) A pause-menu subtitle too long for one line (the setup's own ran off both edges of the panel) wraps onto two smaller lines, on every page.
 Done Oct 15 (console; quest/src/app/QuestOptions.swift, Sources/PauseMenu.swift, Renderer.swift): (a) three rows added to QuestOptions.setupPage before Start Playing; (b) q_menusize (the open panel follows QuestControls.menuPanelSize every frame); (c) q_subs toggles Settings.subtitles; (d) q_vib with PadManager's sample buzz; (e) both setup subtitles now say a row changes with the trigger and that it can all change later in VR Comfort & Controls (all but the difficulty, when the first world's row is there); (f) q_subs gets the Accessibility wording as its help, q_menusize and q_vib keep theirs, and Start Playing is still last; (g) PauseMenu.layout leaves 38 px over the rows when Font.width(subtitle) > 228 and Renderer wraps such a subtitle with wrapLines onto two lines at 0.85 (0.72 if needed) of the scale at y 14 and 23.
Q2c-console-54. [DONE] Quitting asks first (one stray trigger pull on "Save and Quit Game" in the pause menu, or "Quit Game" on the title screen, closed the app to the headset's home; getting back meant relaunching and waiting for the world to load).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing lost to one stray click): leaving the game is always a deliberate choice.
 (b) Save and Quit Game (pause menu) and Quit Game (title screen) open a "Quit Blocksmith?" page instead of quitting at once.
 (c) It says what will happen: the world is saved first and is waiting on the title screen next time.
 (d) Cancel is the first row and where the cursor starts; B or Back returns to the page he came from.
 (e) The Quit row ignores a pull in the first 0.4 s after the page opens, so a double pull (or the laser resting where the old row was) can't quit.
 (f) Save and Quit to Title still goes straight to the title (it never leaves the game).
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/PauseMenu.swift): (a) PauseMenu.confirmQuit, a third confirm page beside Mode and Reset; (b) the shared "quit" id (the pause menu's Save and Quit Game and the title's Quit Game) now opens .confirm with confirmQuit; (c) title "Quit Blocksmith?", subtitle "Your world is saved first and waits on the title screen next time.", the quit row's help says it saves then closes; (d) rows Cancel (back) then Save and Quit, resetCursor puts the cursor on Cancel, back clears the flag and pops to the page before; (e) quit_yes is ignored until 0.4 s of wall clock (CFAbsoluteTimeGetCurrent, since the game clock stands still while paused) after the page opened; (f) totitle is unchanged.
Q2c-console-55. [DONE] What he points at is named ("I don't know what this is" came up again and again in the voice notes: in the headset there is no tooltip for the world, so a strange block or creature stayed a mystery unless he broke it or it hurt him).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, first-hour clarity): any block or creature can be identified by pointing at it.
 (b) Resting the laser (the crosshair on a screen) on one block or creature for half a second shows its name in a small label at the middle of the view: "Deepslate Iron Ore", "Zombie", a named horse's own name.
 (c) A block his held tool can't harvest adds, in amber, what it needs: "needs a Stone Pickaxe or better" (the same naming as Q2c-console-33).
 (d) The label fades in and out and stays about two and a half seconds; sweeping the aim across the landscape shows nothing (it needs the aim to rest on the same block or creature), so walking around stays quiet.
 (e) Nothing in menus, while dead, or on air and plain water; the text background setting applies to it like other messages.
 (f) Options > Interface "Look-at Names: On/Off" (default On; Reset Options turns it back on).
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/Game.swift, Renderer.swift, Settings.swift, PauseMenu.swift): (a) Game.lookAtLabel, drawn by the HUD; (b) the nearer of mobs.raycast (8 blocks) and the block target is named (customName or the kind's name; Game.blockName from the item registry) after 0.5 s on the same key; Renderer draws it centred 12 px under the middle of the view; (c) Game.harvestNeed (factored out of harvestHint, which now uses it too) adds the tool line in amber when survival, requiresTool and !Mining.canHarvest; (d) 0.15 s fade in, 0.4 s fade out, shown from 0.5 to 3 s; the key is the block's position or the mob itself, so a moving aim keeps resetting it; (e) no label under a menu, while dead, on AIR or WATER or with nothing aimed; its dark band follows Text Background; (f) Settings.lookNames, an Interface row with help, true again on Reset Options.
Q2c-console-56. [DONE] Boats stop rocking the view (a small boat rides the chop everywhere and the swell at sea, more in storms; the rider's view rose and fell with every wave, a seasickness trap in a headset; Steady Saddle smoothed only horses' jumps and bucks).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, comfort): no ride in the game bobs the horizon unless he asks for it.
 (b) With the option on, a boat's bobbing barely moves the horizon: the view follows the hull's height through the same eased filter as the saddle, while a real climb or drop (onto a wave crest, down a waterfall) is still followed.
 (c) Every other mount gets the same steadying: pigs, striders, llamas, camels and minecarts on bumpy rails.
 (d) The option is renamed "Steady Ride" (it was Steady Saddle), with a help line naming horses, boats and other mounts; on by default and set by the Comfort presets as before.
 (e) Only the view is steadied: the boat still visibly rides the waves under him, and the view never strays more than a block and a half from the seat.
 (f) Ships at the helm or on deck keep their own comfort (the deck ring and the eased heave of P261), unchanged.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/ViewStep.swift, Game.swift, quest/src/app/QuestOptions.swift): (a) Game.updateViewStep's steadying now runs for whatever he rides; (b) the m.gaited condition is gone, so a boat's heave (chop and swell) goes through the second-order filter (rate eased at 3/s, position at 2/s) that follows real climbs without lag; (c) pigs, striders, llamas, camels, minecarts and coach seats likewise; (d) the row reads "Steady Ride" (QuestSettings.steadySaddle, the same saved key, so a player's choice carries over), with a help line naming horses, mounts, boats and minecarts; the Comfort presets still switch it; (e) the filter offsets only the view (p.viewDY) and clamps it to 1.5 blocks from the seat; the boat's motion is untouched; (f) ships are not riding (world.ships.aboard/pilot paths), so their deck ring and P261 heave smoothing are unchanged.
Q2c-console-57. [DONE] A world's statistics (nothing recorded what he had done in a world beyond the advancements: how long he had played it, how far he had gone, how much he had mined, built, fought, crafted and eaten; a store player likes to look back at a world's story).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, ready to sell): each world keeps a short, satisfying record of his play.
 (b) Pause > Statistics lists this world's time played, days survived, distance on foot and riding, blocks mined and placed, creatures defeated, deaths, items crafted, food eaten and fish caught.
 (c) Numbers read naturally: "3 h 12 min", "12.4 km", "1,204".
 (d) Saved with the world, each world its own; a world from before this shows what has happened since (and its days from its own clock).
 (e) Counting costs nothing noticeable: a few additions per event, the distance once per game tick.
 (f) The page fits on one screen with a Back row, and its subtitle names the world.
 Done Oct 15 (console; new Sources/Stats.swift; Game.swift, Effects.swift, Fishing.swift, CraftingBook.swift, PauseMenu.swift): (a) Stats, a per-world counter table; (b) the pause menu's "Statistics" row (after Advancements) opens PauseMenu page .stats, seven info rows built by Stats.rows; counters: breakBlock with drop (mined), a successful placement (placed), a player kill in the mob death path (kills), Game.die (deaths), CraftBook.craft's made x result count (crafted), Game.eat (eaten), a landed fish (fish), and per game tick time and horizontal distance on foot or riding (ships count as riding); (c) Stats.count adds thousands commas, distance switches to km with one decimal, time reads min or h + min; (d) encoded into the world extras as "stats" and decoded on every world load (a world without it starts at zero; days come from the world's own time); (e) one dictionary add per event and per tick (survival, persistent worlds only); a jump over 8 blocks in a tick (teleport, respawn) isn't distance; (f) seven rows plus Back, subtitle = the world's name.
Q2c-console-58. [DONE] More silent no-ops explain themselves (seeds pressed onto grass, a boat aimed at nothing it could sit on, a minecart off the rails and bone meal on something that can't grow all did nothing at all: the classic first-hour farming and travel mistakes).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing broken-looking): like Q2c-console-33 and -39, a use that does nothing says why.
 (b) Seeds, carrots or potatoes pressed onto grass or dirt: "Plant it on farmland: till grass or dirt with a hoe first"; nether wart off soul sand: "Nether wart grows on soul sand".
 (c) A boat aimed where it can't sit: "Point at water or the top of a block to set the boat down".
 (d) A minecart aimed off the rails: "Minecarts go on rails".
 (e) Bone meal on something it can't grow: "Bone meal only works on plants that can still grow".
 (f) Same manners as Q2c-console-39: on a fresh press only, each reason at most every four seconds, with the soft refusal click and a light tick in the hands; nothing changes when the use does work.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/Farming.swift, Boats.swift): (a) four more refusals through Game.placeRefused; (b) Game.useItemOnBlock: a def.plants item on grass, dirt, coarse or rooted dirt or podzol says to till first; nether wart off its soil names both in the game's own words (Items.name, so the IP name check stays clean); occupied farmland stays silent; (c) placeBoat with no water or block top in reach refuses (and consumes the press); (d) a Carts.variant item not on a rail refuses; (e) bone meal whose useBoneMeal fails refuses; (f) useItemOnBlock and placeBoat run only on useNow, and placeRefused keeps its 4 s per-reason gate, click and PadManager tick; the working paths are untouched.
Q2c-console-59. [DONE] A world's story where he looks (Q2c-console-57's statistics sat on their own page; choosing a world showed its mode and day but not how much of his life was in it, and the death screen never said how long he had lasted).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, ready to sell): the numbers that make a world feel his show up at the moments he cares.
 (b) The Worlds list rows add the time played ("3 h"), and the world page's description adds "3 h 12 min played".
 (c) The death screen says how long this life lasted: "You lasted 1 h 12 min this time" (play time since the last respawn; "under a minute" when short).
 (d) Pause > Statistics gains "Longest life", kept with the world.
 (e) Worlds from before the statistics show no time at all rather than zero.
 (f) The list reads the time from world.json's extras, which it already decodes: no world is loaded to show it.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/Stats.swift, WorldStore.swift, BaseSundries.swift, Game.swift): (a) Stats gains life, lifeBegan/lifeEnded and playedIn; (b) WorldStore.Info.played (from the extras' "stats" time) adds ", N min" or ", N h" to rowLabel (the name is shortened first if needed) and "3 h 12 min played" to describe; (c) deathDetail ends with "You lasted ... this time." from Stats.life (time minus lifeStart; "under a minute" below 60 s), in survival persistent worlds; respawn calls lifeBegan; (d) die calls lifeEnded, which keeps bestLife, shown as "Longest life" (or the current life if longer) on the Statistics page; (e) played is nil without stats and the list/description skip anything under a minute; (f) WorldStore.list already decodes WorldMeta for each world, so this is one string scan per world.
Q2c-console-60. [DONE] Knockback as a comfort option (a zombie's hit shoved him back about a block and a half with a hop, and a blast threw him further: the view moving without him moving is the strongest nausea trigger left in combat, and there was no way to soften it).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, comfort options): being hit hurts and is felt, without the world lurching.
 (b) "Knockback: Full / Reduced / Off" on VR Comfort & Controls: Reduced pushes 40% as far, Off not at all; damage, the red flash, the thump in the hands and the heartbeat are unchanged.
 (c) It covers every push he takes: creature and weapon hits, arrows and shots, and explosions.
 (d) The Comfort presets set it: Comfort Off, Moderate Reduced, Free Full.
 (e) New players start at Reduced, matching the Moderate preset the headset starts on; a help line says what each does.
 (f) Only the headset: the Mac keeps the full push.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/Game.swift, Explosion.swift, quest/src/app/QuestSettings.swift, QuestControls.swift, QuestOptions.swift): (a) Game.knockbackScale multiplies every push on the player; (b) "Knockback: Full / Reduced / Off" (q_knock: 1, 0.4, 0) on VR Comfort & Controls; only player.vel is scaled, the damage path before it is untouched; (c) Game.hurtPlayer (creatures, weapons, arrows, shots) and Explosion's push both use it; (d) applyComfort sets 0 / 0.4 / 1 for Comfort / Moderate / Free, and the q_comfort help mentions it; (e) QuestSettings.knockback defaults to 0.4 (in snapshot, so Reset Options returns it there), help line q_knock; (f) QuestControls copies it into the game each frame; the Mac never sets it, so it stays 1.
Q2c-console-61. [DONE] Mining can be a toggle (breaking a block means holding the trigger for seconds, block after block: tiring over an hour and hard or impossible for players with weak or painful hands, and there was no alternative).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, accessibility and comfort): mining doesn't require holding a trigger down.
 (b) "Mining: Hold / Toggle" on VR Comfort & Controls (default Hold): with Toggle, one pull at a block starts mining it and it keeps going after the trigger is let go, until the block breaks.
 (c) It stops when the laser leaves that block, the trigger is pulled again, the held item changes, or a menu opens; the next block needs its own pull, so it never tunnels on by itself.
 (d) Only blocks: with a creature under the laser or a gun in hand the trigger works exactly as before.
 (e) Starting and stopping are felt: a short tick in the aiming hand.
 (f) The help line says how it works; Reset Options returns to Hold.
 Done Oct 15 (console; quest/src/app/QuestControls.swift, QuestSettings.swift, QuestOptions.swift): (a) QuestControls.mineToggleUpdate runs right after the trigger is read each frame; (b) "Mining: Hold / Toggle" (q_minetoggle, QuestSettings.mineToggle, default false); with Toggle a fresh pull (0.6 in, 0.35 out) at game.target latches it and p.rt stays 1 after the trigger is released; (c) the latch drops when the target is no longer that block (it broke, or the laser moved), the held item changes, a menu or pause is up, the player dies, or a second pull comes (that pull is swallowed until released); (d) no latch with heldGun, and none (or it drops) while mobs.raycast finds a creature nearer than the block; (e) a 220 Hz tick on latching, 120 Hz on stopping, in the aiming hand through HandedInput (Vibration scales them); (f) help line q_minetoggle; the key is in QuestSettings.snapshot, so Reset Options returns to Hold.
Q2c-console-62. [DONE] Sudden relocations fade in (an ender pearl landing, a portal, waking in bed, a respawn, a command teleport or being set at a ship's helm moved the whole world in a single frame: a hard cut, the most jarring thing a headset can show; only the teleport locomotion had a blink, and only with the vignette on).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, comfort): the world never jumps under him without a softening.
 (b) Any jump of the player by more than three blocks within one game tick fades the view in from dark over a quarter of a second.
 (c) Ordinary fast movement never triggers it: riding, ships, falling and gliding stay far under three blocks a frame; the teleport locomotion keeps its own blink.
 (d) It works whatever the Comfort Vignette is set to (it is a fade, not the vignette), so Free players get it too.
 (e) Respawns and dimension changes get it as well, softening the cut from the death and loading screens.
 (f) Two jumps close together (a portal straight into a fall respawn) make one fade, never a flicker.
 Done Oct 15 (console; quest/src/app/QuestControls.swift): (a) QuestControls.relocateFade, drawn by screenEffects as a full-view black tint at its value; (b) afterTick compares the feet after Game.tick with lastFeet (taken at the end of update, before the tick) and sets the fade to 1 past 3 blocks; it falls at 4 a second (0.25 s); (c) at 72 Hz even a 42 b/s frigate moves 0.6 blocks a frame; the teleport locomotion moves the feet inside update, before lastFeet, so it is not counted; (d) it is a tint independent of QuestSettings.vignette; (e) respawn, dimension changes and a freshly loaded world (lastFeet starts at the origin) all exceed 3 blocks; (f) a new jump just sets the value back to 1, one continuous fade.
Q2c-console-63. [DONE] No stack thrown away by a stray pull in the headset's inventory (the inventory window floats in a larger panel; a trigger pull with the laser just off the window, moving a stack between chest and pack, threw the whole carried stack out into the world, near lava or a cliff gone for good).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, nothing lost to one stray click): dropping items from a screen is always deliberate in the headset.
 (b) While a stack is on the cursor, a "Drop" box shows in the panel's margin to the right of the window, labelled, with the stack's count.
 (c) Only a pull inside that box throws the stack out (the grip throws one, as before); a pull anywhere else off the window does nothing and the stack stays on the cursor.
 (d) The box lights up while the laser is on it.
 (e) A screen too wide to leave room for the box (the crafting book at large sizes) simply can't drop by clicking off it in the headset; closing a screen still puts a carried stack back in the pack.
 (f) The Mac is unchanged: clicking outside the window drops, as in the reference game.
 Done Oct 15 (console; Sources/Menu.swift, MenuInput.swift, Renderer.swift): (a) Menu.dropBox / inDropBox, shared by the click handling and the drawing; (b) Renderer draws the box (dark red, framed, "Drop" and "xN") right of the window, centred on it, whenever the headset (game.vrHead) has a stack on the cursor (not on pause pages); (c) MenuInput's off-window drop now needs vrHead == nil or the laser in the box; the grip still drops one; elsewhere off the window the click falls through and nothing happens; (d) the box turns bright red with a white frame under the laser; (e) dropBox is nil when the margin is under 36 px (the window then has no off-window drop in the headset), and Game.closeMenu still returns a carried stack to the pack; (f) with vrHead nil (the Mac) the old rule applies unchanged.
Q2c-console-64. [DONE] The brewing stand says what it is doing and why it is stuck (it showed a fuel bar and an arrow; with empty glass bottles, no ingredient, an ingredient that does nothing to these bottles or no powder it just sat there, and items it wouldn't take bounced off its slots in silence).
 (a) Intent (Q2 c, clarity, the furnace's Q2c-console-40 for brewing): one glance at a brewing stand says what it is doing or what it needs.
 (b) While brewing: "Brewing: about 12 s", with "Fuel for 7 more brews" under it.
 (c) Stuck, in amber: no bottles ("Put water bottles in the three bottom slots"), only empty glass ("Fill the bottles with water first"), no ingredient ("Add an ingredient on top: start with Ember Wart"), an ingredient that does nothing ("Sugar does nothing to these bottles"), and no fuel ("Add Cinder Powder on the left to fuel it").
 (d) Something a slot won't take says why for a few seconds with the soft click: "Only bottles go here", "Stone isn't a brewing ingredient", "Only Cinder Powder fuels the stand".

 (f) The status sits between the stand and the pack, like the furnace's: the pack moves down to make room.

Q2c-console-65. [DONE] A slot that won't take an item says so, on every screen (dropping a carried item on an armour slot it doesn't fit, an anvil, smithing, loom, beacon or enchanting slot it doesn't belong in did nothing at all: the item stayed on the cursor without a sound, the "is it broken?" feeling in every workstation).

 (b) The soft refusal click plays and a short line above the window says why: armour slots name what fits ("Only boots go here"), other slots "Stone can't go in that slot".
 (c) The line fades after two and a half seconds and never covers the slots or the window.

Q2c-console-66. [DONE] The anvil says why nothing comes out (two items that can't be combined, a repair with the wrong material, an undamaged tool, a book whose enchantments don't fit, or too few levels left the output slot empty with no word except the cost when there was one).

Q2c-console-69. [DONE] The grindstone and the stonecutter say what they take (both showed empty slots and an empty output: nothing said that the grindstone strips enchantments or joins two worn copies, or that the stonecutter wants stone and a picked shape; wrong inputs gave nothing without a word).

## THE GAME'S SPINE (read first; every item serves this)
Play by the rules until you have iron, ideally diamond. Then you can take a village. Then the military bases (rare, tiered, GoldenEye-style), then the navy and its frigates, then hell, then the Reich below hell. Killing the Reich's leader beats the game. Gear tiers, tool upgrades and vehicles are the keys between stages.

FROM THE OCT 11 AM PLAYTEST (Remington on the Quest, build 0.129; check each against current claude/quest-port first):
G12. [capitals] [DONE: Lane A thread, 2026-10-11, PR #49; docs/status/tidebreaker-gaps.md] Capitals are far too common (two within 500 blocks) and one sat at the player's spawn with a crawler; make capitals rare, never near spawn, and procedurally varied in size (a pool of capital buildings where only some spawn per city).
G13. [frigate, with G1] [DONE: Lane A thread, 2026-10-11, PR #49; docs/status/tidebreaker-gaps.md] The naval/main gun does far too little damage: against a crawler it should tear off a huge chunk or destroy it outright. Ships should not carry Capital soldiers: the frigates are their own navy faction (A3).
G14. [done, Lane A, 639959b + G20 PR: two stone cells with iron doors, bunks and chains (check frontier: jail cell behind bars and an iron door); emerald tip, BANK signs and a town board line (check G14 emeralds)] [towns] The jail is broken (find what is wrong in a town jail and fix it). Emerald-to-cash is undiscoverable: make the bank exchange obvious (signs, a tip, a notice board line) and make emeralds findable in survival (where they come from, documented in the recipe book or a tip).
G15. [done, Lane A, 639959b: check G15 sheriff] [towns] Stealing a gun from a townsperson must be very hard before iron armour (the six-iron rule); verify the sheriff/deputies respond and the gun is guarded.
G16. [done, Lane A, 639959b + G20 PR: 16 health, 2.0 s fuse, 0.8 s setback a hit (check G16 Powderwight)] [mobs] The Powderwight is too hard to kill before it detonates, even with an iron sword; tune its health, fuse or telegraph so a prepared player can win.
G17. [done, Lane A, 639959b: hell's rock rises through the deep layers, hell-only spawns (check deep G17)] [hell] Entering hell from above ends at a lava-pool cut-off; make the transition from the deep rock into hell gradual and readable. Hell mobs must be hell's own types everywhere (check B6 covers all spawns).
G18. [done, Lane A, 639959b: grass in patches with tall tufts (check G18 grass patches, plants)] [looks] Plain grass looks barren: add more ground cover (tall grass tufts, short grass that rises slightly off the block, variety) within the Quest frame budget.
G19. [done, Lane A, 639959b: docs/status/difficulty.md (check G19 difficulty)] [new, Lane B] Difficulty levels: easy, medium, hard chosen when creating a world and locked afterwards; they change enemy speed, damage, health, hunger and spawn density. Document the table.

[done, G20 PR: OriginalSites.swift; docs/status/ip-content-audit-oct11.md] [IP, from P19] Replace the remaining close copies of reference features with original designs (docs/status/ip-content-audit-oct11.md: the Hollow + Hollow Spire + Void Dragon, Cinder Fortress / Boarling Keep, Sea Temple, Forest Manor, Proving Halls). The murk depths, Buried Citadel and Deep Stalker summons are already out (P19).
