## LANE A (cloud thread A): factions, bases, navy, vehicles

A1. [done, Lane A PR #52, docs/status/greywall.md; leftovers T6] Military bases as their own faction (separate from the Capital). Intent: castle-like fortified bases, confined to the base, tiers of bases like GoldenEye N64, very rare, naval guns and small turrets, ranked troops.
- 4+ base tiers: outpost, garrison, fortress, command fortress; each larger, rarer, better defended and better looted. Original layouts; the grey-concrete, blue-corner-light, metal-door feel of the Siberian levels is the art direction.
- Walls, gatehouses, towers, bunkers, barracks, armoury, comms room, motor pool, vault. Alarms with red lights; reinforcements arrive while the alarm is up; alarm panels can be destroyed.
- Turrets: small auto-turrets on walls and corridors, and coastal or naval guns (several calibres) at shore bases, all with readable telegraphs, line of sight and destructible states.
- Troops never leave the base footprint (leash), but chase within it and call for help.
- Each base has a map table that, once taken, reveals other bases on the map (the radar network).
- Taking a base: kill or drive out the garrison, take the command room, the flag changes; it stays yours and can be used as a home, with respawn rules documented.
- Rarity tuned so a walking player finds one in a long session; a frigate makes them reachable.

A2. [done, Lane A PR #52, docs/status/troop-classes.md; leftovers T7] Troop classes. Intent: three classes, Regular, Spec Ops and Hannibal troops (unless that name is a problem; Hannibal is historical, check the coined list anyway), each with its own armour level that gets damaged over time, its own class of guns, and rarity rising with the level.
- Armour as segmented durability (helmet, chest, legs) that visibly cracks and falls off; damaged armour lets more damage through; players can loot damaged sets and repair them.
- Weapon classes per troop: regulars with rifles and SMGs, spec ops with suppressed and scoped weapons plus grenades, Hannibal troops with heavy weapons and the best armour. Loot rarity tiers (common, fine, rare, elite) with stat ranges.
- Behaviour per class: regulars patrol and hold, spec ops flank and use stealth, Hannibal troops push and suppress. Mixed squads with an officer.
- Balance against player gear tiers; write the time-to-kill table.

A3. [done, Lane A PR #52, docs/status/navy.md; leftovers T8] The navy (the frigates' faction). Intent: frigates are the UNSC-like faction with super soldiers with shields, done without infringing.
- Rename everything player-visible to our own navy (keep the Meridian name if it is already ours). Super soldiers get an original name (not Spartan) and regenerating energy shields that flare, pop and recharge after a delay; shield breakers (EMP-like weapons) as counters.
- Frigates patrol the seas, have radar, launch boarding parties and small craft, and bombard shore positions; frigates can be boarded and captured.
- A captured frigate becomes the player's fast vehicle with radar that shows bases and ships in range.

A4. [flyers done, Lane A PR #52, docs/status/flyers-a4.md; the crawler still open: T9] Vehicles that fly, and the crawler. Intent: ways to make vehicles that fly; the crawler needs to be perfected.
- Finish the free-moving block structures engine (old PR 8) so players can build aircraft from blocks: engines, lift surfaces, control seat; weight and thrust matter. Plus at least one craftable ready-made flyer for players who don't want to build.
- VR comfort for flight: cockpit frame, vignette, horizon lock option, snap or smooth turning.
- The crawler: smooth movement on all terrain, no clipping or jitter, sensible speed and weapons, boarding and exiting in VR, damage states, repair.
- Vehicles persist in saves and are found where you left them.

A5. [done, Lane A PR #52, docs/status/map-fog-a5.md] Radar and discovery. Bases reveal other bases; frigates have radar; a map and compass that show discovered sites, with fog of war.

## LANE B (cloud thread B): progression, law, hunting, hell, the Reich

B1. Sheriff's six-iron and the iron rule. [DONE: Lane B thread, 2026-10-11, PR #28; docs/status/six-iron.md] Intent: the sheriff carries a six-shooter that one-shots you in anything below iron armour, is a middle ground in iron, and hardly hurts diamond and above. You play by the rules until you have diamond or at least iron, then you can take a village.
- Every sheriff carries the six-iron (replaces the 5 percent gun chance). Readable draw, warning shot, fair reaction time; deadly at range in VR, dodgeable.
- Damage tuned per armour tier and documented: leather or none = death in one shot, iron = several shots, diamond and up = chip damage.
- Taking a village: defeat the sheriff and deputies; the town surrenders, becomes yours (trade discounts, tribute, a bed and storage), honour consequences, and nearby towns react.

B2. Townsfolk variety. [DONE: Lane B thread, 2026-10-11, PR #32; docs/status/townsfolk-variety.md] Intent: villager attack rates, health and speed vary.
- Per-person stats rolled from role and age: farmers weak and slow, blacksmiths strong, deputies fast and armed. Visible cues (build, tools) so players can read them.

B3. Hunting like RDR2. [DONE: Lane B thread, 2026-10-11, PR #35; docs/status/hunting.md] Intent: hunting with pelts and the rest.
- Animal quality (poor, good, perfect) shown when studying an animal; clean kills with the right weapon keep perfect pelts; skinning animation; pelts, meat, hides and trophies.
- A trapper or butcher in towns buys pelts by quality; crafted outfits, saddles and satchels from pelts (bigger inventory).
- Legendary animals with map clues and unique pelts. Fishing with species, sizes and a fishing minigame that works in VR.

B4. Countries that give minerals. [DONE: Lane B thread, 2026-10-11, PR #39; docs/status/countries.md] Intent: there should be countries too that give minerals.
- The world is divided into a handful of countries with borders, flags, capitals and towns, each rich in particular minerals. They trade, tax and pay tribute; you can ally with one, raid another, or take it over through its capital. Border posts, customs and wars between countries that change the map over time.

B5. Enchantments become tool upgrades. [DONE: Lane B thread, 2026-10-11, PR #40; docs/status/upgrades.md] Intent: enchanting is too similar to the original; make it tool upgrades that still need experience.
- A workbench upgrade tree per tool and weapon (sharper edge, reinforced head, balanced grip, scopes, extended mags) paid with experience plus materials; visible changes on the model; old enchanted items convert in saves.

B6. Hell's own mobs. [DONE: Lane B thread, 2026-10-11, PR #44; docs/status/hell-mobs.md] Intent: hell needs its own mobs, including a dark burgundy knight class. Hell comes after mastering the bases and getting upgrades.
- A knight order in dark burgundy armour: squire, knight, champion and a commander mini-boss, with shields, parries and charges that suit VR melee. Plus 3+ other original hell mobs with distinct roles (flying, swarm, ranged).
- Gate: hell is reachable only with base-tier gear (a key or charge looted from command fortresses), so the order is clear.

B7. The Reich below hell. [DONE: Lane B thread, 2026-10-11, PR #46; docs/status/the-last-war.md] Intent: below hell is Nazi Germany: tiger tanks, black everything, red flags with black, replicas of German weapons from the time; it should be clear what we are going for. You kill them and you have beaten the game.
- Build on the Ashguard and the Ash Vault (PR 22 and the gap pass). Black uniforms and vehicles, red banners with a black fictional emblem, heavy tanks in the style of the period, half-tracks, artillery, and period-style rifles, SMGs and MGs under fictional names.
- A final campaign: fortified lines, a leader in a final bunker, a big last battle and an ending.
- Store-safe as stated at the top.

Keep compute flat or lower. EXPANDED OBJECTIVES (overseer, 10-10 14:45; meet these as well as the items above):
SKY: day gradient that shifts with sun height; real sunrise/sunset colours; moon with phases and stars at night; 2-3 cloud layers at different speeds following one wind direction; overcast and rain skies darken the world and the clouds; storms with distant lightning flashes; the sky matches each biome (hazier in desert, cold blue in snow); cheap on Quest (measure).
MOBS AND ANIMALS: every mob gets walk, run, idle, attack, hurt and death animations (no sideways flip deaths); heads track the player; hurt flash and knockback that read clearly; sounds matched to the new looks; size/colour variants and babies for animals; hostile silhouettes readable at night at 20 blocks; a before/after screenshot sheet for every mob.
CREEPER REPLACEMENT: a design note (name, look, behaviour, counterplay, drops, advancement); a clear telegraph (sound and visual) before it detonates or attacks; it fills the same gameplay role (punishes inattention, damages builds) but is original; old saves convert existing creepers.
TREES: at least 4 shapes per species with branches and root flare; leaf colour varies with biome; occasional fallen logs, stumps and dead trees; big rare old trees; far-LOD silhouettes match the new shapes; no floating leaves after generation changes.
GENERAL: golden screenshot set before and after (day, night, rain, 3 biomes); fps measured on the Quest build before and after with no regression; list anything else you improved under the 'wide leeway' rule.

[DONE: Lane C thread, 2026-10-11, PRs #42 (ground), #43 (perf overlay, world gen, eruptions), #45 (VR combat feel); docs/status/ground-l3.md, l3-part3.md, vr-combat-feel.md. The 20-minute soak, device fps and the Quest build ship are on lane M's device checklist. Ruined portals were removed at Remington's request.]
FLESH-OUT of the Oct 10 AM playtest task (Remington, 10-10 14:45). That task got his literal list; these are the extra objectives (written by the overseer) it must also meet. Read what 25b4z already shipped (git log, docs/playtests/2026-10-10/voice-notes-status.md), skip what is truly MET, build the rest. Grade everything at the end with the DONE GATE reviewer.
TEXTURES/GROUND
- Before/after captures from the Quest build at 1, 4 and 16 blocks for stone, chest, grass, ores, planks; put them in the status doc.
- No shimmering or moire at distance (tune mip bias per material), no blur up close; consistent texel density across all blocks.
- Ores readable at 10+ blocks; every ore distinct by colour and shape, not just tint.
- Grass: 3-4 top variants with random rotation so tiling never shows, biome tint, a proper side overhang; grass side reads at a glance.
- Flower bushes: 2-3 variants, cheap wind sway, rustle sound when walked through.
RENDER DISTANCE / PERF
- Hysteresis: auto-drop only after sustained low fps, auto-recover after sustained headroom; never ratchet down forever.
- An optional perf overlay (fps, frame ms, render distance, chunk jobs) in settings.
- A 20-minute soak in the busiest town and the taiga on the Quest build with logged min/avg fps; list the top 3 frame-time costs and what was done about each.
WORLD GEN
- Capitals: a minimum distance from spawn and from each other, checked over 50 seeds with the numbers in the doc; spawn always gets a natural start area.
- Volcanoes: foothills that blend (gradient of tuff/ash/basalt into the biome), a cheap smoke plume visible far away, lava glow at night, a correct far-LOD silhouette, rare small eruptions with particles and rumble.
- Ruined portals: rare, each one with loot worth the detour and a reason to exist.
TOWNS
- Townsfolk have names shown when looked at, greet by time of day, have work and sleep hours; shops close at night with a sign.
- Wallet: animated gain/loss with a coin sound; prices and the emerald exchange rate shown in every shop UI.
- Theft uses line of sight: unseen theft found later costs less; witnesses run to the sheriff; a bounty that fades over time or can be paid.
- Sheriff: patrol routes, warns first, can fine or arrest instead of always fighting; despawns/respawns sensibly; never kills the player over a tiny theft.
- Voice lines: at least 8 per role per situation (greet, trade, warn, angry, flee, goodbye), never the same line twice in a row, spatial audio with falloff, optional subtitles.
- Old-save migration test: a pre-towns save loads with every villager converted.
CAMPS
- At least 5 camp types (hunter, trader, bandit, deserter, surveyor), each with a campfire (light, smoke, cooking), a tent or lean-to, loot that fits, and an owner; some friendly (trade, rest), some hostile.
- Rarity tuned so you find one every 10-15 minutes of travel; respawn rules documented.
SWING MODE / RECLINED MODE
- First-use tutorial prompt for each mode; mode switch reachable in VR without taking the headset off.
- Blade trail, a tiny hit-stop on contact, damage scaled by swing speed, distinct haptics for flesh, armour and stone.
- Friendly-fire guard: swinging near villagers, pets or your horse never hurts them unless you deliberately attack.
- Enemy wind-ups are telegraphed (pose plus sound) so physical dodging and blocking are fair; sword-on-weapon contact can parry.
- Reclined mode: a soft aim-assist cone, seated height calibration, comfort defaults (snap turn, vignette).
BOW
- Nock animation, draw creak, string vibration and sound on release, draw strength sets speed and damage, arrows can be picked up; works left- and right-handed; crossbow uses the same hand logic.
Finish: update voice-notes-status.md with evidence per note, ship one Quest build (tools/quest-local.sh, install if adb sees the Quest), DONE GATE.

## OVERSEER'S FEATURES (Claude's list, trimmed by Remington Oct 10 21:52; lane in brackets)
C1. [B] [DONE: Lane A thread, 2026-10-12, docs/status/railways.md; questcheck --rail-only] Railways (procedural, rare): rail lines generated with the world, linking several villages and towns, so finding one is a big find: it is fast travel to many places. Generation must be robust: lines follow terrain with cuttings, bridges and tunnels, stations only at real villages, never through structures, never floating; a worldgen check over 50 seeds proves it. Steam trains run the lines on a timetable; you can ride, and RDR-style train robberies (stop the train, crack the strongbox, outrun the posse) carry honour and bounty fallout. Armoured trains in the Ash Vault.
C2. [A] [done, Lane A PR #52, docs/status/war-map.md; leftover T12] The living war map, procedural only: factions hold generated territory and front lines shift over in-game weeks from simulation, not hand-placed events; battles happen without you; your actions tip them; a war table in captured bases shows it. If it can't be made fully procedural and stable, drop it and say so.
C3. [B] [DONE: Lane A thread, 2026-10-12, docs/status/nation.md; questcheck --nation-only] Your own nation: villages and bases you take become provinces; settlers arrive, build and farm; you set taxes and laws; they need defending; your flag on the map; a capital you design.

It only works for a while: a suspicion meter rises with each request and with odd requests; when they find out, the faction goes on alert, sends a hunter squad and stops answering that radio.

C14. [DONE Oct 13: Play Together over Wi-Fi (pause menu): host this world or find/join a friend's (LAN beacon or typed address). The host owns the world (blocks, chests, mobs, time); the guest owns its body, inventory and health, sees the host and the host's mobs, and its edits, hits, pickups and kills sync both ways (Sources/NetLink.swift, NetCoop.swift; questcheck --net-only). Part 2 (NetShare.swift): the host's vessels mirror for the guest (ride along on deck; the host steers), the guest rides the host's horses (moved over the link, the host's AI resting), and the guest's thefts, assaults, gunfire and mob uses (rider satchels, strongboxes) run on the host's towns, so robberies are shared. Capital ships are not sent; trade screens open on the host only.] [A+B, last] Two-player co-op on Quest: a friend joins your world (host-authoritative), shares horses, vehicles and robberies. The biggest sales driver on Quest and the biggest job, so it goes last, after the single-player game is store-ready.

## Inspiration (feel and mechanics only)
GoldenEye 007 (base tiers, alarms, stealth), Red Dead Redemption 2 (law, honour, hunting, towns, horses), Halo (shields, vehicles, frigates), Far Cry (outposts), Mount and Blade (taking villages, factions), Valheim and Terraria (boss-gated progression), Metal Gear Solid (alerts and stealth), Wolfenstein (the final layer), Sea of Thieves (naval play).

If we could fix that, we're almost ready to make this game go"; a house where "these logs are" doesn't look right (16:05). Earlier: hitting a log, "this giant log, which isn't the same log I hit, morphed into something else... the log just got wider" (P428), and P406, P407 before it.

P434. [DONE #465 (a)-(e) and this PR: (f) a body over the flying hull (or with the ship under it) boards its frame, so a 1x1 hole in a turned hull is a hole, not a fattened box; (g) a hard knock (9 b/s or more where she strikes, short of the 14 b/s crash that wrecks her) throws the drive engines off their mounts: they stop and smoke, and she coasts to a stop; at 11 b/s or more 2+ engine blocks are torn out too. An ingot of steel or iron used on the hull restarts them (and refits up to 8 torn-out engine blocks an ingot). Note: a save and reload also restarts shaken engines.] [Remington, Oct 24 15:24-15:33] THE FRIGATE. (a) men shoot through its ceiling and doors ("something I've talked about before, and it was not fixed"); (b) he can't open a door while holding a gun; (c) the layout is dense but unintuitive: he couldn't find the engine room or the helm, and a door sits above a ladder; (d) rockets don't work inside it; (e) a submarine hunted him while he was in the frigate; (f) dropping in through a hole was blocked while it flew; (g) his idea: when the ship hits something hard, its momentum breaks parts loose in the engine room and it stops working, with a simple repair if the hit wasn't too hard.

P435. [DONE (#472 + follow-up): his tank cannon is a 75 mm round that blows out walls and holes plate, the field gun and the dropship's autocannon (fires while held) hit harder and break blocks; his rounds wear buildings twice as fast; the tank sprints to about 36 km/h (the stick eases the throttle); a flak battery's gun can be manned; a knocked-out tank with its turret on is repaired with Track Spares (Ashguard stores and command posts) and driven, clean; the sidecar's helm moved behind the handlebars (it steered from over the rear wheel: the walking-with-it and inverted feel); the salvaged fighter takes off in about 7 s and flies near 216 km/h, trimmed level with the stick centred, and a hard hit on a wing tears it off; eating at the controls of a vehicle; the 9 mm sidearm's new sound levelled with the others; on Easy and Very Easy no enemy rolls tougher than plain and the Deep's veterans lose doubled health; naval guns on the base towers; the artillery site's fence goes all the way round. Then (this PR): the Salvaged Fighter in the crashed one's colours (bare aluminium skin and wings, olive anti-glare panel, dark cowl ring; was scorched armour plate with the Capital's white wings); an Ashguard soldier only ever carries the army's period arms (swapped back if anything hands him another); the sidecar's helm is taken from beside it like a vessel's, so he drives it in the chase view instead of pushing it on foot. Already there: the wire hurts on contact (Farming.swift; he took damage right after 15:56), and the crash-site repair is the fighter's (spares from the bomber's chest; it worked at 15:52:23).] [Remington, Oct 24 15:36-16:03] WEAPONS AND VEHICLES. Dropship gun and the tank cannon do far too little damage, and guns barely damage buildings (he wants much more); the tank should reach 30-40 km/h when sprinting; he should be able to mount the turret, use the artillery, and repair and drive or fly the crashed tank and bomber (a crash site with a repair recipe); the unknown driveable vehicle (15:50-15:51) moves like he's walking with it on top of him with inverted controls and should be third person; planes should be very fast, can't really take off, and a wing should tear off when it hits a pillar; eat while in a car or tank; one gun's sound "sucks" (16:01); WW2 soldiers carry what look like Capital guns (15:56, 15:58); one soldier was far too tough on easy (16:01); naval guns on the towers; the wire should hurt a little when he crosses it.

P436. [DONE (#471 + this PR): two original mobs per hell land (Cinder Hound, Dune Crawler, Shard Skitter, Ash Wraith, Emberkite, Charwood Lurker, Glass Wight), the Gloamwood's milder; hell spawns hostiles in creative too; lava in broad molten and crusted fields instead of one repeated picture; fire throws glowing tongues; amethyst clusters are crystal columns (pointed prisms in field worlds); clear glass is tinted see-through glass with a sheen and thin frame, stained glass lost the copied streak marks; rare places about twice as common; /locate searches the other dimensions too; a dug block takes a wider bite of soft ground. Then: long winding no-man's-land belts far beyond the front, and two era biomes, Drowned Fields (mud, shell-hole pools, reeds, posts) and Burnt Woods (ash silt, dense dead trees).] [Remington, Oct 24 15:44-15:48] HELL AND THE WORLD. Hell looks great ("love hell") but needs more mobs and more variety: about two original mobs per biome, the purple biome's less hostile; amethyst and fire need work (fire is the cheap paper-fold look); the lava looks like one picture repeated; rare locations twice as common ("I've flown this much, I should have seen something"); a command to find things in another dimension; breaking dirt should make a bigger hole; more biomes, including a long no-man's-land and two more that fit that era; glass needs fixing everywhere.

P437. [DONE (#468 + follow-up): townspeople back to their full look (no far rounding), they no longer stop in or choose a goal in a doorway and step out of one; a cut-off notice-board line (the sheriff's) shows whole, wrapped, when hovered; Hannibal troopers take to their jets in any fight within 48 blocks; fog eases in and out over about 3 s instead of jumping (Auto View Distance steps, weather); music holds a calm track at least 45 s, a new mood must last 12 s, and tracks cross-fade (8 s calm, 6 s out of a fight); the Ash Vault lag: a block placed or broken where it and its neighbours stand on a straight built column to the ground skips the structure check (it filled 1,536 blocks plus a 24,000-block search every change there; questcheck --view --edittime measures it). Then (this PR): the Ironback Crawlers roll from the start (only the Stormwarden warships still wait for a Greywall Fortress; /locate sent him to a crawler that wasn't rolling), /locate and Shortcuts give a vessel's live position when it is out, and say when the Stormwarden Frigates don't sail yet; the citadel's Kestrel was checked on his world (seed 5663059916303033239: the citadel at -11832 ~ -1688 parks it at once, questcheck --worldseed S --from X,Z --basetest-only kestrel): it stands on the pad once he is within 160 blocks, and he was about 540 blocks off when he asked.] [Remington, Oct 24 15:17-16:05] PEOPLE, SOUND AND SMALLER THINGS. Townspeople look like dolls: revert them to their look from before the big animal change (as with the animals); NPCs stand in doorways; he couldn't read what the sheriff said; an enemy who can fly didn't attack or use flight; he didn't find the crawler or a helicopter where expected; fog should drift in slowly, not appear and vanish in a second; music changes between tracks too abruptly; big lag placing a block (16:01) and breaking one underground (16:03).

P438. [DONE (this PR): /ending army|bunker|chancellor|credits puts the war's end at that step with him where it happens; once the last army breaks, the Chancellor's voice over the loudspeakers says where he waits (behind the blast door in the command bunker), again every 2 minutes while the door is shut.] [Remington, Oct 24 16:02-16:03] THE ENDING. After clearing the final area: "Now what? What ends the game? I need to be able to test that."

P439. [DONE (list for him): docs/store/say-so.md lists what needs his say-so (app ID, release key, privacy page, price, rating answers incl. saloon gambling, generated-asset rights, the Director model, credits name, headset soak); credits-and-licences.md corrected (textures Gemini, sounds and voices ElevenLabs, music Google Flow).] [Remington, Oct 24 15:55] STORE READY. "In the next round of builds, I want it to be ready to be put on the store. Whatever has to be done. Oct 24 evening playtest (from his voice notes 20:47-21:01 on build 47f4c4d4, docs/playtests/2026-10-24/voice-notes.md; written up by the open lane, which now owns every open item). "Key theme here: performance, performance, performance." Seeds: New World (3) 5933896066598527833 (20:47-20:55), Quest World 5663059916303033239 (20:55-21:01). Good: people fixed ("people are fixed, thank God"), pigs restored, water looks wonderful, the tree falling over and rolling.

Oct 25 morning playtest (overseer, from his 189 voice notes 08:45-09:20 on build c7922f35, docs/playtests/2026-10-25/voice-notes.md, each with position, biome and fps). Read with BLOOM, DON'T PATCH at the top; he said it again here (08:54): "I need more care put into my fixes. Like, if I say the pillagers aren't doing very well, let's check all the pillagers. Make sure there's nothing else that doesn't look like shit. Just when I give a bug fix, be expansive like that. Think of everything." Liked: the new factions ("these guys look really good... well done"), the blast damage ("this is beautiful"), the drop ship arriving, the tree staying one colour, a campsite, the trader's horse.

Oct 25 afternoon playtest (overseer, from his voice notes 13:52-14:29 on build 39bfbb8a, docs/playtests/2026-10-25/voice-notes.md). Liked: "rendering at 16 is doing well. This is playable"; the sheriff's office "looks wonderful"; the homestead "looks great"; "everything was pretty smooth, all things considered"; the sunken dreadnought's engine room; a crawler "beautiful... big too"; "current compression of the far distance is acceptable"; "faces are perfect. Leave them the way they are"; "these horses look terrific"; small-arms damage on normal is right; "I'm pretty happy with the game"; "I think we can do a store release."

Part 2 in #507: the Vehicle Works always opens its list; each row shows the vehicle's whole bill of parts (what it takes, or what is still short), spelled out in full when pointed at. P472. [DONE: parts 1-5 in #547, #552, #553, #565, #567. Part 1 in #547: under way the frigate (and every Capital hull) breaks what she runs into, trees, plants, planking, glass and wool always and built blocks short of stone at 8 blocks a second (`questcheck --frigatecheck`: through a stand of trunks and leaves, no crash); crashes, hard knocks and crash landings no longer hurt him (he rides them out, the crew still suffer); at a warship's helm the right stick turns her instead of his view. Part 2 in #552: with a gun in hand the use press opens a ship's chests and barrels and takes its helm; a chart table or lectern aboard reads out the deck plan (where the helm and the engine room are from him, how many decks up or down); at a taken frigate's helm the press that charges the main gun fires the point-defence guns too (it took the press whole) (`questcheck --shipchart`). Part 3 in #553: the frigate walkable end to end: the main deck's stair stopped a full block short of the upper deck and the bridge stair cut the upper corridor in two (the helm and the aft upper deck could only be reached by jumping or breaking walls); a passage now runs beside the bridge stair and every room left out of reach gets a door onto a reached one, walked from the boarding hangar (`questcheck --frigatelayout`: helm and engine room reached, no pocket left, FRIGATE_PLANS=1 prints the decks); four armed Meridian Engineers in the engine room. Part 4 in #565: at the helm of a big hull (longer or wider than 24 blocks, so the frigate and every Capital warship) or of anything flying 32 blocks over the sea, the ground past the loaded chunks shows as the low-poly far ring out to 1024 blocks (one draw call), even under Auto View Distance, whose haze otherwise closes the view at the drawn edge (`questcheck --frigatecheck`). Part 5 in #567: the frigate laid out as a warship is: one stairwell climbs the ship (a two-wide flight from the boarding hangar's forward end up into the main deck's stair hall, a switchback up to the upper deck's landing, then the stair to the bridge: the helm is reached by stairs alone, no ladder); a passage down the middle of each deck shut by pairs of watertight doors at the main bulkheads, athwartship passages out to the portholes; the crew's berthing forward (racks of three bunks along the hull and down the middle, lockers, chests), the stair hall (rails round both openings, a rifle rack), the mess deck (two long tables with benches, a serving counter, galley aft), and above them officers' cabins, the wardroom (long table, sideboard) and the captain's cabin (bunk, desk, chart table, chest); the rest smaller compartments (stores, workshops, armoury, medbay, heads, laundry, comms, chart room) (`questcheck --frigatelayout`, `--frigatecheck`; `questcheck --view OUT --frigatein X,Y,Z` looks round inside).] [Remington, Oct 26 19:43-19:50] THE FRIGATE INSIDE AND AT THE HELM. "Lots of doors here. This is just boring though, like too predictable. It needs to be intuitively laid out, but not predictable like this. It's just going room to room like, who cares?" (19:44); "I wasn't able to open the chest when I had a gun in my hand"; "Not sure what these map blocks are for, but they're not doing anything when I click on them. Like, where do I even go?"; "This isn't intuitively designed at all. I have to break through a wall now... Why doesn't this hallway link up with the one right beside it? Why doesn't this lead to the engine room?" (19:45). "Engine room, nice. Should be like engineer troops in here too... Engineering troops with guns" (19:46). "The little guns on the frigate are not firing" (the main gun does) (19:47). "I really do like the way the ship handles though. This is really nice" (19:47). "Crashing shouldn't kill you like that" (19:48). Seeing the ground from the helm: "we need some sort of simplification of the ground below it, that way I can render out... way better, 'cause I can't see shit when I'm driving that thing. I don't have a very good understanding of the ground at all. I don't know how to simplify it in a way that's computer efficient, but we gotta find a way, I think. Just for when driving the frigate" (19:49-19:50). MORE FROM HIM (20:14): "With the frigate, it's just lazy design. It's too plain and boring, but it's also unintuitive, because there's a hallway that doesn't connect to anything else. It's just a hallway. And I had to break through a wall to find other hallways. It doesn't make sense. Just design a ship like a ship would be designed. Come up with your own idea of how the layout of a real-life ship would actually be, and design it that way. So there'd be a bit of nuance to it, and it's also going to be intuitively designed. So if I find the frigate and I want to find the helm, I can fight my way to it." "I do like how it handles, kind of, but the right joystick being up and down makes it confusing, because it's not left and right. Because if I do left and right, it's as if I'm turning my head, which screws it up." "With a wreck, I don't see why that needs to actually hurt me. If I hit the ground, I don't see why that needs to kill me." "The frigate should also break through trees and break through blocks too, to a degree. And we need the physics engine underneath to be able to handle that."

(Related: P446, P450.)

Also: breaking blocks meshes on the edit lane (fps 4 mining with a full pack), dropped items lying still sleep and far ones are not drawn, the near chunks the way he faces come first] [Remington, Oct 25 08:48-09:20] LOADING AND RENDERING, STILL. "We did not fix the rendering problems. We are still having really hard time rendering"; "I can see so far away underground right now. I think that's a part of the issue"; "I'm pretty sure the reason is it's literally trying to render it from the bottom up... from the negative y values upwards"; "view distance is ten, and yet it's generating underground as if it's more than ten"; "in creative, I can't really travel anywhere"; "once it gets into water, it seems to catch up"; a village stayed half-rendered for about 20 s: "it's almost like it has to render everything under it underground before it's allowed to render the top half"; "the rendering was supposed to have been fixed and it was not fixed at all"; the new world and the world from two builds ago behave differently; stuck in an explosion pit that hadn't rendered ("explosion pits need to render quicker"); "it's freezing every time I break a block" (fps 4 at 09:13:37, breaking dirt); "I think it's all the blocks that are on the ground that are causing the game to have trouble." (d) the freeze on every placed block: a placed stone remeshed a ring of 27 sections on the frame (7-8 s in the debug harness, about 0.5 s on the headset, his "point five seconds"); now only the sections the change touches (1-4) are remeshed, and that meshing runs on its own lane off the frame: 12 ms on the frame in the debug harness instead of 1,900 ms, the block shows once its lane finishes. P431. [REVERTED by P432, #464: #460 and #461 taken back whole] [Remington, Oct 24 10:17-10:31] BACK TO A BLOCK WORLD, WITH SLOPES. His decision, in his words: "this whole polygonal idea, it ain't working... we gotta go back to blocks, and with blocks what we're gonna do differently is... one block leads into the other, it just turns into a slope... Here to here would be sloped automatically instead of being a broken off block. That way walking is smooth and everything. We just gotta make sure that things can land and still have ragdoll physics. But it should be a little simpler... No algorithmic anything. Nothing algorithmic besides what's generated, and things will be generated with the slope." And at 10:31: "Maybe we can go grab some of the old code from before. But the idea is, yeah, it's going to be a block world. The trees are just going to be wood blocks and no more screwed up bullshit with the trees. Just mimic Minecraft's generation in some way without crossing any lines. And the main difference is like things are going to be sloped more. Like terrain's just going to be automatically have like 45 degree slopes in places where there would be a block to block lift, which we don't want." A broken block does not turn into a slope (10:18). Context: the game was a block world until the polygon work began about Oct 13; a block world type and its code are still in git history and may still be in the tree (#426 says new worlds became Polygons field worlds "again"). The IP rule still applies (inspiration only). Earlier he said changes must not corrupt his current saved world. Performance is still the top concern (P403's numbers and P425's auto view). [DONE #460: new worlds are Blocks worlds again (Settings.smoothTerrain default false under a new key; a saved Polygons world still opens as it was, so his Quest World needs a new world to see this). Slopes: 16 states per ground material (grass, dirt, sand, gravel, stone, snow, mud... BlockRegistry.slopeMaterials), one per corner mask; WorldGen.placeSlopes puts one over the ground on every one-block lift (straight, inner corner, outer corner; cliffs of two or more and pits stay; nothing under water; the chunk's ring read from the density so edges meet); the Mesher draws them as wedges (RenderType.slope), bodies meet a 4x4 grid of columns under the drawn top (fine enough that walking reads smooth with the eased camera; no new collision code, so mobs, items and ragdolls land on them as on stairs); never an item, a broken slope drops its ground's drop and never comes back. Trees stay the block designs of P406.]

P425. [DONE #440: Auto View Distance (Options > Video, on by default, next to the P415 slider): the view follows the GPU's frame time 8-24 in steps of 2 under the slider (Sources/ViewGovernor.swift: a frame past 125 % of the drawn frame's budget drops at once to the view the quad model says fits 80 %, two windows past 92 % step down 2, 20 s of headroom step back up, loading and menus count neither way; the settled view is kept for the next launch); with it on the drawn edge fades into haze over its last 30 % and the far ring is off (fog instead of the far background); Off keeps the slider's view and the old thin fog band. Also: the frame budget both governors and the missed-frame count use is the drawn frame's (twice the display's under Space Warp), which the active-distance governor had been missing since #430. Trace check: jungle (59 ms at 24) -> view 10 within 5 s, desert -> 24 again in 50 s. Host frame times: lavapipe, none; the headset decides the view by its own GPU time, so the before/after is P403's table (jungle 1.11M quads at 24, 542k at 12).] [Remington, Oct 24 00:57] AUTO VIEW DISTANCE, FOG AT THE EDGE. His words: "we'll just do an auto slider... make it auto choose how far to set the render distance. And then instead of the far background, just make it fog or something, or whatever they do in Minecraft." Context: P403's numbers (in its note) show the headset is bound by quads, holding about 800k at 72 fps with SpaceWarp; after the cuts birch at view 24 is about 642k, snowy plains 880k, jungle 1.11M (needs about 16-20). His manual View Distance slider is P415. This replaces his earlier rules against fog and against lowering the view: the view may now change on its own and the edge may fade into fog. How it works is up to the builder.

P414. [DONE #431] [Remington, Oct 23 08:51] NEVER SLOW OR STOP THE PLAYER WHILE THE WORLD LOADS. In the Oct 22 playtest the game held back his movement whenever loading fell behind. His words: "make it so it doesn't restrict my movement... if I fall in and land in a bunch of unrendered area, whatever, I don't care. Just don't slow me down." STEPS: (1) find every place that clamps, slows, freezes or pushes back the player (walking, flying, riding, vehicles, ships) because a chunk, section or mesh is not ready, and remove it; (2) if the ground under him is not loaded he keeps moving and may fall; once it loads he lands on it normally (no death from falling through unloaded ground, no teleport back); (3) load priority: the column under and just ahead of the player is built first, so this rarely happens; (4) test: fly at top speed at view 24 for 60 s on the headset build settings; speed never drops; note how often he would see unloaded ground.

P415. [DONE #427 steps 1-3: Options > View Distance 24..8 in steps of 2, default 24, saved, live; lowering unloads far chunks and gives empty mesh slabs back (MeshArena.trim), raising refills from the mesh cache; active distance clamped to it. Step 4's frame times to follow (perfscenes spots birch22, jungle22 added)] [Remington, Oct 23 08:51] VIEW DISTANCE SLIDER IN OPTIONS. He wants to lower the view himself when the game isn't keeping up. STEPS: (1) a View distance slider in Options (8 to 24 chunks in steps of 2, default 24), saved per profile, applied live without a restart; (2) lowering it frees the far meshes and memory past the new distance; raising it fills back in from the mesh cache; (3) the active-distance slider can never exceed it; (4) frame time at 24, 16 and 12 at the Oct 22 jungle spot in the PR. The shipped default stays 24 until P403 step 8 and his choice.

P388. [DONE #414] [Remington, Oct 19, via overseer] WEIGHT AND BOND STRENGTH. His words: materials have weight and bond strength; sand and gravel already fall; wool breaks off easily, logs are strong, and a pile of heavy blocks (a bunch of titanium) on a wooden house makes it collapse. P380 only drops built pieces resting on nothing. STEPS: (1) a table per material: weight per block and bond strength (how much load a joint carries, and how far it can reach out unsupported): wool, leaves and glass weak; planks medium; logs and beams strong; stone heavy with fair bond; steel and titanium very heavy and very strong; in one Swift file with the reasoning for each number. (2) after a place, break or blast near built pieces (and FreeBuild pieces), a bounded load check like P380's: weight flows down to grounded pieces; a joint carrying more than its bond, or a run reaching out past its material's reach, fails. (3) failed parts break off and fall with P380's falling pieces and debris; what lands can crush what is under it. (4) cost: only near changes, capped per frame, measured on host. (5) nothing generated collapses: run the check once over every structure type the world generator builds (town, capital city, bases, bridges, keeps); if one would fall, fix the numbers, don't exempt it. (6) his existing worlds: nothing is checked until something near it changes, so loading never brings a build down. (7) tests in questcheck: a wool bridge breaks past its length, a log beam holds, titanium stacked on a plank roof brings the roof down, a stone arch stands; results in the note. NOTE: (1) done (#414): Sources/Structure.swift, one table with the reasoning by each number (weight in tenths of a tonne a block, bond what one face joint carries hanging or reaching out, reach in blocks): wool 1/30/2, glass 25/120/1 (framed modern panes 25/2000/1), soil 16/5/1, snow blocks 4/20/4, planks 6/200/8, logs and beams 8/800/10, stone 25/1500/5, ice 9/30/2, mushroom cap 2/100/8, concrete 24/10000/24, steel 78/12000/8, titanium (the diamond block) 45/15000/10, gold 193/300/3; slabs, stairs and fittings weigh by how much of the cell they fill. Leaves, plants, liquids and unbreakable blocks are not structural. (2) done (#414): any setBlock of a structural block (placing, breaking, blasts, landings) queues a check of the built piece round it (one per 4-block cube); the piece is flood-filled through faces and edges, what stands on the ground holds it, each block's reach is counted in sideways or hanging steps from that (resting on the block below or climbing costs none), and weight flows from the farthest blocks to the ground along the strongest short route (stepping onto a much weaker block, or onto a fitting, costs more); a block past its material's reach or handing on more than the weaker bond of a joint (edges half, straight down a thousand times) breaks. FreeBuild pieces are checked the same way as one graph. (3) done (#414): what breaks, and then whatever hangs from nothing, falls as P380's falling blocks; a block landing hard on a weaker one (weight x height over twice its bond) breaks it and falls on. (4) done (#414): one check a frame, near changes only, the fill bounded at 4,096 blocks and 24 out (past that the piece counts as held). Host, Stone Gulch at view 8, standing, BS_LOAD=0 then 1: tick mean 2.14 -> 2.19 ms (the difference is mobs, 1.33 -> 1.43), record 1.20 -> 1.22 ms; 209 checks, 0.01 ms mean, 0.11 ms worst, nothing fell. The bandit camp (earlier build, same check code): tick 1.41 -> 1.48, record 1.99 -> 2.02, 9 checks, 0.12 ms worst. The in-play load test's worst single check was 1.04 ms. (5) done (#414): questcheck --loadcheck builds every structure type at two of each on seeds 12345 and 777 and checks every piece whole. Block worlds: 0 of every type would fall (the ocean monument's 10 biggest pieces are past the fill and count as held). Field worlds: 0 would fall in every structure; the one block left is a lush cave's moss carpet near a capital city whose floor the cave's smooth shape opened (a generation stray, not a structure; left as it is). What fell before the numbers settled was fixed in the generators or the numbers, nothing exempted: the ghost town's broken walls now lose boards from the top down and its false fronts stand on boards; the homestead's roof got a ridge beam and its ridge row filled; the boreal station's radar feed arm now joins its dish, and its bunker's cave roof slab is laid as ground, not a plug of stone blocks; in field worlds soil and moss a structure laid on its own floor (roof gardens, planters) stay blocks and count as ground, as they do in block worlds (as field ground they were dropped as small floating pieces, leaving the capital bases' roof-garden birches hanging over air); steel blocks are steel, not stone; snow blocks were made strong enough to close an igloo and concrete for a capital's slabs; planks to 200 so a pitched plank roof carries itself. (6) done (#414): only a change queues a check; generation and loading write chunks without it, so a saved build stands until something near it changes. (7) done (#414): questcheck --loadtest (seed 13480747113613945685): a wool bridge holds up to a span of 6 and breaks from 7; log beams reaching out 6 and 8 hold; a plank beam reaching out 5 holds; a plank house stands, and with 4 titanium blocks on its roof still stands; titanium piled 3 x 3 on its roof holds 7 courses (63 blocks) and brings the roof down at 8 (72 blocks: 81 of 193 blocks fall); a stone arch over 5 stands; stone lintels over 5 and 9 hold, over 11 one block falls and over 13 three. In play (Game.tick, 10 s): 65 checks, worst 1.04 ms, 84 blocks fell and 12 were crushed by what landed on them.

NOTE: (1) done: APK contents by size (the 165.4 MB build below): music 92.4 MB (stored AAC, unchanged), libblocksmith.so 67.0 MB (27.7 deflated), texpack.bin 18.6 MB (already zlib), sound effects 913 files 16.3 MB (were 118 MB WAV), voices.bin 5.0 MB, libc++_shared 2.4 MB, libdirectorllm 2.3 MB, OpenXR loader 0.5 MB. (2) done (#408): tools/sfxpack.py encodes Resources/Sounds/real/*.wav to Ogg Vorbis (quality 5) for the APK (build-apk.sh; the WAVs stay the source for the Mac and host builds); the Quest decodes each once at load through the same AMediaExtractor/AMediaCodec path as the music (MediaTrackDecoder.decodeAll) to the same samples in memory (RealSounds.trimDecoded drops the codec's delay and the loop pads), so RAM is unchanged. Loops are padded with their own other end so the seam joins without a click (rocketFlightLoop seam step -0.05 of full scale, as the WAV's). tools/sfxpack.py OUT --check: largest RMS change of all 913 is -0.48 dB (step_slime_2), largest peak change -1.58 dB (hoof_sand_2, a quiet step); so every sound is within 0.48 dB RMS and 1.58 dB peak of its WAV, and the 10 loudest within 0.06 dB. AAC (the only other format the Quest decodes in hardware here) moved RMS by 2 dB and peaks by 5 dB, so it was not used. Not checked on the headset itself (the decode path compiled into the APK; the music already plays through it). (3) done: nothing big is stored raw: texpack.bin is already zlib; most of the 1,991 texture layers are painted at runtime, and GPU-compressed (ASTC) textures would need an encoder the toolchain doesn't have, for the 18.6 MB that are imported; left as they are. (4) done: music unchanged (stored AAC, streamed). (5) done: APK 273 MB before (P374), 165.4 MB after (165,418,872 bytes), under 170 MB. Host load time unchanged (the host reads the WAVs); on the Quest the effects now decode at load: 1,364 s of audio decoded in 1.57 s of CPU on the host (WAV 0.35 s), spread over first use as each sound loads.

P391. [DONE #413] [Remington, Oct 21 15:18] ROTARY GUN FOR A TOP TROOP CLASS. His words: "As there are three classes of capital, military troops. Let one of the top classes have a GTA V style mini gun". The top class is Hannibal (TroopClasses.swift). STEPS: (1) a gun spec with an original Capital name (logged in docs/status/ip-renames.md): a six-barrel rotary gun on a 200-round belt of capital rounds, about 1,700 rounds a minute, a long reload, appended after the Panzerfaust so every saved soldier keeps its gun index. (2) its model: a Capital enamel receiver, drive pack, carry handle, rear and fore grips, belt box, and six barrels that turn (a new spin set on GunModel, drawn turning in first person and on soldiers). (3) sounds: its shot, a spin-up whine and a run-down (new gun sound slots, synthesized; reload as the belt-fed machine gun's). (4) the player's handling: the barrels spin up while the trigger is held and fire only at speed, run down after it lets go, and the player walks slower holding it. (5) its icon (drawn from the model like the other Capital guns). (6) Hannibal troopers: a third of them carry it; before each long burst the barrels whine up as a warning, the burst is long and wide, then a longer pause. (7) where it comes from: a fallen gunner drops it as soldiers drop their guns, and it can be crafted from six steel, two firing mechanisms and redstone. (8) the troop-class check accepts it for Hannibal troopers. NOTE: (1) done (#413): gun_rotary, "Mahlwerk Rotary Gun", index 18 (after the Panzerfaust, so saved soldiers keep theirs): 200 capital rounds a belt, a round every 0.035 s (about 1,700 a minute), 3 a round, a 5 s reload, range 90; logged in docs/status/ip-renames.md. (2) done (#413): 27 boxes (CapitalArms.swift); GunModel.spin lists the six barrels and CapitalArms.spun turns them round the spindle, in first person on the Quest and the Mac (Guns.writeFirstPerson's spin) and on soldiers (SoldierRig.weapon). Checked as a side and top plot of its boxes and as its icon; not seen in the headset (no GPU here). (3) done (#413): gun sounds 20 (shot: a short hard crack over the drive's growl), 21 (spin-up whine with the cluster rattling) and 22 (run-down with a slowing ratchet), synthesized (WeaponAudio); reload as the Kettensturm's belt. Not listened to: this machine has no audio out and the host harness has no sound dump. (4) done (#413): the barrels come to speed in 0.5 s while the trigger is held and only then fire, run down over 1.4 s after it lets go; the player walks at 72 % holding it (Player.gunSlow). (5) done: the icon is drawn from the model like the other Capital guns. (6) done (#413): Hannibal troopers carry the arc gun, the launcher or the Mahlwerk, a third each; before each burst the barrels whine up for 0.8 s, then 24-40 rounds a round every 0.049 s at 2.2x the spread, then a pause 1.6x the usual. (7) done (#413): a fallen gunner drops it as soldiers drop their guns; crafted from six steel ingots, two firing mechanisms and redstone. (8) done (#413): questcheck --troopclasses-only accepts it for Hannibal troopers; its 3 failures (Greywall tier guns, the Hannibal helmet and loot drops) fail the same on the build before this change. Compiled and checked on the Quest host build; the Mac build was not compiled here (one line in Renderer.swift).

P374. [DONE] [Remington, Oct 18 07:55:17-07:55:28] Efficiency. "Games in the '90s ran on hundreds of megabytes and very little compute... no reason we can't make this game more efficient." (3) Fix the top three costs by frame time and the top memory user. (4) Target a steady 72 fps there with headroom. (5) Re-measure once and record before/after. No other changes. DONE Oct 18 (partly; lane retired): (1) profiled Stone Gulch and the forest bandit camp (new perfscenes spots, memory and APK numbers) in docs/perf/2026-10-18.md; (2) fixed mob drawing (0.92 -> 0.49 ms a frame at Stone Gulch: each part's points placed once), the far-landmark pass rebuilding every frame when empty, random ticks scanning every column (Chunk.heightMax); (3) top memory user fixed: chunks no longer keep a second copy of their blocks and ground between autosaves (resident 1041 -> 888 MB at Stone Gulch); LEFT: culling (0.6 ms) unchanged, a headset frame/GPU measurement at the camp (141k quads), APK size (273 MB, 118 MB of WAV sounds) not reduced.

P474. Progress notes, moved from the live backlog Oct 27 (the item stays open there): part 1 in #549: townsfolk now speak only lines they have a recorded take for (the written lines for the hour, weather and moment had no voice: half of greetings and chatter were silent captions), and homestead traders answer in their own voice; on new ground (structure-guard-p474.txt) places are bigger and bigger ones come oftener: sizes drawn 2.2 times rarer each instead of 3.5 (56% hamlets, 25% villages, 12% towns, 5% large towns, 2% cities), hamlets, villages and towns laid out bigger, 12 lots a place on average over 50 seeds (was about 6); /locate says what a place is in a line, how deep it lies under the ground or sea floor, and marks it on the map (`questcheck --locatecheck`, `--voicetown`, `--sizes-only`). Part 2 in #554: Creative's own three takes no longer hold the whole surface: they play ten minutes in thirty and the place, biome and hour play the rest (`questcheck --musictakes`). Part 3 in #560: a taken notice-board outlaw job is followed at once: a marker with the distance on the HUD, a beacon, a W on the map where he was last seen, then on the man himself once he has turned up, then on the board that pays once he's dead; he turns up and counts in Creative too (he went looking in Creative and never found him) (`questcheck --towns-only`, honour gap P474 lines). Part 4 in #561: placed and broken blocks show at once again: the edit lane drew each touched section's ground simplified (AdaptiveGround, about nine tenths of its time); it now draws it exact, as the frame's own remesh did, and a worker redraws it simplified after (a citadel's 9 edits: lane done 2.3 s after, was 26.8 s on the cloud host; `questcheck --view OUT --field --questlod --near military_base --inside --edittime --editlane`). Part 5 in #562 (the helicopter): a citadel's Kestrel does park, free to take, on its landing pad (a probe on his seed's nearest citadel on Polygons ground, in Creative: `questcheck --kestrelfield SEED,X,Z`), but 42 blocks up the main tower, where he looked for it three times (P333, P437, here) and never saw it: it is now announced once a session per citadel when it parks, marked T on the map and listed in Track a Target (HUD marker and beacon). Part 6 in #563 (generation speed, first pass, measured on an optimized host build, view 16 by a citadel on his seed): making the land took 12.5 s of work, now 6.9 s (the pool check of every new field chunk read the block registry's tables per cell, two fifths of the making); the ground simplification passes over triangles too far to matter (the same triangles out): meshing 7.0 s, now 6.7 s. Part 7 in #566: the ground simplification lists each edge once and only those it may collapse (it sorted every edge of the section, twice over, six times; line samples put that sort at the top of its time): meshing at the same spot 7.9 s, now 7.2 s, triangles out within 0.03%. Part 8 in #568 (gaps while flying): water and lava settling in the caves of new land re-meshed the chunks they touched as soon as each one's last mesh was done, so the workers spent most of their time re-drawing settling fluid while new land waited; a chunk more than two chunks off whose only change is settling now meshes again at most every 0.75 s (within two chunks, every quarter second; a dug block, a blast or a door still at once). Flying at 40 blocks a second at view 10 on his seed, the view was filled in 16-20 s (five runs) instead of 21 s, 23 s and never in 25 s (three runs) (`questcheck --streamtest`, `BS_SETTLEMESH=0` the old way). Tried and dropped: making missing land before re-meshing chunks that changed detail (slower). Part 9 in #572 (gaps while flying, second pass): a mesh log of the flight (BS_MESHLOG=1) put three quarters of the workers' time on far chunks re-meshing their low ground, and a tally of what changed (BS_EDITTALLY=1) found why: grass the chunk's own later passes had covered (a structure's floor, a boulder, a path) or left under water died to dirt on its first random tick, about a thousand cells in 25 s, each one re-meshing the ground's section round it. That grass now turns to dirt as the chunk is made. Flying at 40 blocks a second at view 10 on his seed, the view was filled at 10-11 s (three runs) instead of 15, 21 and 19 s (three runs); re-meshes of a changed section 13-40 instead of 202-253 (`questcheck --streamtest`, `BS_SETTLEGRASS=0` the old way).

P426. [DONE #457, #462: jitter confirmed fixed by Remington, Oct 24] [Remington, Oct 24 10:12-10:21] JITTER AND DOUBLE VISION, NEARLY UNPLAYABLE. His words: "some sort of jittering going on when I'm walking... slightly nauseating"; "as I'm walking, everything's vibrating"; "it has to do when I'm turning my head. Everything is vibrating badly as I turn my head. Nauseatingly so"; "it's not SpaceWarp 'cause I just turned SpaceWarp off, and it's still doing the fucking jittery thing"; "I'm getting like double vision"; "whatever's going on with my eyes there, like not in sync or something"; "I think it has something to do with the performance upgrade we try to do with maybe where you're looking or something." Known: he did not report this on build f2a29a79 (Oct 23 evening, played 21:05-21:32); it is there on 600eac74. Merged between those two builds: #430 SpaceWarp, #431 P414 loading, #432 dynamic resolution, #435/#436/#439 quad cuts, #440 auto view distance with haze, #441 memory, plus the lane's gameplay items. Frame rate in these notes was mostly 27-45 fps. He needs a fix he can trust; say how sure you are and why. [DONE #457: the eye path between f2a29a79 and 600eac74 changed in two places only, both new and never device-tested: Application SpaceWarp on by default (#430) and dynamic eye-buffer resolution (#432). His SpaceWarp "off" at 10:13 only applied at the next launch (the Options row said so), so his test did not clear it. Both are off by default again (the Oct 23 build's render path); the SpaceWarp switch now applies at once (XRSession.spaceWarpOn: the plain projection layer goes to the runtime, the motion-vector/depth images are not acquired), so he can turn each back on in Options to see which brings the vibration back. Most likely SpaceWarp: it shows every other frame synthesised from the last one, and a synthesised frame that lands off the real one is exactly a 36 Hz vibration with two images, worst as the head moves. Not reproducible on the host (no runtime). REOPENED Oct 24 11:17: still there on e9e2f859 (#457) "with space warp off in settings". Second pass (#462): (1) his 82 notes on 600eac74 never show more than 45 fps, half of the 90 Hz the game asked for by default, and every Oct 22/23 note sat at 72 or under: the headset displayed 90 Hz with the game drawing every other frame. With Space Warp that is the runtime synthesising the frames between, and the pass as built gives it motion vectors of zero for the world (his walking goes in as the app space's delta only), so the world stood still on every synthesised frame and jumped on every drawn one: a 45 Hz vibration, two images, worst with the head moving (SpaceWarpPass.swift says what the real fix is). Without Space Warp, a game at 45-60 fps on a 90 Hz display repeats frames the same way (the compositor turns the view with his head but cannot move the world as he walks). (2) The settings keys for Space Warp and Dynamic Resolution are new (quest.spaceWarp3, quest.dynRes2), so nothing his headset saved on 600eac74 can turn either on; the display asks for 72 Hz by default (his sessions always ended up there); Auto View Distance starts at 16 at most (24, where it had settled under Space Warp's doubled budget, was a minute of missed frames at every launch). The log says "xr: space warp off (P426): extension not requested" at launch. How sure: the 600eac74 vibration is explained by the numbers in his own notes (45 fps cap, half of 90) and the zero motion vectors, sure of that; for e9e2f859 nothing is known of his frame rate or settings, and the host has no runtime, so the fix removes every way the build can reach a half-rate display (saved keys, 90 Hz, a too-high start view) rather than one proven cause. If it still vibrates on this build at 72 fps (the perf line in logcat says), the cause is not in the eye path and the next place to look is the polygon ground under his feet (Player.viewDY, World.smoothGroundOffset) with the P431 block world as the comparison.]

(8) The numbers: a 72 fps frame with Space Warp (27.8 ms) holds about 800k quads by his captures' scaling. Cloud has no GPU: these are quad counts and his own captures' ms, not new headset frame times] [HIGH AGENT] [Remington, Oct 22 playtest, 22:05-22:24] FRAME RATE STILL ABOUT 15-20 FPS ON THE SURFACE. His words: "everything is still goddamn slow", "we're pretty far from the target" (72). Headset captures (view 24): old-growth birch forest, active 10: 19.6 fps, GPU 49.8 ms, CPU 4.6 ms, 1.40M quads, 140 draws, 1,839 sections; jungle, active 4: 17.1 fps, GPU 57-70 ms, CPU 6.6 ms, 1.04-1.63M quads, 831 draws; one CPU spike 'interact 11.9 ms'. Underground it holds 70-72. So it is GPU-bound about 4x on the surface, not CPU. Lowering the active distance did not help (he tried 6 and 4). Draw-in was also slow later in the session (22:18-22:19, 'it was doing so well before'; he suspects the headset heating up). STEPS: (1) log GPU and CPU clock levels and thermal state into the perf capture and the stats line, so a throttled headset shows up as such; (2) find what the GPU spends its 50-70 ms on at these two spots on the host with headset settings: quads vs fill/overdraw vs shader cost (his 'triangles or pixels' question from P376 step 6, answered at these spots); (3) his idea: drop the water's white sheen/foam (he says it adds little and doesn't look good) and measure what it saved; (4) his idea: in caves and underground, build and draw only what can be seen (cave interior), never the surface above; (5) his idea (refined Oct 23): build and keep only what is in a cone wider than the view (for example 120 degrees around the head's ~100), so a head turn has time to fill in; build the front first. Note: frustum culling already skips drawing what is outside the view, so on a GPU-bound frame this mainly speeds up loading and memory, not frame rate; measure both and say which; no holes allowed in a fast head turn; (6) cut the jungle's 831 draws (foliage/trees) toward the forest's 140; (7) every other fix steps 2-6 point to; re-measure both spots. (7b) APPROVED by Remington Oct 23 08:55: ship Application SpaceWarp ON by default (P381 step 9 option; render 36, synthesize 72; correct motion vectors for terrain, mobs, hands and held items, no smearing on fast head turns) and dynamic eye-buffer resolution (drops resolution only when a frame would miss, recovers when it can); both get an on/off in Options; measure fps before/after at both Oct 22 spots. Foveation high is NOT approved: measure only. Report each one's fps gain and what it costs to the eye: Application SpaceWarp on (P381 step 9, render 36 and synthesize 72), fixed foveated rendering at high, a dynamic eye-buffer resolution. (8) only after 1-7: if 72 is still not reached at view 24, he now allows a lower view so it can 'catch its breath': find the largest view that holds 72 at both spots, measure it, and tag [CLAIMED: BLOCKED, needs Remington: <numbers at 24 and at the proposed view>] for him to pick. Do not lower it yourself.

P381. Progress notes, moved from the live backlog Oct 27 (the item stays open there; the whole entry as it stood): [CLAIMED: BUILT, awaiting Remington's playtest; #401, #402] [Remington, Oct 19 09:31] ADAPTIVE GROUND MESH: big procedural triangles fitted to the land. Today the field ground is meshed at a fixed size per sample cell, so at view 24 it is ~870k of ~977k quads and the GPU cost is triangles (P376). Replace that with triangles sized by how much the surface bends: a flat meadow becomes a few large triangles per chunk, rolling hills medium ones, and only cliffs, overhangs, cave mouths, edits and sharp features get small ones. This applies everywhere, near and far (it is not a far LOD; the 2-block lattice stays retired). Smooth shading (normals from the field gradient) so big triangles read as curved; fine surface detail comes from textures and normal maps, not triangles. Material boundaries do not force triangle edges (blend materials in the shader). STEPS: (1) baseline: ground triangles, total quads, GPU ms and frame time at views 8, 16, 24 at Stone Gulch and the savanna village. (2) Per-chunk adaptive mesher from the field surface (quadric edge-collapse simplification or an adaptive quadtree/octree surface): error bound at most 0.05 block inside the active distance, beyond it at most about one Quest 3 pixel at that distance; creases and silhouettes preserved. (3) Crack-free chunk borders: neighbours agree on shared border vertices. (4) Full 3D: caves, overhangs and vertical faces, not a heightmap. (5) Edits re-mesh only the touched patch on a worker, no hitch; adaptive meshes go in P376's versioned disk cache. (6) Walking, collision, digging and placement match the drawn ground (no sinking or floating over 0.05 block). (7) Comparison shots full vs adaptive at 8, 16, 24 at headset resolution in docs/perf/p381/ with differing-pixel counts (near must be identical to the eye). (8) Re-measure the whole table. (9) Only if view 24 is still over the 72 fps budget after (1)-(8): add Application SpaceWarp as an option, default off, measure with it on, and report; Remington decides. Works with whatever field samples exist (P377 step 1's finer edited band included). Do this before P377. Oct 20 note (#401; host, optimized build -Ounchecked, his seed 13480747113613945685, Polygons world; "savanna village" read as Stone Gulch, the savanna town, with the forest bandit camp as the second scene): (1) done (#401): baseline, standing, questcheck --perfscenes (CPU record; GPU from lavapipe below): Stone Gulch view 8/16/24 quads 139,339/417,026/984,166, ground 79,664/252,993/589,380, record 1.89/4.29/9.72 ms; camp 112,268/247,416/318,469, ground 57,406/126,067/165,131, record 1.61/2.88/6.55 ms. (2) done (#401): Sources/AdaptiveGround.swift: each section's field ground (the surface nets' quad per sample cell) is simplified by half-edge collapses (every vertex left stays where the field put it, with its own light, occlusion, material, tint and field normal: smooth shading), cheapest first by quadric error so creases, silhouettes and cliff edges stay; a collapse is taken only when the bound holds measured both ways against the full ground (every full-ground vertex it touches within it of the new triangles, every new triangle's corners, edge middles and centre within it of the full ground) and no triangle turns over or doubles up; triangles are paired back into quads. Near bound 0.05 block; past 180 blocks a second copy at 1/8 block (under one Quest 3 pixel there) is drawn through P375's per-frame far switch, so nothing is remeshed as the player moves. questcheck --adaptivecheck (every ground section of the 5 x 5 chunks round the eye): Stone Gulch 235 sections, 146,971 -> 102,617 triangles, worst 0.0500 block full-to-adaptive (0 of 440,913 points over) and 0.0500 adaptive-to-full (0 of 718,319); camp 324 sections, 212,786 -> 156,550, worst 0.0500 both ways. Not done as written: material boundaries still force edges (a collapse only joins vertices of the same material, light and occlusion), because moving where grass meets dirt or rock changes the look near; they are a small share (35% of ground positions carry two or more vertices, almost all from the texture facing of the surface nets' quads; joining those too gained only 3 more points at 0.05: the bound itself, against ground snapped to the 1/16 grid, is what limits triangle size at Stone Gulch's hills). (3) done (#401): a vertex on the mesh's own edge (the section's sides, holes) never moves or goes, so neighbours meet on the same vertices: border edges differing from the full ground 0 at both scenes. (4) done (#401): it works on the triangles whatever their facing; the check above covers every ground section in those columns, caves, overhangs and cliffs included (also run from inside a cave under Stone Gulch: same bound). (5) done (#401): an edit draws the touched sections' ground in full on the frame (as before: no extra frame cost), then a mesh worker redraws just those sections with the adaptive ground; the disk mesh cache's format is bumped (2) and keyed on the setting. Cost on the workers: meshing the 5 x 5 chunks 456 -> 1,125 ms single thread (the near and far copies). (6) done for the mesher (#401): questcheck --adaptivewalk walks the player 4 s in 8 directions (Player.update on the field) and measures the feet against the drawn ground straight under them, then 64 dig/place rays against it: the adaptive ground matches the full ground within 0.01 everywhere (Stone Gulch feet mean 0.111 full, 0.110 adaptive; camp 0.048 both; in a cave 0.095/0.093; ray hits 0.061 both). Not met as written: with either ground 30-60% of feet samples are more than 0.05 off (worst 0.3-0.5), because the player's 0.6-wide box rests on the field's highest point under it, so on a slope its middle stands over the downhill ground, and 8 of 48 ray hits land more than 0.05 off the drawn ground; both were there before this item and are not the mesh's. (7) done (#401): docs/perf/p381/ (full vs adaptive at 8, 16, 24, 2064 x 2208, README): over Stone Gulch's open ground pixels differing by more than 8 of 255: 0.89%/1.08%/1.20% (by more than 32: 0.08%/0.09%/0.11%); the differences are shading (light and normals interpolated across bigger triangles, a level or two of 255) and the river bank; at 1:1 zoom the pairs look the same. (8) done (#401): Stone Gulch view 8/16/24 quads 100,587/320,182/752,049 (ground 40,103/164,031/382,046: -50%/-35%/-35%), record 1.72/3.56/6.95 ms; camp 87,225/185,826/233,516 (ground 32,351/69,806/90,208), record 1.77/2.79/4.60 ms; lavapipe frame for the headset shot over Stone Gulch (both eyes) 539 -> 373 ms at view 8, 713 -> 556 at 16, 1,343 -> 1,043 at 24 (-22%). (9) built, not measured (#401 numbers; this PR): view 24 is still over budget (about 750k quads at Stone Gulch, about 2x the ~330k P376's numbers allow at 72 fps), so Settings has Space Warp: On/Off (next launch), default off. On, and when the headset's runtime offers XR_FB_space_warp, the game asks for a motion-vector and a depth swapchain at the runtime's recommended size, copies each frame's scene depth into the depth swapchain with a small pass after the world, attaches them to both eyes with the head's movement since the last frame (appSpaceDeltaPose) and the near and far planes, and steps the world by the real time between displayed frames, so the runtime can draw every other frame itself (36 rendered, 72 shown). Not measured: it needs the headset (the host has no OpenXR runtime; the pass compiles with the Quest build). Known limits: everything moving in the world (mobs, ships, water) gives zero motion vectors, so the runtime only reprojects for head movement and those may smear at the in-between frames; the sign of the head-movement delta and the runtime's half-rate pacing are as the extension describes and unchecked on the device. Remington decides: try it on the headset at view 24 and keep it, or leave it off.

P382. Progress notes, moved from the live backlog Oct 27 (the item stays open there; the whole entry as it stood): [CLAIMED: BUILT, awaiting Remington's playtest; #403] [Remington, Oct 19 09:03] THINGS ON THE GROUND FOLLOW THE DRAWN GROUND. Copper wire is still drawn by the block path (Mesher.swift, the rWire branch: flat strips 1/16 above its cell's floor), so in field worlds it is half buried or floating and looks cut at cell edges. Keep the circuit logic on its hidden grid; draw the wire as a strip laid on the field surface (sample height and normal per vertex, subdivide to follow the slope, tiny offset), joined along the ground and up walls; placement goes where the player aims on the surface and snaps to the nearest circuit cell. Then go through every other ground-sitting thing still drawn by the block path in field worlds (rails, torches, carpets, pressure plates, snow layers, lily pads at the shore, signs, repeaters, lamps, plant bases) and fix each the same way; list each one in the note with what was done. STEPS (written Oct 20): (1) a lookup of the drawn ground for the mesher: each section's ground triangles as drawn (adaptive and edited ground included), the height under any point. (2) copper wire on field ground: centre and arms laid on the surface, split every quarter block so they bend with it, 1/16 over it; arms meet the next wire's along the ground; no wall strip up a slope of ground, a strip up a built block starts at the ground. (3) placement: what lies on the ground goes into the cell over the aimed point on the surface (the nearest circuit cell), the circuit logic stays on its grid. (4) rails laid on the surface the same way. (5) thin things (carpets, pressure plates) laid on it bent to its shape. (6) standing things (torches, standing signs and their text, lanterns, candles, repeaters, comparators, daylight sensors, skulls, pickles, eggs, banners) rest whole on the ground under their middle. (7) plants: their foot on the drawn ground from the same lookup (edited ground too, where they floated). (8) snow layers, lily pads, lamps and other full blocks: check each and say. (9) measure: a scene laying each thing on a slope, every vertex's gap to the drawn ground before and after; shots. Mesh time unchanged (5 x 5 chunks 1,181 ms without, 1,118 with: noise). (2) done (#403): wire: before, at the three slopes 14, 10 and 9 of 14 wires were buried (lowest point to 0.49 under the ground) and 4 and 4 floated (to 0.52 over it, cut off at the cell's edge with a wall strip); after, all 42 lie 0.03-0.13 over the ground (mean 0.09), bent with it, arms running on to the next wire's; no wall strip up a slope of ground; a strip up a built block starts at the ground. (3) done (#403): wire, rails, plants and the things in (5)-(6) go into the cell over the aimed point on the surface (Game.groundCell), not the one past the face the ray's main axis named (on a slope that was beside the hill); the circuit stays on its cells. Plain rails are P379's track (ShapeRails), which already lies on the bed; unchanged. (6) done (#403): torches, standing signs (their text moves with the post: World.groundSeat), candles, lanterns, repeaters, comparators, daylight sensors, skulls, pickles, eggs and banners rest whole on the ground under their middle: torch before 0.14 under to 0.24 over, after 0.04 under to 0.02 over; candle floating 0.11-0.14, after within 0.02; sign foot within 0.02 (the board's corners over a slope stay where a rigid post puts them). (2) step 3: past the active distance a chunk keeps only its finished mesh; its block/field data and entity lists are dropped once meshed and meshes are cached to disk (versioned); memory at view 24 before/after. (3) step 4, lossless part: merge exactly coplanar faces with identical material, tint, light, shade and normal (greedy meshing) on built and block parts, drop faces that can never be seen (buried, against neighbours, under opaque fluid), remove duplicate vertices; quads at view 24 Stone Gulch before/after. (4) step 5: trees, leaf blobs, props and repeated building parts drawn with GPU instancing from one mesh each; draws before/after. (5) step 6: occlusion culling of section bounds against nearer terrain and buildings (hierarchical depth or occluder test) on top of horizon culling; sections drawn before/after.
