(7) commit the full vs merged comparison shots at 8, 16 and 24 chunks to docs/perf/p375/ so he can see them. (8) re-measure the whole table; only then, if still over budget, tag P376 BLOCKED with every step's numbers and the remaining options. Oct 19 note (#396; host, optimized build -Ounchecked, Stone Gulch, Polygons world, view 24 standing, BS_SHELLS/BS_STRIPS/BS_OCCLUSION=0 on the same binary for the before): (1) done (#396): "far ticking N" on the headset's stats line and in questcheck --perfscenes; it reads 0 standing and flying at views 8 and 24; mobs, items, arrows, TNT, campfires, fire, falling cells and fluids past the active distance park until it reaches them, ships past it sleep. (2) done (#396): chunks past active + 2 keep only their meshes (shells: 1,067 of 2,085 chunks at view 24); meshes cached to disk per chunk (meshcache/, versioned header: format, block and texture tables, build); blocks/field/light memory 193/188/87 MB -> 92/90/37 MB (-237 MB), resident 2,378 -> 2,304 MB (mesh slabs, 352 MB, unchanged). (3) partly done (#396): greedy merging was already on for flat-lit faces; added strips (faces whose light and shade change along only one axis) and dropped faces under lava; only 3,637 faces saved at view 24, because the ground (572k of 977k quads) is dual-contoured, not block faces. Duplicate vertices NOT removed: 59% of opaque vertices are distinct in their section (13.3M of 22.4M); sharing them needs an index buffer per section (12 bytes a quad as UInt16, which caps a section at 65,536 vertices; 24 as UInt32), so memory stays the same or grows 34%, and it removes vertex shading only, not one triangle, while (6) shows the cost is triangles. Not built for that reason; say if you want it anyway. (4) NOT done: draws are already one multi-draw per 32 MB slab (298 at view 24), so instancing cannot lower them; trees and leaf blobs are each a different shape (no two trees share a mesh) with light baked per vertex, so one instanced mesh per kind would change their look; leaf blobs are 80k and round shapes 62k of 977k quads, so even perfect instancing saves memory, not triangles. (5) done (#396): software occlusion culling after horizon culling (192 x 112 depth of solid section runs, tile min/max, nothing within 32 blocks tested); 817 sections hidden at view 24 (0.93 ms); sections drawn 2,946 -> 2,129, draws 552 -> 298, quads 1,520,239 -> 977,450 (-36%), cull 9.02 -> 7.79 ms, record 15.04 -> 11.55 ms; flying 1,423 -> 1,377 sections, 620k -> 593k quads, record 7.91 -> 7.36 ms; view 8: 281 -> 273 sections, record 3.03 -> 3.28 ms (occlusion costs 0.37 ms for 8 sections there). (6) done: lavapipe GPU time at view 24, Stone Gulch, headset shot (2 eyes): 2,180 ms at 2064x2208, 1,768 ms at half (1032x1104), 1,409 ms at quarter; at view 8 749 -> 294 ms. Per pixel the cost is the same at both views (about 0.12-0.13 us a pixel), so at view 24 full resolution about 1,630 of 2,180 ms (75%) is triangle work and 25% pixels: THE COST IS TRIANGLES. (On the headset QuestSettings.resolutionScale can confirm it.) (7) done (#396): docs/perf/p375/ (full vs merged at 8, 16, 24, README). (8) re-measured above. Still over budget: his headset spent 54-72 ms of GPU on 1.6-2.7M quads; 977k quads at view 24 is about 36% fewer, so roughly 35-46 ms against 13.9 ms, still about 3x over. [CLAIMED: BLOCKED, needs Remington: view 24 at full detail is about 1M quads (2M triangles) standing at Stone Gulch after steps 1-7, and the GPU cost is triangles, so 72 fps needs about 3x fewer. Options: (a) Application SpaceWarp (render 36 fps, the headset makes every other frame; same detail, some smearing on fast motion); (b) a small visible far simplification past about 12 chunks (ground on a 2-block lattice there halves its triangles), your call since it changes the look; (c) build (3)'s shared vertices and (4)'s instancing anyway (no triangle saving; vertex work and memory only); (d) a shorter view (about 14 chunks at 72 fps by these numbers), which you have ruled out.]

P378. Progress notes, moved from the live backlog Oct 27 (the item stays open there; the whole entry as it stood): [Remington, Oct 19 audit] FINISH P363 (lava and water): it was tagged BUILT with no lava work and with fluids still simulated on block cells. STEPS: (1) part (a): lava gets its own look and behaviour: slow viscous flow, a glowing surface with darker cooling crust, light cast on the ground near it, no block steps; compare against his 15:56 Oct 17 voice-note screenshot in docs/playtests/. (2) part (c): water clinging to a wall where it should not be: find the cause and fix it. (3) far water and lava drawn as field surfaces at every distance, not merged cell water past the near radius (P339's identical-far rule, POLYGON STRICT); measure the triangle cost against P376's budget. (4) flow and settling decided by the drawn ground (height and slope of the field surface), not only by cell neighbours: real rules, not patches on the cell fluid. (5) before/after shots at the 15:56 spot in the note. Oct 19 note (#398; host, optimized build -Ounchecked, his seed 13480747113613945685, a field world): (1) done (#398): lava has its own look: a dark cooling crust in drifting plates over glowing molten rock (the plates creep a few centimetres a second, the cracks between them glow hottest and rim the crust orange), drawn in world space so the pattern runs on across quads with no cell steps, and every lava face glows at full light; its edge over open ground is a rounded front sloping off its top (thick for lava, thin for water), never a cell-high wall. Its flow is the slow lava flow as before (a lava tick every sixth water tick, reach 2 outside the Emberdeep). Light on the ground: lava's light now passes into ground cells the field's surface runs through (density 1-16 of 32), so the drawn ground round it is lit; at the 15:56 spot the open cells beside its lava read light 0-7 before and 12-13 after. Against his 15:56 shot (lava as a block-textured slab with a step, standing out of the wall): the slab is gone (see (2)) and what lava is left there is a held pool with a rounded edge lighting the floor; shots in (5). (2) done (#398): the cause of the water on the wall at 15:38 (and of the lava slab at 15:56) was sealed pockets: the generator leaves water and lava in pockets and cracks inside the rock, walled off from the cave by rock thinner than a cell; the drawn ground can't hold a wall that thin, so the pocket showed through it as a strip of water on the wall (with the fluid surfaces off it shows as a dark slot along the wall, as in his shot). Fixes, as each chunk's field is made (FieldFluids.swift): a small body (64 cells or fewer) with ground straight over most of its top and on every side becomes the rock round it; a small body over open air or in a wall over a drop is taken out; a pool whose rim the field drew lower than its water (an open side, a low lip with air over it, a gap under it) has the rim raised to the water's top, of the rock beside it, where it joins ground; inside ground the fluid's drawn level stays a quarter block inside the ground's own surface. Lakes, rivers and the sea are never taken out (a seed 12345 river shot is unchanged). At 15:38 (view 4) 6,931 pocket cells became rock or went and 351 rim cells were raised; at 15:56 (view 3) 4,246 and 301. --poollog lists each body taken out and why. (3) done (#398): water and lava are drawn as field surfaces at every distance (no merged cell water past the near radius): Stone Gulch at view 24, 709,384 quads with far fluids as cells, 709,561 as field surfaces (+177 quads, 0.02%; P376's cost is the ground's 572k quads). (4) done (#398): flow follows the drawn ground: of the sides the cell rules leave (open, nearest a drop), only those whose drawn floor (where the field crosses zero under the cell) is lowest take the flow, so water runs down a bank or a slope the cells see as flat; pools are held by the drawn ground ((2)). Check (questcheck --fluidslope 40, seed 12345 plains, 20 sources on sloping ground, 40 water ticks each): the water reached 1,060 cells, their floors on average 2.69 blocks below the source's, 0% on higher ground; at the source's own height 47 cells, 0.48 blocks lower on average, 0% higher. With the cell rules alone (--cellflow) the same ground gave the same (2.71 lower, 0% higher): on that ground the cells' own drop search already heads downhill, so the drawn-floor rule decides only where the cells see no drop or several equal ones; I found no such spot in that area to show a difference. (5) done: before/after shots at 15:56 (4126.6 -32 3502.5, yaw 351, pitch -6) and 15:38 (4131.7 -20 3668.2, yaw 130, pitch 21) in /mnt/project-files/playtest-oct19/ (p378-1556-before/after, p378-1538-before/after; not in the repo: no binaries). P375. Progress notes, moved from the live backlog Oct 27 (the item stays open there; the whole entry as it stood): [Remington, Oct 18 20:13, after playing build d3ebef00] VIEW DISTANCE 24 AT FULL FRAME RATE, FULL DETAIL. After P339 the headset view is fixed at 24 chunks and the game runs at about 5 fps: unplayable. He wants to see 24 chunks away and does NOT want the distance lowered, capped or removed. P339's rule stands: everything out to 24 looks exactly like the land near him (full-detail meshes, materials, trees and buildings as real models; no coarse or simplified far land, no blur). The only difference past the active-distance slider is that nothing there simulates. Target: 72 fps on Quest 3 at view 24 in the busiest scene (Stone Gulch, and the polygon field world), active distance at its default. HEADSET DATA (Oct 18 20:00-20:11, build d3ebef00, view 24, his own world, from blocksmith.log perf lines): 13.6-18 fps, worst frames 68-101 ms. GPU 54-72 ms per frame against a 13.9 ms budget, so GPU-bound by about 5x. CPU about 12 ms, of which tick (all simulation) is only about 1.2 ms and world 0.0; the rest is command recording (record 10-12 ms). Draws 1,000-1,600, sections 3,000-4,700, quads 1.6-2.7 million (about 3-5 million triangles), cull 5-6 ms. So step 2 (no simulation past the slider) is already nearly true and will not fix this: the cost is triangles and draw recording. Steps 4-6 (fewer triangles with no visible change, merged region draws, instancing, occlusion) are the main work; measure which of triangles or fragment cost dominates first. STEPS: (1) Measure first: frame time at view 24 vs 8 on the host build with headset settings, split CPU (chunk generation, meshing, ticks, AI, culling, draw submission) and GPU (draw calls, triangles, overdraw, shader cost); table in the PR. Find what actually eats the 5 fps before changing anything. (2) Hard split view vs active: beyond the active-distance slider nothing ticks or updates (mobs, AI, random ticks, fluids, fire, crops, block entities, lighting updates, sounds, physics). Those chunks are scenery only. Prove it with a counter on the stats line. (3) Far chunks keep only their finished full-detail mesh in memory: drop their block or field data and entity lists once meshed; cache finished meshes to disk so revisits and head turns never re-generate. (4) Fewer triangles without any visible change: merge coplanar faces (greedy meshing) on block parts, drop faces that can never be seen (buried, under water, against neighbours), and remove duplicate vertices. Same look, fewer triangles. (5) Draw calls: merge far sections into large region buffers per material (e.g. 8x8 chunks per draw), and draw repeated models (trees, props, building parts) with GPU instancing. Draw calls should stay roughly flat as distance grows. (6) Culling: frustum plus P339's horizon culling on the region buffers, and occlusion culling for sections hidden behind terrain or buildings; use the Quest's multiview so both eyes cost one pass; check fixed foveated rendering is on. (7) All far generation and meshing off the main thread with a per-frame time budget; walking and head turns must never drop a frame while the far ring fills in. DECISION (Remington, Oct 18 21:57): far geometry may be simplified ONLY where it cannot be seen: triangles merged when the change is under about one Quest 3 pixel at that distance (screen-space error from the headset's resolution and field of view); no visible change in shape, silhouette, colour or materials, trees, buildings and landmarks keep their real shapes, no blur, fog walls or lowered distance; proof is side-by-side headset-resolution screenshots at 8, 16 and 24 chunks (full vs simplified) plus the frame-time table in the PR; BLOCKED with numbers if even this misses 72 fps. (8) Re-measure the same table. If 72 fps at 24 with full detail is reached, merge and tag [CLAIMED: BUILT, awaiting Remington's playtest] with the numbers. If it is not reachable on the Quest 3 hardware even after steps 2-7, do NOT lower far detail or cap the distance: tag it [CLAIMED: BLOCKED, needs Remington: ...] with the measured numbers, the remaining biggest cost, and his options. Oct 19 slice (#391; host, optimized build, Stone Gulch, Polygons world, standing / flying): view 24 draws 1,536,556 quads (3.07M triangles: ground 869k, other opaque 513k, leaf blobs 80k, round shapes 62k, translucent 13k), 2,946 sections; view 8 draws 127k. Steps 2 and 7 were already true (his own log: tick 1.2 ms; meshing on workers with a per-frame handover). Built: (5) 32 MB mesh slabs on the Quest, so each batched terrain pass is a few draws: 681 -> 552 draws at view 24 (standing), 300 -> 214 flying; (4, under his 21:57 decision) GroundMerge: up-facing field ground whose 2x2 to 16x16 quads lie within 1/8 block of one quad, with every vertex's material, tint, light, shade and normal equal, keeps a merged copy that sections at least 180 blocks away draw (1/8 block is under one Quest 3 pixel at 25 px a degree there); measured: 85,500 quads merge into 15,231 but only 1.05% fewer quads drawn at view 24 (1,536,563 -> 1,520,344) and 1.2% at 16, record 7.08 -> 6.72 ms; headset-resolution shots (2064 x 2208, 104 degrees) full vs merged at 8, 16, 24 chunks differ in 0, 475 and 788 of 9.1M pixels (single pixels on the far horizon), in /mnt/project-files/p375-shots. [CLAIMED: BLOCKED, needs Remington: full detail at view 24 is about 3M triangles a frame at Stone Gulch and his headset spent 55-72 ms of GPU on 3-5M; sub-pixel merging can only remove about 1% because a block at 384 blocks is still about 3 Quest 3 pixels wide, so nearly every far triangle is visible by his rule, and 72 fps would need about 4-5x fewer. Options: (1) one headset session at view 24 with Foveated Rendering on High (and a lower render scale if offered) to see whether the GPU time is triangles or pixels: if it barely moves, it is triangles; (2) Application SpaceWarp: render at 36 fps with the headset synthesising every other frame, same detail, some smearing on fast motion; (3) allow a visible but small far simplification (e.g. 2-3 pixels of error past 12 chunks, or ground on a 2-block lattice past 16), his call since it changes the look; (4) a shorter view at 72 fps (about 9-10 chunks by his numbers), which he has ruled out.]

P339. [CLAIMED: BUILT, awaiting Remington's playtest] [11:50-11:57, 12:00-12:01] Performance and the far view, again ("this one's been failed too many times... we're not getting performance"): auto render fell to 15 and 20, and far terrain "still sucks". (a) The game records its own performance: when frame time stays bad, it saves a capture (scene, position, render distance, CPU vs GPU time, top systems, draw and triangle counts) next to the voice notes so the pull brings it in, and a short haptic or sound tells him a capture was taken ("hey, we've got an issue"); (b) use his captures from the next playtest and QuestSim runs of his seed to fix the real top costs; (c) target: holds render 24 at 72 fps in his world on the surface and in hell; (d) measured before and after numbers from the device in the BUILT note; (e) when built, tag it as built and awaiting his playtest per POLYGON STRICT, not DONE. [Remington, Oct 16 16:00: "performance is still not where I'd like it to be. It's good, not great."] Performance is the top priority alongside P338: every frame-time win counts. Oct 16 evening repeat (build 46151e50) [20:04, 20:08, 20:14]: forcing 24 he got 12 fps; even in survival at 20 chunks the ground around him takes forever to appear; a far volcano draws before the land around it. His idea, build it: two distances, a short SIMULATION distance where things move and think, and a long VIEW distance where everything is drawn (trees, buildings, mobs as still poses) but nothing updates. [Remington, Oct 17 13:09, NEW PLAN, replaces the earlier parts]: "24 render distance, who cares... you can see 24, but it would be the loading distance that matters... what entities and things are actually working in that distance, but you can still see really far... replace the current thing where the distance looks less polished, like a compression. I have always complained about it in each of my runs." STEPS: 1 split the setting in two: VIEW distance fixed at 24 chunks (everything is drawn) and an ACTIVE distance slider (4-16, default 8): inside it mobs, vehicles, physics, fluids, crops, machines and AI run; outside it nothing ticks, chunks are generated, meshed and drawn only, and mobs out there are drawn in their last pose; 2 the Auto governor never touches the view distance again: when frames suffer it lowers the active distance and far mesh density instead; 3 far land is the real world, not FarRing's coloured height field or the horizon shell: chunks out to 24 draw from simplified meshes of the actual terrain with the same materials, textures and lighting as near ground, so it reads as the same world, just less dense; trees and buildings far off as simplified real meshes or impostors baked from the real model with matching lighting, never flat blobs; retire FarRing / HorizonRing inside 24 chunks; 4 far chunk meshing and uploads run on worker threads spread across frames, far chunks cached compactly, memory measured on the Quest; 5 measure on the headset, his seed, surface and hell: 72 fps at view 24 / active 8; 6 screenshots at 8, 16 and 24 chunks in the built note. Oct 17 playtest (build 129, 77871062) [15:32, 16:04, 20:24-20:25]: heavy lag at the start; "something's hogging up all the compute"; the Auto governor dropped render distance to 8 and he was not happy ("love that", sarcastic). With the NEW PLAN the view distance never drops; find what spiked at 16:04 (log frame times by subsystem). BUILT Oct 18 (Sunderfall lane, PR #381): (step 1) the view distance (generated, meshed, drawn) and a new ACTIVE distance (Options > Video and the pause screen on the headset: 4, 6, 8, 10, 12, 16; default 8) are separate: inside the active distance mobs think and move, crops grow, weather and seasons touch blocks, creatures spawn; past it a mob stands in its last pose (a ship's crew keeps up with its deck); on the headset the view is fixed at 24 chunks (the pause screen shows the active row instead of render distance), mobs drawn to 16 chunks; (step 2) Auto steps only the active distance, with no toast (NO TEXT), and the view never drops; (step 3) the far land inside 24 is the real ground's own meshes already (lod 1 past 5 chunks: the field dual-contoured on a 2-block lattice, real materials and light), FarRing and the horizon shell only draw past the loaded disc; (step 4) meshing stays on the worker queue with the 1.5 ms handover a frame; (a) two 5 s windows in a row missing more than 15% of frames outside menus save vn-perf-<stamp>.txt beside the voice notes (place, biome, view and active distances, CPU/GPU split, slowest tick stage, record stages, sections, draws, quads, cull, chunks, jobs, mobs, memory; both windows) and give one short sharp buzz on both hands, at most every 2 min; the bug-notes pull brings them with the notes. Host numbers (unoptimized build, lavapipe, seed 12345 field world): 16 chunks 997 chunks gen 83 s + mesh 114 s, cull 0.91 ms; 24 chunks 2085 chunks gen 144 s + mesh 236 s, cull 1.91 ms (the section walk doubles). Not measured here: headset fps and memory at view 24 / active 8 (steps 5-6 and (c)-(d) need his next session and its perf captures).

P338. Progress notes, moved from the live backlog Oct 27 (the item stays open there; the whole entry as it stood): [11:52, 11:58-11:59] POLYGON FOR REAL (see POLYGON STRICT and THE POLYGON DIRECTION): "we're still operating from a block-by-block basis... these are problems with the fact that we're still using a block overlay... big task, I know, it's gotta be done." What he saw: tree trunks not attached to the ground, dripstone not joined to the ground and drawn as "paper fold" cards up close, lava not meeting the ground, the far view still bad. (a) Finish the handoff's open steps: step 9 pathing on the field, step 10 at least two density samples per block (finer if the budget allows), step 11 water and lava as field-surface fluids that meet the ground; (b) every natural feature is a real mesh joined to the field: dripstone, stalactites, spikes, roots, trunks, logs, boulders; no cards or billboards for anything solid; (c) ground under and around structures, roads and rails is shaped in the field, not cut or banked as cells; (d) the far view draws the field's real silhouette at every distance; (e) add a debug counter of natural-terrain block faces and cell lookups per frame in a field world; both must read 0 on his seed at the spots in the notes; (f) list in the BUILT note every place that still reads the block grid in a field world (grep World.block / getBlock / cell lookups on field-world paths) and why; anything left is a bug to fix, not a note; (g) when built, tag it as built and awaiting his playtest per POLYGON STRICT, never DONE. [Remington, Oct 16 16:00: "there can't be a grid... no block grid... it's gotta figure something out. Whatever it is, it's got to be compute efficient."] (h) No block grid anywhere the player can see or feel: no cube cells in terrain, digging, placing, collision, pathing or the far view. Internally the field can keep sample points (every smooth-terrain engine does), but they must be fine enough and adaptive (sparse chunks, an octree or level-of-detail samples: finer where there is detail, coarser where there is none) so nothing rounds to block size; (i) player building moves off the block grid too: building pieces (walls, floors, beams, stairs, roofs, doors, windows) placed freely and snapped to each other and to the ground, not to a world grid; existing block worlds and builds keep loading; (j) it must cost no more frame time or memory than the block engine it replaces on the Quest: measure both and put the numbers in the BUILT note. Oct 16 evening repeat (build 46151e50) [19:42, 19:46, 19:51, 19:58, 20:04, 20:06, 20:09]: "this is all blocks"; lava still conforms to blocks; walking around he can see where a raised block level used to be; water still shows blocks; floating blocks in a ravine; town plank floors make bumps in the ground; a log floating over water. STEPS (Oct 17, medium effort): 1 list every field-world code path that still reads the block grid (World.block / getBlock / cell lookups) with file:line in docs/status/polygon-field.md; 2 add the two debug counters (natural block faces drawn, cell lookups per frame) to the debug overlay; 3 handoff step 9: Pathfinding.standCost / open use World.fieldBoxes / SmoothField.restHeight in field worlds; 4 step 10: two samples per block in FieldTerrain (fd/fm doubled per axis), save format versioned, old field saves upsampled on load; 5 step 11: water and lava as field-surface fluids meshed against the field; 6 dripstone, spikes, roots, trunks, logs and boulders as real meshes joined to the field (no cards for solids); 7 ground under structures, roads and rails shaped in the field; 8 player building pieces placed freely and snapping to each other and the ground in field worlds (block worlds unchanged); 9 the far view comes from P339; 10 both counters read 0 at the spots in his notes; built note with numbers. Oct 18 slices: steps 1, 2, 3, 5 built (#382); step 7: cells a structure built or cleared keep the ground to their faces and ground it laid (rail ballast, yards) fills its cells, so rails sit on their bed and floors on flat ground (FieldTerrain.split); step 6: pointed driprock is a real tapered spike sunk into the rock it grows from (ShapeSpikes; trunks, logs and boulders were already meshes joined to the field). Step 8: in field worlds building blocks set down free pieces (FreeBuild: walls, floors, stairs, roof panels, posts, doors, windows) at any heading, resting on the drawn ground and snapping to each other's tops, ends, faces and edges; sneak still places a block; block worlds unchanged. BUILT note (Oct 18): steps 1-3 and 5-8 built (#382, #386, #387); view-8 smoke after #387 renders clean (309 sections, 43 draws, 284,970 quads). Field-world grid reads left are listed in docs/status/polygon-field.md. NOT built: step 4 (finer, adaptive samples). Doubling the field per axis costs 8x its memory (two bytes a cell: about 0.2 MB to 1.6 MB a full-height chunk, near 3 GB over the ~1,900 chunks at view 24), so it needs a sparse surface-band layer the mesher and collision can read; that is still to do. Step 10's counters exist but haven't been read at his note spots (no headset here). [OPEN: step 4] Step 4 (Oct 19, after P375's measurements): [CLAIMED: BLOCKED, needs Remington: two samples a block makes the ground's surface 4x the quads (869k ground quads of 1.54M at view 24 at Stone Gulch become about 3.5M, 4.2M quads, ~8M triangles), and P375 measured the headset already 4-5x over its GPU budget at full detail, while (j) says it must cost no more than today. Memory is solvable (a sparse band of fine samples round the surface: ~8 KB a chunk instead of 8x the field), the triangles are not. Options: (1) finer samples only where the ground was edited (digging, blasts, building), which is where the block-size rounding is felt, leaving untouched land as it is; (2) finer samples near the player only, which makes far land coarser than near land (against P339's rule); (3) wait for the P375 decision, since any extra triangles make view 24 worse.] Oct 17 playtest (build 129, 77871062) [20:28]: water not settling onto the beach; his words: "I'm hoping when we change the whole block thing, everything kind of fits into place... a whole physics engine modeled after the real world... don't just fix the error specifically, fix the physics behind it so the error doesn't happen." Fix root causes in the field/physics, not one-off patches.

with honour Respected or better a townsperson near him jumps up on a free gun of their own accord (a companion from then on, for nothing; one a town a day, while he leads fewer than three); a gunner lays the gun for its own shell (a calibre gun's flat, fast one) on the nearest enemy vessel, hostile soldier or anything out for him, never one within 7 blocks of him, and fires when it bears, while his sight keeps the other turrets; Found on the way: his ship crews (D40) laid calibre guns as the old lobbed 45 b/s ball, and fired only on vessels and soldiers, not on monsters or raiders after him (questcheck --gunners: rides 14.7 blocks at the gunner's place, swings onto a raider and fires 10 volleys, no volunteer at honour 0, one at Respected, none twice a day); part 7 in #593: fuel, a third liquid: it flows, fills and carries a swimmer as water does, in its own dark amber, with no endless source (two sources a cell apart leave a flow between); fire or lava touching it sets it alight and the fire runs through the pool a cell every quarter second (an 81-cell pool burns away in 3.5 s); a burning body sets the fuel round it alight, it puts nothing out; under it the view goes murky amber and he can't breathe; an empty bucket dips it, a fuel bucket pours it, or at his vehicle's helm or beside it fills the tank by half as a Fuel Can does (the bucket back); a fuel bucket and an iron ingot make a Fuel Can (the bucket back), and it burns in a furnace; the fuel source is the creative palette's fuel block; a fuel depot's three storage tanks are full of it, with an open hatch on top to dip it (new ground only: the tanks of depots already made stay empty) (questcheck --fuelcheck).

DONE.] [Remington, Oct 27 21:12] HOW CLOSE IT LOOKS TO MINECRAFT, FOR THE STORE. Overseer told him the main store risk was copyright and look-alike: a game that started as a feature-parity copy of the genre reference (CLAUDE.md) could be rejected by Meta's review or pulled after a complaint from Microsoft/Mojang, even with every name renamed. His answer: "None of the GoldenEye content is like related at all. Like that, Minecraft's what I'm worried about." Facts: game rules and mechanics are generally not protected; specific expression is (textures, character and mob designs, UI layouts, logos, sounds, distinctive structures and their art). docs/status/ip-renames.md covers names only. Meta's content guidelines prohibit apps that infringe someone else's IP. Neither the overseer nor the lane is a lawyer.
P478. More progress notes (Oct 28). Part 8 in #595, the cracks (18:55): at his 18:55:36 spot (3413, 160, -1440, looking down at his feet) a slit a block wide and four long lay open along the chunk border at z -1440, the sky showing through it: where the ground climbs across a chunk border into the next 16-block layer, the lower chunk's layer there is empty, yet it owns the strip of ground against its neighbour (each layer draws the dual quads up to its +x / +z neighbours' first row), and the mesher's quick exit for a layer above its chunk's highest block dropped it; such a layer is meshed now when a neighbour has blocks there (renders of his spot from two eyes: slit gone). The round black hole beside the ice near his 18:55:29 spot is a designed sinkhole (WorldGen.carveSinkholes), not a crack. Part 9 in #597, the Ash Vault's baked shadows (18:45): his Quest World is an older world switched to polygons (Polygon Terrain (This World)), not a field world, and outside the overworld (the Deep, the hell band: no generator fields there) its ground was still rounded from the blocks with the first version's 8-corner occlusion, which drew a dark smudge along every block step of the vault's grass. That ground now takes its occlusion from the 4x4x4 blocks round each vertex, as the overworld has since P142 (his 18:45:11 spot rendered as his world: blurred ground brightness spread 5.6 -> 1.4 of 255, darkest patch 25 -> 44). Field worlds draw as before.

P484. Progress notes (Oct 28-29): part 1 in #598: nothing sets down wall pieces any more (cobblestone, cobbled deepslate, smooth stone, planks and the rest place as blocks in every world; wall pieces already set down turn back into their blocks); a log set down on the grass stays a log (a stump only with a root lying out from its foot or leaves over it); falling blocks settle in the first open cell where they meet the drawn ground (up to 3 higher) instead of popping into items, and an unsupported roof or floor piece comes down as its block instead of vanishing (questcheck --placecheck); part 3 in #599: iron is common again (16 extra veins a chunk, most in the middle depths); no more little spawner dungeons in new ground; spore blossoms, glow lichen, hanging roots, leaf litter and the like are left where they grow unless cut with shears; picking up an item gives a clear pop with a wooden tock; dropped pieces are bigger and lit brighter close by; the mining ring sits on the face it marks instead of in the block; a craft with a full pack goes onto the cursor; Gravesworn drop Grave Eyes (the spider eye); traders take cash for the emeralds you lack; part 2 in #600: the traders' covered wagon stands on its log wheels instead of hovering (a single log lying on its own keeps its full round), every camp has a bed (hunters, prospectors, traders, outlaws), a Greywall garrison keeps 160 blocks clear of any village in new ground, a village whose spot fails tries up to three other places in its region (more villages), farmers work their whole field instead of crowding their composter, and villagers that bump step aside rather than stacking up.; part 4 in #602: a player squeezes through a gap half a block wide and half a block high (a narrower, lower body when the crawling body does not fit, back up when it does); a hole mined into the drawn ground is redrawn at once instead of waiting for the light pass; a Capital citadel shows on the far land as its hall and three towers, out to 2400 blocks. Part 5 in #604 (his "compression mechanism"): past the loaded land every town, Capital city and Greywall base within 1100 blocks stands as its own buildings in boxes (houses with their roofs, the city's terraces and glass towers, a base's curtain wall, corner towers and keep), drawn at their true place inside the far plane so the middle ring's ground no longer hides them (or the citadels) from above; a citadel not yet planned is planned off the frame instead of stalling it.
